P·L COMMENTARIUM IN CANTICA CANTICORUM 117

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 117 · Haimo of Halberstadt

COMMENTARIUM IN CANTICA CANTICORUM

Haimo of Halberstadt

PL 117, coll. 295–358  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  First English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Commentarium in Cantica canticorum

295ASalomon, inspiratus divino Spiritu, composuit hunc libellum de nuptiis Christi et Ecclesiae, et quodammodo epithalamium fecit Christi et Ecclesiae, id est canticum super thalamos. Unde et Cantica canticorum vocavit hunc libellum, quia omnia cantica superexcellit. Sicut enim dicitur rex regum, et Dominus dominantium, et solemnitas solemnitatum, sic dicuntur Cantica canticorum ob excellentiam et dignitatem. Est autem in hoc obscurissimus iste liber, quia nullae ibi personae commemorantur, et quasi comico stylo compositus est.

Commentary on the Song of Songs

295ASolomon, inspired by the divine Spirit, composed this little book on the marriage of Christ and the Church, and in a certain manner he made an epithalamium of Christ and the Church, that is, a song upon the marriage-chambers. Hence too he called this little book the Song of Songs, because it surpasses all songs. For just as it is said "king of kings," and "Lord of lords," and "solemnity of solemnities," so it is called the Song of Songs on account of its excellence and dignity. Now this book is most obscure in this respect, because no persons are named in it, and it is composed as if in a comic style.

CAPUT PRIMUM.

« Osculetur me osculo oris sui. » Desiderantis vox est Synagogae adventum Christi. Quasi diceret: Toties 295Bmihi adventum suum promisit per prophetas, veniat ergo jam, et osculetur me osculo oris sui, id est per se ipsum mihi loquatur. « Quia meliora sunt ubera tua vino. » Repente ad ipsum, cujus desiderio flagrabat, verba convertens, subdit: « Quia meliora sunt ubera tua, » etc. Per ubera Christi, dulcedo Evangelii intelligitur, quia eo veluti lacte nutritur infantia credentium. Vinum autem austeritatem legis significat; sed ubera Christi meliora sunt vino, quia dulcedo Evangelii melior est austeritate legis. In lege enim nulla reservatur poenitentia, sed praecipitur ut qui peccat occidatur: Evangelium autem dicit: « Nolo mortem peccatoris, » etc.

« Fragrantia unguentis optimis. » Unguenta sunt dona Spiritus sancti, vel etiam opiniones virtutum, 295Cde quibus Apostolus: « Christi bonus odor sumus. » « Oleum effusum nomen tuum. » Nomen tuum, o Christe, effusum est. Chrisma Graece, Latine unctio, unde Christus: nomen Christi ab unctione vocatum est. Solet enim Spiritus sanctus olei nomine appellari, juxta quod Psalmista ait: « Unxit te Deus tuus oleo, » id est Spiritu sancto. Hoc oleum effusum est, quando haec gratia, quam Christus singulariter habuit, data est omnibus electis: unde et a Christo Christiani dicuntur, participio nominis Christi. Et bene non stillatum, sed effusum oleum dicitur, quia abundanter haec gratia omnibus data est fidem Christi recolentibus. « Ideo adolescentulae dilexerunt te. » Adolescentulae dicuntur animae electorum, quia in baptismo reliquerunt sordes veteris hominis, et renovatae 295Dsunt in Christo. Bene autem feminino genere dicuntur, quia animae sanctorum quo majoris fragilitatis 296Asibi consciae sunt, eo amplius Christum diligunt. Vox Ecclesiae:

« Trahe me post te, curremus. » Hic est permutatio personae. Nam hactenus locuta est Synagoga, hic incipit loqui Ecclesia de gentibus: « Trahe me post te, curremus. » Quia (inquit) meam infirmitatem cognosco, et video me nihil meis viribus boni posse agere, tua gratia trahe me ad tui imitationem, vel etiam, cum coelum ascenderis, trahe me post te. « Introduxit me rex in cellaria sua. » Hic loquitur de Christo sponso suo ad animas fideles. Cellaria Dei sunt aeterna beatitudo, et supernae patriae gaudia, in qua introducta est jam Ecclesia per fidem et spem, quandoque introducetur per rem. « Exsultabimus et laetabimur in te. » De donis (inquit) tuis exsultabimus 296Bet laetabimur. Sed quia scimus hoc non nostri esse meriti, in te, non in nobis exsultabimus: hoc est quod Apostolus ait: « Qui gloriatur, in Domino glorietur. » « Memores uberum tuorum super vinum, » id est, memores gratiae tuae et misericordiae tuae, qua salvati sumus. Ubera enim sunt ipsa gratia, qua salvati sumus. Recordantes etiam quia tu austeritatem legis uberibus doctrinae evangelicae temperare dignatus es. « Recti diligunt te. » Illi (inquit) qui recti corde sunt, diligunt te, id est, illi qui nihil suis meritis tribuunt, sed omnia tuae gratiae deputant. Nullus enim te diligit nisi rectus, et nullus est rectus nisi qui te diligit. Ecclesia de suis pressuris:

« Nigra sum, sed formosa, filiae Hierusalem. » Hic rursus loquitur Ecclesia ad sanctas animas: Nigra 296Csum, quia persecutiones patior, sed formosa sum virtutibus, o filiae Hierusalem, id est animae fideles. Nigra sum, id est, deformis persecutionibus et aerumnis quas sustineo, sed formosa sum decore virtutum. Quomodo autem nigra sit, et quomodo formosa, ostendit cum subdit: « Sicut tabernacula Cedar, sicut pelles Salomonis. » Ita enim distinguitur: Nigra sum sicut tabernacula Cedar, formosa sicut pelles Salomonis. Cedar filius Ismaelis fuit, qui interpretatur tenebrae. Ismaelitae vero semper in tabernaculis habitare soliti sunt, et non habent domos. Dicit ergo Ecclesia se nigram esse sicut tabernacula Cedar, propter persecutiones quas patiebatur a filiis tenebrarum, quod interpretatur Cedar. Hinc Psalmista dicit: « Habitavi cum habitantibus Cedar, » id est, 296Dconversatus sum inter persecutores et peccatores infidelitate nigros. Formosam vero dicit se sicut pelles, 297Aid est tabernacula Salomonis, qui pacificus interpretatur, quoniam digna erat visitatione et consolatione sponsi sui Christi inter angustias. Et sicut tabernacula ex pellibus mortuorum fiunt animalium, ita Ecclesia, tabernaculum videlicet Dei, ex his construitur qui seipsos cum vitiis et concupiscentiis mortificant.

« Nolite me considerare quod fusca sim, quia decoloravit me sol. » O animae fideles et devotae Deo, « nolite me considerare quod fusca sim, » id est quod tribulationibus afficiar, quod persecutionibus opprimar, « quia decoloravit me sol, » id est persecutionis fervor meum splendorem quodammodo obfuscavit. Ac si diceret: Interiorem pulchritudinem meam cogitate, et illam attendite, non illam quam foris pati videor. Tale est quod et Apostolus dicit: Nolite deficere 297Bin tribulationibus meis pro vobis, quae est gloria vestra. Non enim sunt condignae passiones, etc. Sol autem in Scripturis multos habet sensus. Aliquando significat ipsum Dei mediatorem et hominum; unde scriptum est: « Vobis timentibus nomen meum orietur sol justitiae, » id est Christus. Significat Ecclesiam sicut David dixit: « Fulgebunt justi sicut sol. » Aliquando fervorem persecutionis, sicut hic, et in Evangelio, ubi dicitur de semine quod cecidit supra petram, et orto sole exaestuavit. Quod ipse Dominus aperit exponens: Facta autem persecutione propter verbum, scandalizantur. « Filii matris meae pugnaverunt contra me, » etc., usque, « Non custodivi. » Unde coeperit haec persecutio, ostendit cum subdit: « Filii matris meae pugnaverunt, » etc. Vox est 297Cprimitivae Ecclesiae. Synagoga mater est, cui per Prophetam dicitur: « Et vocaberis civitas fidelis et mater civitatum, filia Sion. » Hujus filii sunt Judaei, qui pugnaverunt contra Ecclesiam primitivam, quando excitaverunt persecutionem contra credentes, et omnes dispersi sunt per regiones Samariae. « Posuerunt me custodem in vineis. » Vinea una fuit primum Ecclesia Hierosolymis, sed ob Judaicam persecutionem dispersis credentibus omnibus, multae vineae factae sunt, id est multae Ecclesiae constructae per universum mundum. Dicit ergo Ecclesia primitiva: « Posuerunt me (scilicet apostoli et doctores) custodem, » id est ut essem custos ecclesiarum. Unde et in Graeco bene dicitur: « Disseminati sunt, » ubi nos legimus, « dispersi sunt. » Ad hoc enim divinitus dispersi sunt, 297Dut plures Ecclesiae fierent per universum mundum. « Vineam meam, » id est unam illam Ecclesiam quae in Hierosolymis fuit, non custodivi. Hoc non ad votum neque ad mentem est referendum, sed ad locum. « Non custodivi, » subaudis ut esset ibi ubi coepit.

« Indica mihi quem diligit anima mea, ubi pascas, ubi cubes in meridie. » Vox Ecclesiae est ad sponsum suum Christum: O sponse, quem diligit anima mea, id est quem tota mentis intentione diligo, indica mihi ubi pascas, id est ubi oves tuas pascere facias; ipse est enim pastor bonus, sicut ipse dicit in Evangelio. « Indica mihi ubi pascas et ubi cubes, » id est requiescas, et hoc in meridie, id est in fervore persecutionis vel tentationis. Laborans enim Ecclesia in 298Apersecutionibus, obsecrat sponsum suum ut ei dicat, in quorum mentibus requiescat. Nam cubare sponsi. requiescere est, sicut ipse dicit: « Super quem requiescit spiritus meus, » etc. Ne enim sancti aestu tentationis arescant, ipse in eorum cordibus cubat et requiescit, atque ab omni fervore tentationum et persecutionum protegit. Quare hoc? « Ne vagari incipiam post greges sodalium tuorum. » Greges sodalium appellat conventicula haereticorum, qui sodales Christi sunt, quia et ipsi greges suos pascunt. Et est sensus: O sponse, indica mihi sanctos et electos tuos, ne forte offendam et incurram in haereticos, putans te me ibi posse invenire. Hunc versum male haeretici distinguebant, tanquam sponsa interrogaret: Ubi pascas, ubi cubes? et ipse responderet: 298BIn meridie cubo et in australi parte.

« Si ignoras te, o pulchra inter mulieres, egredere et abi post vestigia gregum. » Vox sponsi ad sponsam suam Ecclesiam: Ac si diceret: Tu quereris te quasi a me derelictam, et persecutionum fervoribus denigratam, nec consideras quod pulchra sis inter mulieres, id est non attendis virtutum spiritualium pulchritudinem, qua prae caeteris decoraris, licet tentationibus denigreris. Si ignoras ergo te, id est si hanc dignitatem et formositatem tuam non recognoscis, egredere de meo consortio, et abi post vestigia gregum; hoc est, sequere et imitare doctrinam errantium haereticorum, qui contempto vero pastore unius gregis, multos sibi coacervaverunt greges. « Et pasce haedos tuos, » id est peccatores et erroneos 298Cauditores juxta tabernacula pastorum, id est secundum dogmata haereticorum. Unus enim pastor est Christus, qui unum habet gregem, id est unitatem catholicae Ecclesiae. Pastores vero multi sunt haeretici, qui gregem deceptorum hominum sibi aggregant, quos diabolo pascunt: de quibus et Psalmista: « Sicut oves in inferno positi sunt. » Oves vocantur in hoc loco mali, non propter simplicitatem vel innocentiam, sed propter hebetudinem, quia nesciunt resistere pravae doctrinae, sed omnia quae sibi imponuntur a pravis doctoribus, sustinent non bona patientia.

« Equitatui meo assimilavi te, amica mea, in curribus Pharaonis. » Equitatum suum appellat exercitum filiorum Israel, videlicet sexcenta millia, qui 298Degressi sunt de Aegypto, et transierunt mare Rubrum. Equitatum autem suum, illam multitudinem dicit, quia sicut auriga currui praeest, ita Deus plebi praeerat, protegens illam et defendens, et ad terram repromissionis introducens. Huic summo equitatui assimilavit amicam suam, quoniam sicut ille populus per mare Rubrum salvatus est Pharaone demerso, ita Ecclesia gentium per baptismum de diaboli servitio liberata, et ad veram repromissionis terram et evangelicam libertatem introducta est, sicque quae erat ancilla vitiorum facta est amica Christi, emundata et abluta per baptismum a sordibus peccatorum.

« Pulchrae sunt genae tuae sicut turturis. » Turturis 299Anatura est, ut si casu conjugem perdiderit, alium ultra non requirat. Turturi ergo assimilatur Ecclesia, quia ex quo Christus mundum praesentia corporali deseruit, et coelos petiit, Ecclesia in ejus amore tenacissime perseverat, nec recipit ullum adulterinum amatorem, quia contemnit mundum, et solius sponsi sui pulchritudinem mente contemplatur. Genis autem turturis genas Ecclesiae comparat, quia in genis maxillae verecundia apparet; et per hoc pudor et verecundia Ecclesiae ostenditur, quia erubescit aliquid foedum et quod sponso suo displiceat perpetrare. Et est sensus: Noli timere ne vageris per greges sodalium meorum, quia tanto te amoris mei pudore, et tanta verecundia donavi, ut certus sim te me non posse deserere, vel ad alienos deflectere. « Collum 299Btuum sicut monilia. » Per collum Ecclesiae doctores sancti designantur, nam per collum cibi reficiendo corpori ministrantur. Sermones etiam, quibus arcana mentium designantur, per colli fistulam egrediuntur. Sic doctores sancti et cibum nobis salutaris doctrinae ministrant, et etiam secreta Scripturarum nobis manifestant. Hoc ergo collum Ecclesiae quasi monile est, quia castae doctrinae gemmis et virtutum ornamentis sancti doctores decorant. Monile enim ornamentum est virginum vel matronarum.

« Murenulas aureas faciemus tibi, vermiculatas argento. » Murenulae juxta litteram ornamentum sunt colli virginalis et puellaris, videlicet virgulae auri perplexae, intermixtis nonnunquam pulchra varietate subtilissimis argenti filis, et hoc est quod dicit, 299C« vermiculatas argento, » id est in modum vermium terrenorum, quos lumbricos dicunt, distinctas. Murenulae autem dicuntur a similitudine piscis marini, qui murena vocatur. Mystice, murenulae sunt perplexa Scripturarum dogmata, et diversis sanctorum Patrum sententiis inter se conjuncta. Aurum quippe claritatem significat sensus spiritalis, argentum vero nitorem eloquii designat. Murenulas ergo aureas sponsus sponsae suae facit, cum Christus Ecclesiam suam doctrinis sanctorum Patrum sensu et eloquio fulgentibus instruit, atque ad illorum fidem et virtutem imitandam accendit. Notandum quod pluraliter dicit: « Faciemus tibi, » quasi enim Christus se personis doctorum conjungit, per quos ipsae murenulae concatenantur, ut illius Ecclesia exornetur.

299D« Dum esset rex in accubitu suo. » Vox sponsae regem sponsum suum Christum dicit, qui per divinitatem suam omnia regit, et etiam secundum humanitatem de regia Davidis stirpe natus est. Accubitus regis fuit incarnatio ejus. Accumbere enim est recubare vel rediscumbere. Et tunc Christus accubuit, quando pro nostra redemptione se usque ad suscipiendam naturam nostram inclinavit. Et tunc, « nardus mea odorem suum dedit, » quia virtus Ecclesiae magis enituit. Cum enim considerat Deum pro se hominem factum, amplius ad spiritualium virtutum studium et amorem sui Conditoris accenditur. Nardus enim fragrantiam virtutum spiritualium designat. Erant quidam ante incarnationem sancti 300ADei. Sed sicut dictum est, sanctitas magis a tempore incarnationis Christi excrevit, et tunc quodammodo nardus Ecclesiae, id est odor virtutum fortius dedit odorem suum. Juxta litteram, nardus herba est aromatica, crassa et fragili radice, folioque parvo et denso, cujus cacumina in aristas se spargunt. Hoc unguento Maria Magdalene pedes et caput Domini perunxisse in Evangelio legitur in domo Simonis.

« Fasciculus myrrhae dilectus meus mihi: » Myrrha species est aromatica, qua mortuorum corpora condiuntur. Hoc loco passio Christi et sepultura designantur: nam depositum corpus Domini de cruce a Nicodemo et Joseph, myrrha et aloe conditum est, et involutum linteis cum aromatibus, ac 300Bsepulturae traditum. Dicit ergo Ecclesia sponsa ejus: « Fasciculus myrrhae dilectus meus mihi » factus est, quia propter me mortuus est et sepultus est. « Inter ubera mea commorabitur. » Nemo dubitat locum cordis inter ubera esse. Dicit ergo: « Inter ubera mea commorabitur. » Id est cordis mei memoria aeternaliter habebitur, et nunquam tantorum ejus beneficiorum obliviscar: sed sive in prosperis sive in adversis, recordabor ejus qui me dilexit, et mortuus est.

« Botrus Cypri dilectus meus mihi in vineis Engaddi. » Cyprus insula est ubi vites nobiles esse feruntur, maximos botros procreantes. Engaddi locus similiter esse fertur, habens arbusculas liquorem balsami desudantes. Loquitur ergo sponsa de resurrectione Christi sui sponsi: « Botrus Cypri dilectus 300Cmeus mihi. » Ac si diceret: Sponsus meus, qui mortis amaritudinem pro me gustavit, et fasciculus mihi quasi myrrhae fuit, in resurrectione sua factus est mihi botrus Cypri, quando me gaudio suae resurrectionis laetificavit. Vinum enim laetificat cor hominis; et hoc in vineis Engaddi, quia ex sua resurrectione quasi suavissimo balsami odore per universum mundum redoluit, et fragrantiam suae fidei late dispersit. Engaddi autem fons haedi interpretatur, per quod significatur baptisma, et sacrificium in quo peccata omnia abluuntur; haedus namque peccatores significat. Baptismus ergo fons est haedi, quia peccatores suscepit, sed immaculatos et mundatos ab omnibus peccatis reddit.

« Ecce tu pulchra es, amica mea. » Vox sponsi 300DChristi ad sponsam suam Ecclesiam. « Ecce tu pulchra es: » Bis eam repetit esse pulchram propter perfectionem operis et munditiam mentis. « Ecce tu pulchra es, » id est in bonis operibus perfecta es, sancte et pie et juste vivendo, secundum Apostolum. « Ecce tu pulchra es, » mundum et simplicem cordis habens intentionem, quia non propter terrena commoda vel mundi gloriam, solummodo Ecclesiis Dei placens bona operaris. « Oculi tui columbarum: » Columba simplicissima avis est, et per hanc, simplicitas atque innocentia Ecclesiae figuratur; quasi enim oculos columbarum habet, quia ab omni fictione et simulatione se immunem custodit. Vel etiam oculos columbarum habet, quia scientiam divinarum 301AScripturarum casto et simplici intuitu intelligit, spirituales et divinos sensus in ea requirens; nam quoniam Spiritus sanctus in specie columbae apparuit, recte doctrina coelestis in oculis columbae intelligitur.

« Ecce tu pulcher es, dilecte mi, et decorus. » Ac si diceret: Tu quidem me pulchram in opere et cogitatione esse dicis, sed ego omnem hanc pulchritudinem a te me credo habere et cognosco. Tu enim es pulcher et decorus, tu es fons totius boni, et quidquid boni habeo, tua gratia habeo Pulcher autem et decorus dicitur, quia et divinitatis perpetuitate, et etiam susceptae humanitatis dignitate mirabilis est. « Lectulus noster floridus. » Lectulus est qualiscunque tranquillitas, vel requies praesentis 301Bvitae. Nam quasi in bello est Ecclesia, dum pro Christo diversa tolerat certamina, quasi vero in lectulo requiescit, cum aliquantula pace sibi concessa fruitur. Sed hic lectulus floridus est, hoc est, varietate virtutum quasi vernantibus floribus distincta est requies Ecclesiae. Tunc enim liberius contemplationi divinae insistit, tunc jejunia, vigilias et caetera bonorum operum liberius exsequitur.

« Tigna domorum nostrarum cedrina, laquearia nostra cypressina. » Domus Ecclesiae sunt conventicula per totum orbem diffusa. Tigna vero et laquearia sunt ipsae personae fidelium, ex quibus Ecclesia constat. Sed attendendum quod tigna ad munimentum sunt domus, laquearia ad ornatum. Ideoque per tigna designantur doctores, quorum 301Cdoctrina fulcitur et sustentatur Ecclesia: per laquearia vero, sancti simplicitate gaudentes, qui suis virtutibus exornant Ecclesiam, non autem doctrina muniunt. Et hoc attendendum quod laquearia tignis affixa sunt, sic et vita fidelium in Ecclesia e doctoribus pendet, quia illorum doctrina instruitur et roboratur. Bene autem tigna cedrina et laquearia cypressina dicuntur. Nam utraeque arbores imputribilis naturae sunt, sanctos designantes, qui immarcescibili desiderio flagrant amore sui Conditoris, et contemptis transitoriis ad aeterna festinant. Cedrus etiam serpentes odore suo fugat, sic sancti doctores daemones vel haereticos fragrantia doctrinae suae repellunt.

CHAPTER ONE.

« Let him kiss me with the kiss of his mouth. » This is the voice of the Synagogue longing for the coming of Christ. As if she said: So many times 295Bhas he promised me his coming through the prophets; let him then come now, and let him kiss me with the kiss of his mouth, that is, let him speak to me through his very self. « For thy breasts are better than wine. » Suddenly turning her words to him for whom she burned with desire, she adds: « For thy breasts are better, » etc. By the breasts of Christ is understood the sweetness of the Gospel, because by it, as by milk, the infancy of believers is nourished. But wine signifies the severity of the law; yet the breasts of Christ are better than wine, because the sweetness of the Gospel is better than the severity of the law. For in the law no penance is reserved, but it is commanded that he who sins be put to death; whereas the Gospel says: « I will not the death of the sinner, » etc.

« Fragrant with the best ointments. » The ointments are the gifts of the Holy Spirit, or also the good reputations of the virtues, 295Cconcerning which the Apostle says: « We are the good odor of Christ. » « Thy name is as oil poured out. » Thy name, O Christ, is poured out. In Greek it is chrisma, in Latin unctio (anointing), whence "Christ": the name of Christ is called from anointing. For the Holy Spirit is wont to be called by the name of oil, according to what the Psalmist says: « Thy God hath anointed thee with oil, » that is, with the Holy Spirit. This oil was poured out when this grace, which Christ had in a singular way, was given to all the elect; whence too they are called Christians from Christ, by participation in the name of Christ. And well is the oil said not to be dripped but poured out, because this grace has been given abundantly to all who cherish the faith of Christ. « Therefore the maidens have loved thee. » The maidens are said to be the souls of the elect, because in baptism they have left behind the filth of the old man, and have been renewed 295Din Christ. And rightly are they named in the feminine gender, because the souls of the saints, the more conscious they are of their own greater frailty, 296Athe more do they love Christ. The voice of the Church:

« Draw me after thee, we will run. » Here is a change of person. For up to this point the Synagogue has spoken; here the Church of the Gentiles begins to speak: « Draw me after thee, we will run. » Because (she says) I know my own weakness, and I see that by my own powers I can do nothing good, by thy grace draw me to the imitation of thee; or also, when thou hast ascended into heaven, draw me after thee. « The king hath brought me into his storerooms. » Here he speaks of Christ her bridegroom to the faithful souls. The storerooms of God are eternal blessedness and the joys of the heavenly fatherland, into which the Church has already been brought by faith and hope, and one day will be brought in in very fact. « We will exult and rejoice in thee. » In thy gifts (she says) we will exult 296Band rejoice. But because we know that this is not of our own merit, we will exult in thee, not in ourselves: this is what the Apostle says: « He that glorieth, let him glory in the Lord. » « Being mindful of thy breasts above wine, » that is, mindful of thy grace and thy mercy, by which we have been saved. For the breasts are that very grace by which we have been saved. Remembering also that thou hast deigned to temper the severity of the law with the breasts of evangelical teaching. « The righteous love thee. » Those (she says) who are upright of heart love thee, that is, those who attribute nothing to their own merits but assign everything to thy grace. For no one loves thee except the upright, and no one is upright except him who loves thee. The Church concerning her own afflictions:

« I am black, but beautiful, O daughters of Jerusalem. » Here again the Church speaks to the holy souls: I am black, 296Cbecause I suffer persecutions, but I am beautiful in virtues, O daughters of Jerusalem, that is, faithful souls. I am black, that is, disfigured by the persecutions and hardships which I endure, but I am beautiful with the comeliness of the virtues. And how she is black, and how beautiful, she shows when she adds: « As the tents of Cedar, as the skins of Solomon. » For it is distinguished thus: I am black as the tents of Cedar, beautiful as the skins of Solomon. Cedar was the son of Ishmael, and is interpreted "darkness." But the Ishmaelites were always accustomed to dwell in tents, and have no houses. The Church therefore says that she is black as the tents of Cedar, on account of the persecutions which she suffered from the sons of darkness, which is what Cedar is interpreted to mean. Hence the Psalmist says: « I have dwelt with the inhabitants of Cedar, » that is, 296DI have consorted among persecutors and sinners, black with unbelief. But she says she is beautiful as the skins, 297Athat is, the tents of Solomon, who is interpreted "the peaceable," since she was worthy of the visitation and consolation of her bridegroom Christ amid her straits. And just as tents are made from the skins of dead animals, so the Church, namely the tabernacle of God, is built up from those who mortify themselves together with their vices and desires.

« Do not consider me that I am brown, because the sun hath discolored me. » O souls faithful and devoted to God, « do not consider me that I am brown, » that is, that I am afflicted with tribulations, that I am oppressed with persecutions, « because the sun hath discolored me, » that is, the heat of persecution has in a certain manner darkened my splendor. As if she said: Think upon my inner beauty, and attend to that, not to that which I seem outwardly to suffer. Such is what the Apostle also says: Do not faint 297Bin my tribulations for you, which are your glory. For the sufferings are not worthy, etc. But the sun in the Scriptures has many meanings. Sometimes it signifies the very Mediator of God and of men; whence it is written: « Unto you that fear my name the sun of justice shall arise, » that is, Christ. It signifies the Church, as David said: « The just shall shine as the sun. » Sometimes it signifies the heat of persecution, as here, and in the Gospel, where it is said of the seed that fell upon the rock, and when the sun was risen it was scorched. This the Lord himself opens up in explaining it: But when persecution arises for the word's sake, they are scandalized. « The sons of my mother have fought against me, » etc., as far as, « I have not kept. » Whence this persecution began, he shows when he adds: « The sons of my mother have fought, » etc. This is the voice 297Cof the primitive Church. The Synagogue is the mother, to whom it is said through the Prophet: « And thou shalt be called the faithful city, and mother of cities, O daughter of Zion. » Her sons are the Jews, who fought against the primitive Church, when they stirred up persecution against the believers, and all were scattered throughout the regions of Samaria. « They have placed me keeper in the vineyards. » At first the Church at Jerusalem was one vineyard, but through the Jewish persecution, when all the believers were scattered, many vineyards were made, that is, many churches were built throughout the whole world. The primitive Church therefore says: « They have placed me (namely the apostles and teachers) as keeper, » that is, that I might be the keeper of the churches. Whence too in the Greek it is well said: « They were disseminated, » where we read, « they were scattered. » For to this end were they divinely scattered, 297Dthat more churches might be made throughout the whole world. « My vineyard, » that is, that one Church which was at Jerusalem, I have not kept. This is not to be referred to her will nor to her mind, but to place. « I have not kept, » supply: so that it should be there where it began.

« Show me, thou whom my soul loveth, where thou feedest, where thou liest in the midday. » This is the voice of the Church to her bridegroom Christ: O bridegroom, whom my soul loveth, that is, whom I love with the whole intention of my mind, show me where thou feedest, that is, where thou causest thy sheep to feed; for he is the good shepherd, as he himself says in the Gospel. « Show me where thou feedest and where thou liest, » that is, where thou restest, and this in the midday, that is, in the heat of persecution or of temptation. For the Church, laboring in 298Apersecutions, entreats her bridegroom to tell her in whose minds he rests. For to lie down is, for the bridegroom, to rest, as he himself says: « Upon whom my spirit resteth, » etc. For lest the saints wither in the heat of temptation, he himself lies down and rests in their hearts, and protects them from all the heat of temptations and persecutions. Why this? « Lest I begin to wander after the flocks of thy companions. » By the flocks of the companions she means the conventicles of the heretics, who are the companions of Christ, because they too feed their own flocks. And the meaning is: O bridegroom, show me thy saints and thy elect, lest perchance I stumble and fall in among the heretics, thinking that I can find thee there. This verse the heretics wrongly divided, as though the bride asked: Where dost thou feed, where dost thou lie? and he himself answered: 298BIn the midday I lie, and in the southern part.

« If thou know not thyself, O most beautiful among women, go forth and depart after the steps of the flocks. » This is the voice of the bridegroom to his bride the Church: As if he said: Thou complainest that thou art as it were forsaken by me, and blackened by the heats of persecution, and thou considerest not that thou art beautiful among women, that is, thou attendest not to the beauty of the spiritual virtues by which thou art adorned above the rest, although thou art blackened by temptations. If then thou know not thyself, that is, if thou dost not recognize this dignity and comeliness of thine, go forth from my company, and depart after the steps of the flocks; that is, follow and imitate the teaching of the erring heretics, who, despising the true shepherd of the one flock, have heaped up for themselves many flocks. « And feed thy kids, » that is, sinners and erring 298Chearers, beside the tents of the shepherds, that is, according to the doctrines of the heretics. For there is one shepherd, Christ, who has one flock, that is, the unity of the Catholic Church. But the shepherds are many, the heretics, who gather to themselves a flock of deceived men, whom they feed for the devil: concerning whom the Psalmist too says: « They are laid in hell like sheep. » The wicked are called sheep in this place, not on account of simplicity or innocence, but on account of dullness, because they know not how to resist depraved teaching, but bear all that is laid upon them by depraved teachers, with no good patience.

« To my cavalry, in the chariots of Pharaoh, have I likened thee, my love. » By his cavalry he means the army of the sons of Israel, namely the six hundred thousand who 298Dwent forth out of Egypt, and passed through the Red Sea. And he calls that multitude his cavalry, because as a charioteer presides over his chariot, so God presided over his people, protecting and defending it, and bringing it into the land of promise. To this supreme cavalry he likened his love, since as that people was saved through the Red Sea with Pharaoh drowned, so the Church of the Gentiles was freed through baptism from the servitude of the devil, and brought into the true land of promise and to evangelical liberty; and thus she who was the handmaid of the vices was made the love of Christ, cleansed and washed through baptism from the filth of sins.

« Thy cheeks are beautiful as the turtledove's. » It is the nature of the turtledove 299Athat, if by chance it lose its mate, it seeks no other thereafter. The Church, therefore, is likened to the turtledove, because from the time that Christ left the world by his bodily presence and sought the heavens, the Church perseveres most tenaciously in his love, and admits no adulterous lover, because she despises the world and contemplates in her mind the beauty of her bridegroom alone. And he compares the cheeks of the Church to the cheeks of the turtledove, because in the cheeks the modesty of the countenance appears; and by this the shame and modesty of the Church is shown, because she blushes to perpetrate anything foul or that would displease her bridegroom. And the meaning is: Do not fear that thou wilt wander through the flocks of my companions, because I have endowed thee with so great a modesty of my love, and so great a bashfulness, that I am certain thou canst not forsake me or turn aside to strangers. « Thy neck 299Bis as jewels. » By the neck of the Church the holy teachers are designated, for through the neck food is ministered for the restoring of the body. The utterances too, by which the secrets of minds are made known, go forth through the channel of the neck. Thus the holy teachers both minister to us the food of saving doctrine, and also make manifest to us the secrets of the Scriptures. This neck of the Church, therefore, is as a necklace, because the holy teachers adorn it with the gems of chaste doctrine and the ornaments of the virtues. For a necklace is an ornament of virgins or of matrons.

« We will make thee chains of gold, inlaid with silver. » The little chains, according to the letter, are an ornament of the neck of a virgin or a girl, namely little rods of gold intertwined, mixed here and there with a beautiful variety of the finest threads of silver, and this is what he says, 299C« inlaid with silver, » that is, marked out in the manner of the earthen worms which they call earthworms. And the chains are called murenulae from their likeness to the sea-fish which is called the murena (lamprey). Mystically, the little chains are the intertwined doctrines of the Scriptures, joined together by the diverse sentences of the holy Fathers. For gold signifies the brightness of the spiritual sense, while silver designates the luster of eloquence. The bridegroom, therefore, makes golden chains for his bride, when Christ instructs his Church with the teachings of the holy Fathers, shining with sense and eloquence, and kindles it to imitate their faith and virtue. It is to be noted that he says in the plural: « We will make thee, » for Christ as it were joins himself to the persons of the teachers, through whom these very chains are linked together, that his Church may be adorned.

299D« While the king was at his repose. » It is the voice of the bride, calling her bridegroom Christ "king," who by his divinity rules all things, and also, according to his humanity, was born of the royal stock of David. The repose of the king was his incarnation. For to recline is to lie back or to recline again. And Christ then reclined, when for our redemption he bowed himself down even to the taking on of our nature. And then, « my spikenard gave forth its odor, » because the virtue of the Church shone forth the more. For when she considers God made man for her sake, she is the more kindled to the zeal for spiritual virtues and to the love of her Maker. For spikenard designates the fragrance of the spiritual virtues. There were certain holy ones of God before the incarnation. 300ABut, as has been said, holiness grew the more from the time of Christ's incarnation, and then in a certain manner the spikenard of the Church, that is, the odor of the virtues, gave forth its odor more strongly. According to the letter, spikenard is an aromatic herb, with a thick and brittle root, and a small and dense leaf, whose tops spread out into ears. With this ointment Mary Magdalene is read in the Gospel to have anointed the feet and head of the Lord in the house of Simon.

« My beloved is to me a bundle of myrrh: » Myrrh is an aromatic spice, with which the bodies of the dead are embalmed. In this place the passion and burial of Christ are designated: for the body of the Lord, taken down from the cross by Nicodemus and Joseph, was embalmed with myrrh and aloes, and wrapped in linen cloths with spices, and 300Bgiven over to burial. The Church his bride therefore says: « My beloved has become to me a bundle of myrrh, » because for my sake he died and was buried. « He shall abide between my breasts. » No one doubts that the place of the heart is between the breasts. She says therefore: « He shall abide between my breasts, » that is, he shall be held eternally in the memory of my heart, and I shall never forget his so great benefits: but whether in prosperity or in adversity, I shall remember him who loved me, and died.

« My beloved is to me a cluster of Cyprus in the vineyards of Engaddi. » Cyprus is an island where noble vines are said to be, producing very large clusters. Engaddi likewise is said to be a place, having little trees exuding the liquor of balsam. The bride therefore speaks of the resurrection of Christ her bridegroom: « My beloved 300Cis to me a cluster of Cyprus. » As if she said: My bridegroom, who tasted the bitterness of death for me, and was to me as a bundle of myrrh, in his resurrection was made to me a cluster of Cyprus, when he gladdened me with the joy of his resurrection. For wine gladdens the heart of man; and this in the vineyards of Engaddi, because out of his resurrection he became fragrant, as with the sweetest odor of balsam, throughout the whole world, and spread abroad far and wide the fragrance of his faith. Now Engaddi is interpreted "fountain of the kid," by which is signified baptism, and the sacrifice in which all sins are washed away; for the kid signifies sinners. Baptism, therefore, is the fountain of the kid, because it has received sinners, but renders them spotless and cleansed from all sins.

« Behold thou art beautiful, my love. » It is the voice of the bridegroom 300DChrist to his bride the Church. « Behold thou art beautiful: » He repeats twice that she is beautiful, on account of the perfection of her work and the purity of her mind. « Behold thou art beautiful, » that is, thou art perfect in good works, living holily and piously and justly, according to the Apostle. « Behold thou art beautiful, » having a pure and simple intention of heart, because thou workest good things not for the sake of earthly advantages or the glory of the world, but pleasing only the churches of God. « Thy eyes are dove's eyes: » The dove is a most simple bird, and by it the simplicity and innocence of the Church is figured; for she has as it were the eyes of doves, because she keeps herself free from all pretense and dissimulation. Or also she has the eyes of doves, because she understands the knowledge of the divine 301AScriptures with a chaste and simple gaze, seeking in it the spiritual and divine senses; for since the Holy Spirit appeared in the form of a dove, rightly is the heavenly teaching understood in the eyes of the dove.

« Behold thou art beautiful, my beloved, and comely. » As if she said: Thou indeed sayest that I am beautiful in work and in thought, but I believe and know that I have all this beauty from thee. For thou art beautiful and comely, thou art the fountain of all good, and whatever good I have, I have by thy grace. And he is called beautiful and comely, because he is wonderful both in the perpetuity of his divinity and also in the dignity of the humanity he took on. « Our little bed is flowery. » The little bed is any tranquillity whatever, or rest of the present 301Blife. For the Church is as it were in war, while for Christ she endures diverse struggles; but she rests as it were in a little bed, when she enjoys some little peace granted to her. But this little bed is flowery, that is, the rest of the Church is marked out with a variety of virtues as with springing flowers. For then she applies herself more freely to divine contemplation, then she carries out more freely her fasts, vigils, and the other good works.

« The beams of our houses are of cedar, our rafters of cypress. » The houses of the Church are the conventicles spread throughout the whole world. And the beams and rafters are the very persons of the faithful, of whom the Church consists. But it is to be attended to that the beams are for the fortifying of the house, the rafters for its adornment. And therefore by the beams are designated the teachers, by whose 301Cteaching the Church is propped up and sustained; but by the rafters, the saints rejoicing in simplicity, who adorn the Church by their virtues, but do not fortify it by teaching. And this is to be attended to, that the rafters are fastened to the beams, so too the life of the faithful in the Church hangs from the teachers, because it is instructed and strengthened by their teaching. And well are the beams said to be of cedar and the rafters of cypress. For both trees are of an incorruptible nature, designating the saints, who burn with unfading desire in the love of their Maker, and, despising transitory things, hasten to the eternal. The cedar too by its odor puts serpents to flight, so the holy teachers by the fragrance of their teaching drive off demons or heretics.

CAPUT II.

301D« Ego flos campi et lilium convallium. » Postquam sponsa qualem domum ipsa et sponsus ejus habeant ostendit, ipse quoque sponsus de se qualis sit ostendit dicens: « Ego flos campi, » etc., videlicet quia odorem meae virtutis per latitudinem totius mundi diffundo. Ego flos, id est Deus mundi. Sicut enim campus floribus adornatur et vernat, ita totus mundus Christi fide et notitia decoratur. Sum etiam lilium convallium, quia illis mentibus praecipue meam gratiam tribuo, quae nullam in se spem habentes, mihi se humili devotione submittunt. Convalles significant mentes humiles, quarum lilium Christus est, quia pulchritudo humilium mentium ipse est qui 302Adixit: « Discite a me quia mitis sum et humilis corde. » Potest et hoc quod dicit, « Ego sum flos campi, » ad incarnationem referri. Campus enim non aratur, non scinditur, non ullo vomere prosulcatur. Dicit ergo: « Ego sum flos campi: » id est filius virginis. Non ergo ruris, sed campi florem se vocat, quia virgineae carnis fructus est, secundum vaticinium Isaiae: « Egredietur Virga de radice Jesse. » Lilium convallium est idem Christus, hoc est, filius humilium parentum. Convalles enim humiles et parentes ejus significant. Veniens enim Dei Filius in mundum, non solum homo fieri dignatus est, sed etiam pauper homo pro nobis: nec divites, sed pauperes elegit habere parentes, ut particeps factus paupertatis nostrae, divitiarum et gloriae suae nos participes 302Bfaceret.

« Sicut lilium inter spinas, sic amica mea inter filias. » Videns sponsam suam ad requiem festinare, et labores fugere, ac per quietem praesentis temporis ad aeternam beatitudinem tendere, se per pressuras et labores hujus saeculi illuc pervenire debere: « Sicut (inquit) lilium inter spinas, sic amica mea inter filias. » Spinae, quae pungunt et lacerant, significant perversos quosque vel intra Ecclesiam, vel extra Ecclesiam. Et est sensus: Sic tibi vivendum est, et sic parata esse debes contra omnia adversa, sicut lilium inter spinas est et tamen florere et gratum odorem ex se emittere non cessat, sic amica mea inter filias; quia non solum ab his qui extra Ecclesiam sunt, mala pateris, verum etiam ab illis qui 302Cregenerati per baptismum in filiationem Dei venisse videntur. Et nota quod se superius sponsus lilium appellavit convallium, nunc sponsam suam dicit quasi lilium inter spinas, quia ipse qui vere est lilium, per gratiam facit Ecclesiam suam esse lilium, candidam videlicet virtutibus, vel ab omni vitiorum labe immunem.

« Sicut malus inter ligna silvarum, sic dilectus meus inter filios. » Nunc sponsa laudata a sponso, vicem ei reddit, et dicit qualis quoque ipse sit. « Sicut malus, » inquit, etc. Sicut malus pulchritudinem habet pomorum, et gratum odorem inter agrestia ligna silvarum, ita et mediator Dei et hominum homo Christus Jesus speciali refulget gratia inter filios, id est inter electos. Ipse enim Dei Filius 302Dper naturam, reliqui electi per gratiam potestate ab unico Filio Dei ut sint filii Dei. Hinc Joannes: « Vidimus gloriam ejus gloriam quasi Unigeniti a Patre. » Non qualemcunque, sed quasi Unigeniti a Patre. « Sub umbra illius quem desiderabam sedi; » quia in ejus protectione confido, et ab eo inter adversa gubernari spero. Umbra enim Christi protectio est divinitatis, quia electos tuetur et defendit. « Et fructus ejus dulcis gutturi meo » ; id est divinitatis contemplatio, qua me satiari confido: hoc est enim sanctorum exspectatio, hoc praemium et satietas, visio Conditoris sui. Desiderat ergo umbram Conditoris sui Ecclesia, quia protectionem illius exoptat inter praesentis vitae adversa. « Fructus illius dulcis gutturi meo est, » 303Aquia exspecto perfrui specie visionis illius, et satiari dulcedine charitatis illius.

« Introduxit me rex in cellam vinariam, ordinavit in me charitatem. » Cella vinaria est Ecclesia, in qua est vinum evangelicae doctrinae. In hanc ergo cellam amica sponsi introducitur, quando ex omnibus gentibus in unam Ecclesiam fideles adunantur. Ordinavit (inquit) in me charitatem. Inter omnes primatum tenet charitas, quia in ipsa subsistit Ecclesia. Charitas ergo in Ecclesia ordinatur, quia non temere nec confuse, sed ordinate tenenda est. Deus enim diligendus est super omnia toto corde, tota anima, totis viribus, omni mente. Proximus diligendus est denique sicut se unusquisque diligit. Nam confundere vult charitatem, quae proximi charitatem Dei 303Bdilectioni praeponit. De quibus Dominus in Evangelio: « Qui amat patrem aut matrem plus quam me, non est me dignus. » Vel etiam « ordinavit in me charitatem, » id est, ipse prior me dilexit, deinde qualiter eum diligere deberem docuit. Unde ipse dicit: « Non vos me elegistis, sed ego elegi vos. » Et Joannes dicit: « Diligamus Deum, quoniam ipse prior dilexit nos. »

« Fulcite me floribus, stipate me malis, quia amore langueo. » Postquam Ecclesia vel anima Deo dilecta in se charitatem ordinatam dixit, qualiter velit requiescere, vel in quo lecto cum dilecto sponso suo pausare, ostendit dicens: « Fulcite me floribus, » etc. Alloquitur animas jam perfecte divino amori inhaerentes. Per flores, initia sanctae conversationis intelliguntur: 303Cper mala vero, perfecta bonorum operum exempla. Et est sensus: O sanctae animae, quae dilectioni Conditoris vestri inhaeretis, fulcite me bonorum operum exemplis, et qualiter in exordio virtutum vel in profectu, vel in perfectione bonorum operum vixeritis ostendite, quia amore langueo. Tunc enim anima amore Dei languet, quando ejus dilectioni nihil praeponit, imo quasi ad saecularia opera languida et imbecillis efficitur, nihilque eam delectat nisi meditatio coelestium et contemplatio Conditoris sui.

« Laeva ejus sub capite meo, et dextera illius amplexabitur me. » Cum (inquit) in tali lecto jacuero, laeva ejus, id est sponsi, erit sub capite meo. Per laevam, praesentis temporis prosperitas, vel etiam 303Ddona Dei intelliguntur, quae sanctis in praesenti conferuntur; per dexteram vero, coelestis patriae beatitudo accipitur. Laeva ergo sponsi sub capite sponsae, quia haec omnia dona ad hoc percipit Ecclesia, ut per haec discat suspirare ad aeterna. Dextera vero sponsi eam amplexatur, quia tota intentio Ecclesiae vel animae fidelis haec est, ut ad aeternam beatitudinem quandoque perveniat, et spe sui Conditoris gaudeat. Et bene laeva prius sub capite, post sponsi dextera eam amplectitur, quia ad amplexus aeternae beatitudinis non poterit pervenire, nisi hic mysteriorum coelestium et donorum divinorum studeat esse particeps.

« Adjuro vos, filiae Hierusalem, per capreas cervosque 304Acamporum, ne suscitetis neque evigilare faciatis dilectam, donec ipsa velit. » Vox sponsi adjurantis filias Hierusalem, id est amicas aeternis desideriis intentas, et visionem pacis requirentes, ne quiescentem suam animam suscitent et inquietent. « Adjuro vos, » etc.; hoc est, animam divinae contemplationi deditam, orationibus vel lectionibus divinis occupatam, inquietare et ad exteriora opera suscitare ne velitis; « donec ipsa velit, » hoc est donec, expleto contemplationis tempore, admonente corporis fragilitate, ipsa velit suscitari a sommo internae quietis et ad temporalia agenda procedere. Videamus autem per quid adjuret filias Hierusalem, per capreas videlicet cervosque camporum. Capreae et cervi munda animalia sunt, serpentibus et venenis 304Binimica. Significant virtutes sanctorum, quae munditia spirituali refulgent, et venena fraudis diabolicae non solum cavent, sed etiam insectantur, et ad nihilum redigunt. Et est sensus: Adjuro vos, filiae Hierusalem, per virtutes vestras, quas divina gratia vobis collatas gaudetis, ut animam contemplationi meae deditam non inquietetis, donec contemplationis hora finita, ipsa aliud quid agere velit.

« Vox dilecti mei, » vox Ecclesiae. « Vox dilecti mei, » subaudis haec est. Laetabunda enim Ecclesia quod tantum diligatur a sponso, ut etiam filias Hierusalem pro illa adjuret, ut non eam requiescentem inquietent, dicit: Haec est vox dilecti mei pro me loquentis. Quem desiderans videre dilectum, sed non valens, annectit: « Ecce iste venit saliens in montibus, 304Ctransiliens colles. » Montes et colles intelliguntur perfecti quique sanctae conversationis, sublimiter a terrenis sublevati, et quasi camporum planitiem melioris vitae meritis excedentes. De his montibus Isaias dicit: « Erit mons domus Domini praeparatus in vertice montium, et elevabitur super colles. » Loquitur ergo sponsa de dilecto suo: « Ecce iste venit saliens in montibus, et transiliens colles; » hoc est, etiam secundum humanitatem omnium sanctorum excellentiam, omnem contemplationis divinae puritatem excedens. Nec stare in montibus, sed transilire dicitur, quia etsi ad horam per internam inspirationem cor sanctorum visitat, subito tamen recedit, ut eos in sui dilectionem accendat. Potest etiam ad incarnationem Christi referri, qui veniens quosdam saltus dedit, 304Dquia de coelo venit in uterum Virginis, de utero Virginis in praesepe, de praesepi in baptismum, de baptismo in crucem, de cruce ad sepulcrum, de sepulcro ad coelum. Hinc Psalmista: « Exsultavit ut gigas ad currendum, » etc.

« Similis est dilectus meus capreae hinnuloque cervorum. » Capreae et cervi, quamvis in sua natura multa habeant quae allegoricis sensibus conveniant, hoc habent tamen singulare, quod in planioribus non morantur, sed in arduis et excelsis locis quosdam saltus dare solent. Recte ergo his assimilatur Christus, propter subtilem contemplationem, et quia nullo intellectu divinitatis ejus majestas comprehendi potest. Non autem cervis, sed capreis assimilatur, 305Aminoribus videlicet animantibus, propter humilitatem incarnationis suae, quia non solum homo fieri, sed humilis homo fieri dignatus est. Hinnulo autem cervorum similis dicitur, quia secundum humanitatem de cervis, hoc est, de patriarchis duxit originem; vel etiam propter varietatem virtutum et innocentiam, hinnulo cervorum comparatur. « En ipse stat post parietem nostrum. » Paries, mortalis nostra conditio appellatur, quia quodammodo inter nos et Deum quasi crassus quidam paries constitutus, non permittit nos ejus contemplatione perfrui. Namque primus homo ita conditus est, ut visione et alloquio Dei frueretur; sed postquam peccavit, appositus est quidam paries, ipsa videlicet mortalitas, qua praegravamur. Nam (ut Sapiens dicit) « corpus quod corrumpitur 305Baggravat animam, et deprimit terrena inhabitatio sensum multa cogitantem. » Sponsum ergo suum, quem superius capreis vel hinnulis cervorum comparavit, nunc dicit post suum parietem stare, quia Christus aliquando vicinus fit, cum se quantum humana mens capere potest, contemplari permittit; aliquando vero elongatur, et quasi caprea vel cervus quibusdam saltibus effugit. Quia ergo paries oppositus erat inter nos et ipsum, nec videre illum poteramus, dignatus est in ipso pariete facere fenestras et cancellos, per quos utcunque sentiri utcunque perspici possit. Unde et sequitur: « respiciens per fenestras, prospiciens per cancellos. » Quasi enim fenestrae et cancelli in pariete fiunt, cum ipse sua gratia se contemplari permittit, licet in speculo et 305Caenigmate. Potest et hoc quod dicit, « Ipse stat post parietem nostrum, » ad humanitatem ejus referri; quasi enim post parietem nostrum stetit, cum carnem nostram sibi apposuit, in qua divinitas ejus latuit. Quod vero sequitur: « respiciens per fenestras, prospiciens per cancellos, » hoc ad divinitatem ejus refertur. Ille enim qui per fenestras vel cancellos intuetur, cuncta quae foris aguntur videt, et ipse a nemine videtur, et omnia scrutatur, ipse investigari et comprehendi non potest.

« Et dilectus meus loquitur mihi, » ad praedicandum hortatur me, dicens: « Surge, propera, amica mea, columba mea, formosa mea, et veni. » Vox sponsi ad publicae operationis studia provocantis. « Surge, propera, amica mea, » per fidem et dilectionem; 305D« columba mea, » per innocentiam et simplicitatem; « formosa » per virtutum pulchritudinem, festina « et veni. » Ac si diceret: Surge de strato tuo illo dulcissimo, hoc est de quiete, in qua soli mihi placere in psalmis, hymnis et orationibus desideras, festina « et veni, » id est festina ad utilitatem proximorum, ut illos quoque per praedicationis officium et bonorum operum exemplo tui imitatores facias, et ad salutem perducas. Et notandum quod superius filias Hierusalem adjurabat ne excitarent neque evigilare facerent dilectam: hic vero ipse eam suscitat et surgere ad laborem hortatur, quia omnia tempus habent, et omni rei tempus est sub coelo, tempus contemplationis, tempus etiam actionis. Alio enim 306Atempore debent sancti praedicatores divinae contemplationi insistere, et mentem suam coelesti quiete oblectari; alio vero tempore per charitatem debent curam proximorum agere, et eis bonorum operum exempla praebere, ac divinae contemplationis doctrinam impendere.

« Jam enim hiems transiit, imber abiit et recessit. » Hiemis et imbris nomine, asperitas infidelitatis exprimitur, quae totum mundum tenebat usque ad adventum Christi. Dicit ergo sponsus: « Surge, amica mea, et veni, quia hiems transiit, » sole justitiae apparente, et imber infidelitatis recessit, ac serenitas verae fidei in mundum resplenduit. Tale est quod Apostolus ait: « Nox praecessit, dies autem appropinquavit. »

306B« Flores apparuerunt in terra nostra, tempus putationis advenit. » Finita hieme et imbre flores apparuerunt, decor videlicet virtutum et pudicitiae, et transeunte asperitate infidelitatis, verna fidei successit temperies. « Tempus putationis venit, » hoc est purgationis sanctorum. Putatio enim vinearum sanctorum significat purgationem, quae in Christo et per Christum est; de quo ipse: « Ego sum vitis, vos palmites. » « Vox turturis audita est in terra nostra. » Hoc est vox praedicantium apostolorum. Turtur enim avis est; et quoniam in excelsis semper arboribus morari solet et nidificare, apostolos vel caeteros doctores significat, qui possunt dicere: « Nostra conversatio in coelis est. » Quod etiam gemitum pro cantu habet, sanctorum ploratum significat, qui 306Csuos ad fletum et lamenta hortantur, dicentes: « Miseri estote et lugete, » vel, « luctus vester in gaudium convertetur. » Vox turturis audita est in terra nostra; id est vox praedicantium apostolorum, in terra nostra, hoc est in Ecclesia. De qua Psalmista: « Domini est terra et plenitudo ejus. »

« Ficus protulit grossos suos. » Ficus Synagogam significat, grossi autem dicuntur primitivae et immaturae ficus, inhabiles ad edendum, qui ad pulsum venti facile cadunt. Voce turturis insonante, ficus protulit grossos suos, quia apostolis praedicantibus emerserunt multi de Synagoga Judaeorum, qui in Christum crederent, et tamen legem carnaliter observare vellent, magisque auctoritate legis delectarentur quam dulcedine Evangelii, volentes circumcisionem, 306Dsabbatum et alia legis praecepta juxta litteram observare. De quibus Apostolus dicit: Aemulantur vos non bene, sed volunt vos circumcidi, ut in carne vestra glorientur. « Vineae florentes dederunt odorem. » Postquam vox turturis audita est, postquam ficus protulit grossos suos, vineae florentes dederunt odorem; quia multitudo Ecclesiarum per latitudinem orbis diffusa est, quae flores virtutum et odorem bonae opinionis late de se spargerent. « Surge, propera, amica mea, speciosa mea, et veni. » Noli (inquit), sponsa mea, quam mihi per fidem et dilectionem amicam feci, otioso tempore lentescere, sed surge et veni, et exempla virtutum foris proximis ostende.

307A« Columba mea, in foraminibus petrae. » Si petra est Christus, juxta quod Apostolus ait, « Petra autem erat Christus, » foramina petrae sunt vulnera quae pro salute nostra in cruce suscepit, fixurae videlicet clavorum et lanceae percussura. In his ergo foraminibus columba, id est Ecclesia, moratur, quia totam spem salutis suae in passione sui Redemptoris constituit. Ibi ab insidiis malignorum spirituum quasi a raptu accipitris secura delitescit; ibi nidificat, id est fetus bonorum operum congerit. « In caverna maceriae. » Maceriae ex siccis lapidibus ad munimenta vinearum solent fieri, ad repellendas vulpes vel caeteras nocivas bestiolas. Per maceriam ergo significatur coelestium custodia virtutum, angelicum videlicet praesidium, sicut Isaias de Domini 307Bvinea ait: « Et maceriam circumdedit, et circumfodit vineam. » In caverna ergo maceriae moratur, quia praesidio angelorum undique circumdatur, et a tentationibus diaboli custoditur. « Ostende mihi faciem tuam, sonet vox tua in auribus meis, quia vox tua dulcis, et facies tua decora. » Tu (inquit) dilecta mea, quae in lecto contemplationis dulcissime quiescis, veni et ostende mihi faciem tuam; id est a secreto quietis egredere ad publicam actionem, et pulchritudinem bonorum operum tuorum aliis ad exemplum demonstra. « Ostende mihi, » dicit: quasi enim Deo faciem suam ostendit Ecclesia, cum proximis ad utilitatem et profectum per virtutum opera, quantam intrinsecus habeat pulchritudinem, demonstrat. Hoc est quod Dominus dicit in Evangelio, « Videant 307Copera vestra bona, et glorificent Patrem vestrum qui in coelis est. » « Sonet vox tua in auribus meis. » Vox videlicet praedicationis vel divinae laudis, qua alios facias proficere. In auribus enim Domini vox Ecclesiae sonat, cum per praedicationem ex multorum conversione delectatur Deus. Bene autem dicit: « Ostende mihi faciem tuam, » et vox tua non aliena; tuam videlicet faciem mihi ostende, quam in baptismo tibi dedi, quando mundavi te ab omni peccato. « Vox tua, dicit, in auribus meis sonet, » ut ex sincera intentione dilectionis meae procedat, ut non propter aliud praedices nisi propter me.

« Capite nobis vulpes parvulas, quae demoliuntur vineas, nam vinea nostra floruit. » Vox sponsi praecipientis 307Dsponsae suae et filiabus Hierusalem, sanctis videlicet doctoribus vel animabus fidelibus. « Capite (inquit) nobis vulpes parvulas. » Vulpes est dolosum animal et fraudulentum, in cavernis et specubus habitans, nec recto, sed tortuoso incedens itinere: ideoque designat haereticos vel schismaticos, qui demoliuntur vineas, hoc est Ecclesiam Dei, plebem videlicet fidelium, lacerare et decipere non cessant. Praecipit ergo ut parvulas vulpes capiant, id est, ut haereticam pravitatem in ipso initio debellare et destruere non cessent, ne robusta facta fortius impugnet. « Nam vinea nostra floruit, » id est Ecclesia per universum mundum flores virtutum emisit. Et nota quia cum superius vineas pluraliter dixerit, 308Amodo dicit singulariter, « vinea nostra floruit, » quia ex multis Ecclesiis una est electa Ecclesia.

« Dilectus meus mihi, et ego illi, qui pascitur inter lilia, donec aspiret dies, et inclinentur umbrae. » Vox sponsae, « dilectus meus mihi, » subaudis societur vinculo charitatis et amoris, et ego illi conjungar et consocier vicissitudine mutuae difectionis. Et « dilectus meus, » subaudis det verba exhortationis et divinae promissionis, « et ego, » subaudis ostendam illi faciem meam. Vel etiam dilectus meus dignetur mihi conjungi glutino charitatis, et ego illi conjungar vinculo dignae obedientiae, obtemperans praeceptis ejus qui pascitur inter lilia, hoc est delectatur et jucundatur inter candidas et odoriferas virtutes sanctorum. Et notandum quod superius « lilium » singulariter 308Bdicitur, hic vero pluraliter « lilia, » quia multae sunt virtutes sanctorum, una tamen fides est, et una charitas, quibus praecipue Deus delectatur. Sive etiam, « pascitur inter lilia, » hoc est suavissime delectatur inter virgineos choros. Candor enim liliorum recte munditiae virginali comparatur. « Donec aspiret dies, » hoc est donec transeant nubila praesentis vitae, et appareat dies, hoc est veniat claritas sempiternae beatitudinis. Et est sensus, Dilectus meus mihi praestat auxilium, et ego illi dignam praebeo obedientiam, qui virtutibus sanctorum delectatur, quousque transcursa nocte praesentis vitae, in qua per speculum et aenigma cernitur, appareat dies supernae claritatis, ut facie ad faciem videatur.

« Revertere, similis esto, dilecte mi, capreae aut 308Chinnulo cervorum super montes Bethel. » Vox Ecclesiae sponsi sui speciem desiderantis. O dilecte mi, qui de lecto meo me surgere praecipis, et ut tibi faciem meam ostendam hortaris, revertere, hoc est claritatem divinitatis tuae me speculari permitte, et hoc quod mihi perfecte promittis in alia vita, beatitudinem videlicet tuam, in hac adhuc peregrinatione me saltem in figura et aenigmate habere permitte. « Similis esto capreae aut hinnulo cervorum super montes Bethel. » Jam dictum est superius quod capreae campos despiciunt, et ad alta montium enituntur. Christus ergo capreis comparatur, quia secundum carnem resurgens a mortuis, coelorum alta petiit; hinnulus vero cervorum vocatur, hoc est filius antiquorum patrum. Cervi enim sunt patriarchae et prophetae, 308Dde quibus Christus carnis duxit originem. Fertur hinnulus hanc habere naturam, ut fervente sole opaca et umbrosa loca requirat, in quibus ab aestu protegatur; sic et Christus in eorum mentibus requiescit, qui rore Spiritus sancti ab aestibus carnalium voluptatum temperantur. Ac si diceret: O dilecte, revertere, hoc est saepius me tua visitatione illustrando laetifica, qui per carnem quam assumpsisti, jam coelorum sublimia penetrasti. Montes autem Bethel mentes sanctorum significant, a terrenis per superna desideria elevatas. Nam Bethel domus consurgens, vel domus vigiliarum, vigiles mentes sanctorum significat, quae ad desideranda superna consurgunt, et ad ea percipienda invigilant. 309Ahabentes in se Deum habitatorem, qui dicit per Prophetam: « Inhabitabo in illis et inambulabo. » Super ergo montes Bethel dilectus similis est capreae vel hinnulo cervorum, quia illas visitare dignatur quae domus Dei esse student.

CHAPTER II.

301D« I am the flower of the field and the lily of the valleys. » After the bride has shown what kind of house she and her bridegroom have, the bridegroom too shows what kind he himself is, saying: « I am the flower of the field, » etc., namely because I diffuse the odor of my virtue throughout the breadth of the whole world. I am the flower, that is, the God of the world. For as a field is adorned and grows green with flowers, so the whole world is adorned with the faith and knowledge of Christ. I am also the lily of the valleys, because I bestow my grace especially on those minds which, having no hope in themselves, submit themselves to me with humble devotion. The valleys signify humble minds, whose lily is Christ, because the beauty of humble minds is he who 302Asaid: « Learn of me, for I am meek and humble of heart. » And this which he says, « I am the flower of the field, » can also be referred to the incarnation. For the field is not plowed, not cut, not furrowed by any plowshare. He says therefore: « I am the flower of the field, » that is, the son of the Virgin. He calls himself, then, not the flower of the tilled ground, but of the field, because he is the fruit of virginal flesh, according to the prophecy of Isaiah: « There shall come forth a rod out of the root of Jesse. » The same Christ is the lily of the valleys, that is, the son of humble parents. For the valleys signify his humble parents. For the Son of God, coming into the world, deigned not only to become man, but even a poor man for us; nor did he choose to have rich parents, but poor ones, that, being made a partaker of our poverty, he might make us partakers of his riches and glory. 302B

« As the lily among thorns, so is my love among the daughters. » Seeing his bride hasten to rest, and flee labors, and through the quiet of the present time tend toward eternal blessedness, whereas she must come thither through the afflictions and labors of this world: « As the lily among thorns, he says, so is my love among the daughters. » The thorns, which prick and tear, signify all the perverse whether within the Church or outside the Church. And the meaning is: Thou must so live, and so must thou be prepared against all adversities, that, as the lily is among thorns and yet ceases not to flower and to send forth from itself a pleasing odor, so is my love among the daughters; because thou sufferest evils not only from those who are outside the Church, but also from those who 302Cseem, regenerated through baptism, to have come into the adoption of God. And note that above the bridegroom called himself the lily of the valleys, now he calls his bride as it were a lily among thorns, because he who is truly the lily makes his Church, through grace, to be a lily, namely white with virtues, or free from all stain of vices.

« As the apple tree among the trees of the woods, so is my beloved among the sons. » Now the bride, praised by the bridegroom, returns to him his due, and says what kind he too is. « As the apple tree, » she says, etc. As the apple tree has the beauty of its fruits, and a pleasing odor among the wild trees of the woods, so also the Mediator of God and men, the man Christ Jesus, shines with a special grace among the sons, that is, among the elect. For he himself is the Son of God 302Dby nature, while the rest of the elect are so by grace, having power from the only Son of God that they should be sons of God. Hence John: « We saw his glory, the glory as it were of the Only-begotten of the Father. » Not of any sort whatever, but as it were of the Only-begotten of the Father. « I sat under the shadow of him whom I desired; » because I trust in his protection, and hope to be governed by him amid adversities. For the shadow of Christ is the protection of his divinity, because it guards and defends the elect. « And his fruit was sweet to my palate; » that is, the contemplation of his divinity, with which I trust to be satisfied: for this is the expectation of the saints, this their reward and satiety, the vision of their Maker. The Church, therefore, desires the shadow of her Maker, because she longs for his protection amid the adversities of the present life. « His fruit is sweet to my palate, » 303Abecause I look to enjoy fully the sight of that vision of his, and to be satisfied with the sweetness of his charity.

« The king brought me into the wine-cellar, he set charity in order within me. » The wine-cellar is the Church, in which is the wine of evangelical doctrine. Into this cellar, then, the bridegroom's love is brought, when out of all nations the faithful are gathered into one Church. He set charity in order (she says) within me. Among all things charity holds the primacy, because in it the Church subsists. Charity therefore is set in order in the Church, because it must be held not rashly nor confusedly, but in order. For God is to be loved above all things with the whole heart, the whole soul, all one's strength, all one's mind. Then the neighbor is to be loved as each one loves himself. For he wishes to confound charity who sets the love of his neighbor before the love 303Bof God. Concerning whom the Lord says in the Gospel: « He that loveth father or mother more than me is not worthy of me. » Or also « he set charity in order within me, » that is, he himself first loved me, and then taught me how I ought to love him. Whence he himself says: « You have not chosen me, but I have chosen you. » And John says: « Let us love God, because he first loved us. »

« Stay me up with flowers, compass me about with apples, because I languish with love. » After the Church, or the soul beloved of God, has said that charity was set in order within her, she shows how she wishes to rest, or in what bed to repose with her beloved bridegroom, saying: « Stay me up with flowers, » etc. She addresses the souls now perfectly cleaving to the divine love. By the flowers, the beginnings of holy conversation are understood; 303Cbut by the apples, the perfect examples of good works. And the meaning is: O holy souls, who cleave to the love of your Maker, stay me up with the examples of good works, and show how you have lived in the beginning of the virtues, or in progress, or in the perfection of good works, because I languish with love. For the soul then languishes with the love of God, when she sets nothing before his love, nay, is made as it were languid and feeble toward worldly works, and nothing delights her save the meditation of heavenly things and the contemplation of her Maker.

« His left hand is under my head, and his right hand shall embrace me. » When (she says) I shall have lain in such a bed, his left hand, that is, the bridegroom's, will be under my head. By the left hand is understood the prosperity of the present time, or also 303Dthe gifts of God which are conferred on the saints in the present; but by the right hand is received the blessedness of the heavenly fatherland. The left hand of the bridegroom, therefore, is under the head of the bride, because the Church receives all these gifts to this end, that through them she may learn to sigh for the eternal things. But the right hand of the bridegroom embraces her, because the whole intention of the Church, or of the faithful soul, is this, that she may one day attain to eternal blessedness, and rejoice in the hope of her Maker. And well is the left hand first under the head, and afterward the bridegroom's right hand embraces her, because she will not be able to attain to the embraces of eternal blessedness, unless here she strive to be a partaker of the heavenly mysteries and the divine gifts.

« I adjure you, O daughters of Jerusalem, by the roes and the harts 304Aof the fields, that you stir not up, nor make the beloved to awake, till she please. » It is the voice of the bridegroom adjuring the daughters of Jerusalem, that is, the friends intent on eternal desires, and seeking the vision of peace, that they stir not up nor disquiet his soul while she is at rest. « I adjure you, » etc.; that is, be unwilling to disquiet the soul given over to divine contemplation, occupied with prayers or divine readings, and to rouse her to outward works; « till she please, » that is, until, the time of contemplation being fulfilled, and the frailty of the body admonishing her, she herself wishes to be roused from the sleep of inner quiet, and to proceed to the doing of temporal things. But let us see by what he adjures the daughters of Jerusalem, namely by the roes and the harts of the fields. The roes and harts are clean animals, hostile to serpents and venoms. 304BThey signify the virtues of the saints, which shine with spiritual cleanness, and not only guard against the venoms of diabolical fraud, but even pursue them, and reduce them to nothing. And the meaning is: I adjure you, O daughters of Jerusalem, by your virtues, in which you rejoice as conferred on you by divine grace, that you disquiet not the soul given over to my contemplation, until, the hour of contemplation being finished, she herself wishes to do something else.

« The voice of my beloved, » the voice of the Church. « The voice of my beloved, » supply: this is it. For the Church, rejoicing that she is so greatly loved by the bridegroom, that he even adjures the daughters of Jerusalem for her sake, that they disquiet her not while she rests, says: This is the voice of my beloved speaking for me. Desiring to see whom she loves, but not able, she adds: « Behold he cometh leaping upon the mountains, 304Cskipping over the hills. » By the mountains and hills are understood all the perfect of holy conversation, sublimely raised up from earthly things, and as it were exceeding the level of the plains by the merits of a better life. Of these mountains Isaiah says: « The mountain of the house of the Lord shall be prepared on the top of the mountains, and it shall be exalted above the hills. » The bride, therefore, speaks of her beloved: « Behold he cometh leaping upon the mountains, and skipping over the hills; » that is, exceeding also, according to his humanity, the excellence of all the saints, all the purity of divine contemplation. And he is said not to stand upon the mountains, but to skip over them, because although for an hour he visits the heart of the saints by inward inspiration, yet suddenly he withdraws, that he may kindle them to his love. It can also be referred to the incarnation of Christ, who in coming gave certain leaps, 304Dbecause from heaven he came into the womb of the Virgin, from the womb of the Virgin into the manger, from the manger into baptism, from baptism onto the cross, from the cross to the sepulcher, from the sepulcher to heaven. Hence the Psalmist: « He rejoiced as a giant to run his course, » etc.

« My beloved is like a roe or a young hart. » The roes and harts, although in their nature they have many things which agree with allegorical senses, yet have this as singular, that they do not tarry in the more level places, but in steep and lofty places are wont to give certain leaps. Rightly, therefore, is Christ likened to them, on account of subtle contemplation, and because the majesty of his divinity can be comprehended by no understanding. But he is likened not to the harts, but to the roes, 305Anamely to lesser animals, on account of the humility of his incarnation, because he deigned not only to become man, but to become a humble man. And he is said to be like a young hart, because according to his humanity he drew his origin from the harts, that is, from the patriarchs; or also, on account of the variety of his virtues and his innocence, he is compared to a young hart. « Behold he standeth behind our wall. » The wall is called our mortal condition, because in a certain manner it is set up between us and God as a kind of thick wall, and does not permit us to enjoy fully the contemplation of him. For the first man was so made that he might enjoy the vision and converse of God; but after he sinned, a certain wall was set up, namely that very mortality with which we are weighed down. For (as the Wise Man says) « the body that is corrupted 305Bweigheth down the soul, and the earthly habitation presseth down the mind that museth upon many things. » Her bridegroom, therefore, whom above she compared to roes or young harts, she now says stands behind her wall, because Christ sometimes becomes near, when he permits himself to be contemplated as far as the human mind can grasp; but sometimes he is removed afar, and like a roe or a hart escapes by certain leaps. Because, therefore, a wall was set up between us and him, and we could not see him, he deigned to make in that very wall windows and lattices, through which he might in some way be perceived, in some way be seen. Whence too it follows: « looking through the windows, looking forth through the lattices. » For windows and lattices are as it were made in the wall, when he permits himself to be contemplated by his grace, though in a glass and 305Cin a dark manner. And this which he says, « He standeth behind our wall, » can also be referred to his humanity; for he stood as it were behind our wall, when he set our flesh before himself, in which his divinity lay hidden. But that which follows: « looking through the windows, looking forth through the lattices, » this is referred to his divinity. For he who looks through windows or lattices sees all that is done outside, and is himself seen by no one, and searches out all things, while he himself cannot be searched out and comprehended.

« And my beloved speaketh to me, » he exhorts me to preach, saying: « Arise, make haste, my love, my dove, my beautiful one, and come. » It is the voice of the bridegroom calling to the zeal for public work. « Arise, make haste, my love, » through faith and love; 305D« my dove, » through innocence and simplicity; « my beautiful one, » through the beauty of the virtues, hasten « and come. » As if he said: Arise from that most sweet couch of thine, that is, from the quiet in which thou desirest to please me alone in psalms, hymns, and prayers; hasten « and come, » that is, hasten to the profit of thy neighbors, that thou mayest make them too, through the office of preaching and by the example of good works, imitators of thee, and lead them to salvation. And it is to be noted that above he adjured the daughters of Jerusalem not to rouse nor make the beloved to awake; but here he himself rouses her and exhorts her to arise to labor, because all things have their time, and to everything there is a time under heaven, a time of contemplation, a time also of action. For at one 306Atime holy preachers ought to apply themselves to divine contemplation, and to delight their mind with heavenly quiet; but at another time, through charity, they ought to take care of their neighbors, and to offer them the examples of good works, and to bestow the teaching of divine contemplation.

« For now the winter is past, the rain is over and gone. » By the name of winter and rain, the harshness of unbelief is expressed, which held the whole world until the coming of Christ. The bridegroom therefore says: « Arise, my love, and come, because the winter is past, » the sun of justice appearing, and the rain of unbelief has gone, and the serenity of true faith has shone forth into the world. Such is what the Apostle says: « The night is passed, and the day is at hand. »

306B« The flowers have appeared in our land, the time of pruning is come. » The winter and the rain being ended, the flowers have appeared, namely the comeliness of the virtues and of chastity, and, the harshness of unbelief passing away, the spring mildness of faith has succeeded. « The time of pruning is come, » that is, of the purging of the saints. For the pruning of the vineyards of the saints signifies the purging which is in Christ and through Christ; concerning whom he himself says: « I am the vine, you the branches. » « The voice of the turtle is heard in our land. » This is the voice of the preaching apostles. For the turtledove is a bird; and since it is always wont to tarry and nest in lofty trees, it signifies the apostles or the other teachers, who can say: « Our conversation is in heaven. » And because it has a groaning for its song, it signifies the weeping of the saints, who 306Cexhort their own to weeping and lamentation, saying: « Be miserable and mourn, » or, « your mourning shall be turned into joy. » The voice of the turtle is heard in our land; that is, the voice of the preaching apostles, in our land, that is, in the Church. Concerning which the Psalmist says: « The earth is the Lord's, and the fullness thereof. »

« The fig tree hath put forth her green figs. » The fig tree signifies the Synagogue, and the green figs are the early and unripe figs, unfit for eating, which fall easily at the buffeting of the wind.
