They find, then, the bride seeking her beloved, when the holy teachers instruct and form the soul intent on heavenly desires, either present by their word, or absent by their writings. « They struck me (she says), and wounded me. » The word of God is a sword, the Apostle saying: « The word of God is living and effectual, and more piercing than any two-edged sword, and reaching unto the division of the soul and the spirit. » By this sword the bride is struck and wounded, when the word of the preacher speaks of God, and pierces the hearers as with a certain dart of compunction and love. « They took away my cloak 330Bfrom me. » This cloak here, which above is understood as the tunic, where it is said: « I have put off my tunic, » is namely the covering of worldly desires, which the keepers take away, because whatever remains in anyone's mind of the love of the present world, the holy teachers take it all away, and make him love only the bridegroom Christ. Such men can say with the Apostle: « The world is crucified to me, and I to the world. » « The keepers of the walls. » The walls of the city are the holy teachers of the Church. But the keepers of the walls are those who also instruct even them, who can also preach to others, such as was Paul, who instructed and kept Timothy and Titus, who were surely the walls of this city, that is, exhorted them to the perfection of good work.

330C« I adjure you, O daughters of Jerusalem, if you find my beloved, that you tell him that I languish with love. » The daughters of Jerusalem are the faithful souls, who either still journey in the present, or also enjoy Christ. The daughters of Jerusalem, that is, the daughters of that heavenly city in which is the highest peace and happiness of divine contemplation. But in this place by the daughters of Jerusalem we ought to understand souls desiring God, but still living in this pilgrimage. The bride, then, adjures the daughters of Jerusalem, that they tell the bridegroom that she languishes with love, when a faithful soul adjures other faithful souls desiring God, that they insinuate to Christ, and announce to him, that she is held by love of him. And well does she say that she languishes with love: for the more she begins to desire the heavenly 330Dthings, the more she languishes toward the things that are of the world. Nor is it a wonder if such are said to languish, since the Apostle calls them dead: « You are dead (he says), and your life is hidden with Christ. » Thus, then, to the adjuring bride the daughters of Jerusalem answer.

« What manner of one is thy beloved above another beloved, O most beautiful of women? what manner of one is thy beloved above another beloved, that thou hast so adjured us? » This happens then when a faithful one addresses a faithful brother, and they mutually suggest to one another words of heavenly desires, by which they kindle themselves to the love of the heavenly things. Show me, she says, now desiring, now longing to come to the vision of my Creator, what manner of one thy beloved is, that 331Ais, how Christ ought to be loved. Long since, she says, I began to fear him, but now, charity expelling fear, I long to hear words by which I may be kindled to his love. Thou, therefore, who already seekest him with love, and languishest with love of him, tell me what manner of one thy beloved is above another beloved. The beloved above another beloved is the Son from the Father, as he is God from God, light from light. Or also « thy beloved above another beloved » is Christ from that part by which he is to be loved, not from that by which he is to be feared. For to fear belongs rather to the imperfect, whom fear calls back from sins, but to love belongs to the perfect. Now, therefore, she asks what manner of one the beloved is above another beloved, that is, how Christ ought to be loved, or what manner of one he is toward those who have learned to love him so greatly.

331B« My beloved is white and ruddy, chosen out of thousands. » The voice of the answering Church: « My beloved is white and ruddy »: white by virginity, ruddy by his passion. White, because without sin; for he lived without sin: « for he did no sin, neither was guile found in his mouth. » « Ruddy, » because « he washed us from our sins in his blood. « Chosen out of thousands. » For by a singular grace the man Christ shone forth in the whole mass of the human race, because through him God purposed to save the human race, and he himself is the mediator of God and men, Jesus Christ; of whom the Psalmist says: « There is none that doth good, no not one. » And Solomon: « One man among a thousand I have found, » that is, Christ; and he himself in the psalm: 331C« I am alone until I pass. » « Chosen, then, out of thousands, » because out of the whole multitude of the saints he alone was worthy to hear: « This is my beloved Son, in whom I am well pleased. »

« His head is the finest gold. » The head of the bridegroom is God, according to what the Apostle says, « The head of Christ is God, » which is compared to the finest gold, because as gold is more precious than all metals, so God almighty surpasses and rises above the good things made by him. Whence the Lord: « None is good but God alone. » The head of the bridegroom, then, is the finest gold, because the goodness of God is unspeakable, transcending the goodness of all things, because nothing is good, except by participation in this highest good. « His locks are like the shoots of palms. » The locks of the bridegroom are the multitudes of the faithful, 331Dcleaving to God through faith and love, which are compared to the shoots of palms. The shoots of palms are the more forward and more excellent branches, sometimes of golden color, curling, and always tending toward the heights, and never losing their greenness. Such, then, does the Church call the locks of her bridegroom, namely curling and glowing. Some say that the shoots of palms are a kind of aromatic tree, which in Latin they call the fir or the spatha. For in Greek the fir is called ἐλάτη. And this kind is fit for making ointments. But here we ought to understand the fir, not that great tree which is fit for ships and houses, but (as we said) a kind of aromatic tree: this according to the letter concerning the shoots of palms. The locks of the bridegroom, then, 332Aare compared to the shoots of palms, because the multitudes of the faithful both rejoice in the greenness of faith, and are lifted up to desire the eternal things, and taste the sweetness of the heavenly delight, which the more they perceive, the more they seem to themselves to be sinners and contemptible in their own eyes. Whence follows: « Black as a raven. » For the locks of the bridegroom are black as a raven, because the multitudes of the faithful are conscious of their own frailty, and know that they have no good of themselves. Or also they are « shoots of palms » in hope and victory: but « black as a raven, » as despised and contemptible in the oppressions of this world. The saints are shoots of palms, because through the grace of God they press toward the palm of heavenly victory; but black as a raven, because they know themselves to be weak and sinners 332Bof themselves. The locks of the bridegroom can also be taken as the angelic powers, which cleave especially to God, as the locks to the head, and contemplate him from near at hand. And these are well compared to the shoots, because they are never bent from the uprightness of their state, but remain immaculate in the dignity of their office; and the more they contemplate the brightness of their Creator, the more they see, in comparison with him, how of little use is everything created, and therefore they are said to be black as a raven.

« His eyes are as doves upon the little streams of waters, which are washed with milk. » His eyes are the gifts of the Holy Spirit. Which John shows in the Apocalypse, saying: « I saw a lamb as it were slain, having seven horns and seven eyes, which are the seven 332Cspirits of God sent into all the earth. » Whence also they are well compared to doves upon the little streams of waters, because the Holy Spirit is delighted with pure and sincere minds. For the doves settle not upon pools or marshes, but upon the little streams of waters, because (as has been said) the Holy Spirit makes for himself a dwelling of chaste and clean minds. « Which are washed with milk, » because (as has been said) the Holy Spirit is understood by the name of milk, that is, the grace of God, which is bestowed upon the Church through the Holy Spirit, and by which, as little ones by milk, so the Church is nourished. We can understand by the eyes of the bridegroom the teachers, who are compared to doves for their innocence and simplicity. These doves settle upon the little streams, that they may from afar look out for and beware of the coming of the hawk; so also the holy teachers, poured over with the water 332Dof baptism, are whitened with the brightness of chastity and holiness, « and settle beside the fullest streams, » that is, beside the overflowing knowledge of the Scriptures. But if we think there is any difference between the little streams and the fullest streams, it must be said that by the little streams are designated the Scriptures of the Old Testament; by the fullest streams is understood the copious Scripture of the holy Gospel. These doves, then, are both upon the little streams of waters, and settle beside the fullest streams, because the holy teachers assiduously search out the mysteries both of the Old Testament and of the New.

« His cheeks are as beds of aromatics, set by the perfumers. » The cheeks of the bridegroom are taken as the modest tenderness of Christ, or his severity, or the bearing of his countenance. 333AFor these were his cheeks, when he exulted in the spirit, as the Gospel says, or when he wept over the dead Lazarus, or when he grieved over Jerusalem that was to perish, and many things of this kind. These cheeks of the bridegroom are compared to beds of aromatics; for as beds of aromatics, excellently composed and ordered, delight the sight of the beholders, and offer them the grace of their odor, so the meekness of Christ delighted the absent by its fame, and the present by the very fact, and provoked them to his love. These beds are set by the perfumers. The perfumers are the prophets or the apostles, of whom the one described all these things as things to come, the other as things already done. « His lips are lilies dripping the first myrrh. » By the lips of the bridegroom are taken the words of our Lord, which are compared to lilies, because they announce the rewards of eternal brightness: 333Bthey drip the first myrrh, because they teach that one must come to these things through the mortification of the flesh. Or also the lips of the Lord are compared to lilies, because they teach the works of chastity and purity. They drip the first myrrh, because they insinuate that whatever adversities are to be patiently endured. The lips of the Lord were lilies, when, teaching on the mount, he said: « Blessed are the poor in spirit, blessed are the meek, blessed are the merciful, blessed are the clean of heart. » They drip the first myrrh, when after these he added: « Blessed are they that suffer persecution for justice' sake. »

« His hands are turned and golden. » For by the hands of the bridegroom is fitly taken the working of the Savior. For as one easily works with a lathe, and needs no rule, so the working of the Savior was easy: for whatever 333Che willed, he at once accomplished, because « he spoke, and they were made; » and of whom the Evangelist says, « He was as one having power in his word. » And they were golden, because his working was carried out by the power of the divinity: for in gold the divinity is figured, because as gold surpasses the other metals and is more precious, so the divinity surpasses all its own works in an unspeakable manner. « Full of hyacinths. » The hyacinth is a stone of an airy color. What, then, is taken in the hyacinths but heavenly hope and desire? And the hands of the Lord are full of hyacinths, because all his working stirs us up to the hope and desire of the heavenly things. The hyacinth is also an aromatic flower, of purple color and good odor. The hands of the Lord are full of hyacinths, because on the cross they were pierced by nails, and were sprinkled by the redness of his own blood as with 333Da purple color. But the former exposition of the hyacinth as a stone fits better here. In another translation « stone » is found. For his hands are called turned, full of tharsis. Tharsis is a stone which we call chalcedony. « His belly is of ivory. » Among the members of the human body nothing is more frail than the belly, nothing more tender: and therefore by the belly is taken the frailty of the assumed humanity in Christ. But ivory is the bone of the elephant, which animal they say is most chaste, and of a most cold nature: whence also it is vehemently desired by the dragon, which is of a warm nature. The belly of the bridegroom, then, is of ivory, because the assumed humanity of the Redeemer admitted no corruption, no defect. For he did no sin, 334Aneither was any guile found in him. He assumed indeed the frailty of the flesh, but yet he was free of all sin, as the brazen serpent, lifted up by Moses in the desert, had indeed the figure of a serpent, but lacked the malice of poison. « Set with sapphires. » The sapphire is a stone, having the color of a serene sky, whence in the vision of the Lord it is said: « And they saw the God of Israel, and under his feet as it were a work of sapphire stone, and as the heaven when it is serene. » By the sapphires, then, are understood the works of the divine majesty, which are accomplished in the flesh. The belly of the bridegroom, then, was set with sapphires, because the humanity of Christ shone with heavenly virtues. And it is not said to be full of sapphires, but « set, » namely in such a way that among the sapphires there appeared the whiteness of ivory, because the Lord so 334Bworked those things which were of the divinity, that he nonetheless accomplished those which were of the humanity, and did not leave undone those which were of the divinity. For the working of the divinity and of the humanity in Christ is distinct. For that he hungered, that he thirsted, that he wept, that he was wearied, that at the last he could be crucified and die, were works of the humanity: but that he raised the dead, that he succored all the sick, that he raised himself from the dead, were most evident works of the divinity.

« His legs are pillars of marble, that are founded upon golden bases. » The legs of the bridegroom are the journeyings of the Savior, by which he deigned to become man and to come to us. Which are rightly compared to pillars of marble, because nothing is stronger than marble, nothing straighter than a pillar. 334CAnd the legs of the bridegroom are pillars of marble, because all the journeyings of the Lord are both strong and straight. Of his strength the Psalmist says: « He hath rejoiced as a giant to run the way, his going out is from the highest heaven. » Of his straightness elsewhere: « The Lord is just, and hath loved justices, his countenance hath beheld equity; » and, « All the ways of the Lord are mercy and truth. » These legs are founded upon golden bases, because all things that were to be done through Christ or in Christ were preordained and predetermined by the counsel of the divinity before the foundation of the world. For gold, which (as has often been said) is more splendid and more precious than the other metals, signifies the most sincere secret of the divine counsel. For from the golden bases the pillars of marble proceed, because from the beginning of the world were preordained the incarnation, the nativity, 334Dthe passion, and the resurrection of the Savior, and the other mysteries by which the human race was to be redeemed. Whence also the Prophet, praising God, said: O Lord my God, I will honor thee, I will say praise to thy name, who dost wonderful things: thy ancient counsel let it come true. « His appearance is as of Lebanon, choice as the cedars. » Lebanon is a mountain of Phoenicia, the boundary of Judaea toward the north, and it is higher than the other mountains. The trees of that mountain surpass in height and appearance and strength the timbers of the other forests. As (he says) Lebanon is loftier than the other mountains of the land of Judaea, so our Redeemer is more comely than all the elect, because he is beautiful in form above the sons of men. And as it is more beautiful and taller than the other 335Atrees, so also he, by the grace of the divinity, surpasses all the saints from the beginning of the world.

« His throat is most sweet. » Above, by the lips of the bridegroom, his words were understood; now, by the throat, the inner sweetness of those very words is signified, by which our understanding is refreshed. Whence also the Psalmist: « Taste and see that the Lord is sweet. » And many indeed can easily understand the words of the Lord, but few there are who are able to penetrate their sweetness; that is, who from love and desire strive to fulfill the precepts of God. « And he is wholly desirable. » As if he should say: Why do I linger in describing him member by member? My bridegroom is wholly desirable. Wholly, he says, because he is perfect God, perfect 335Bman. He is wholly desirable, then, because he kindles the angels and all the elect to desire of him, according to what the Apostle says: « On whom the angels desire to look, » and the more nearly he is perceived, the more with greater desire he provokes the beholders to look upon him. « Such is my beloved, and this is my friend, O daughters of Jerusalem. » My beloved, because through faith and charity I cleave to him: my friend, because he deigned to make me, redeemed from the bond of sin, his friend and a partaker of his secrets. Whom the more anyone loves, the more worthy of his friendship he becomes.

« Whither is thy beloved gone, O most beautiful of women? » It is the voice of the daughters of Jerusalem, that is, of the Churches, or also of the holy souls. For 335Cthe bride of Christ is the Church, the daughters of Jerusalem are the Church herself, which consists of the individual souls of the faithful. They say, then: « Whither is thy beloved gone, O most beautiful of women? Whither is thy beloved turned aside? and we will seek him with thee. » For above the bride herself had adjured the daughters of Jerusalem, that they announce to the bridegroom that she languished with love of him; the same daughters of Jerusalem had asked what manner of one the bridegroom was, and she thereupon had set forth the form of her bridegroom; now the daughters of Jerusalem themselves ask whither that bridegroom is gone, that they may seek him with her: this the holy souls do, when they converse with one another of eternal salvation, glowing with love of Christ, and desiring the sight of him; or they diligently investigate him in whose hearts especially Christ rests, that 335Dby their example and conversation they may be edified. But it is to be noted that it is said, Whither is thy beloved gone, or « whither is thy beloved turned aside? » For as it were for a time he turns aside while he is sought, that he may excite a greater desire to seek him. For to this end he very often defers the vows and prayers of the saints, that they may begin to desire him more ardently, as also once to Mary, seeking him, he did not at once show himself, but appearing in the guise of a gardener, at last showed that he himself was the one who was being sought. Christ, then, always hears his elect, but always as it were turns aside, when he does not at once grant what is asked. And well is it said, « we will seek him with thee, » for he who seeks Christ without the Church can err and be wearied, but cannot make progress.

CAPUT VI.

336A

« Dilectus meus descendit in hortum suum ad areolam aromatis, ut pascatur in hortis, et lilia colligat. » Jam supra docti sumus qui sit hortus Christi, sancta videlicet Ecclesia, de qua superius dictum est: « Hortus conclusus soror mea sponsa. » Descendit (inquit) Christus in hortum suum ad areolam, quem ipse praeparavit et excoluit, quem virtutum germinibus consevit, quem sua gratia illustravit, quem muro suae custodiae ab incursu malignorum spirituum defendit. Et bene in hortum suum descendisse dicitur, nam in horto sepeliri voluit, et primum Mariae Magdalenae in horto apparuit, ibique quodammodo primitias Ecclesiae consecravit. Descendisse dicitur, quando de superioribus et coelestibus 336Bsedibus in hortum descendit, quem ipse humili loco plantavit: sed ad hoc ipse descendit, ut hortus (id est Ecclesia) ejus ad ipsum ascenderet. Unde et in sequentibus de horto, id est Ecclesia sua, dicit: « Quae est ista quae ascendit per desertum? » « Ad areolam (inquit) aromatis. » Per areolam anima cujusque fidelis intelligitur, quae disciplina coelesti exculta et diligenter composita, ac laterum parilitate exquadrata est. « Ut pascatur (inquit) in hortis, » id est ut piis sanctorum laboribus delectetur. Ipse enim bonis nostris pascitur, in tantum, ut in paupere ipse reficiatur. « Et lilia (inquit) colligat, » hoc est, ut sanctas animas virtutis maturitate ad perfectum candorem perductas, ab hoc mundo ad se colligat et secum in aeterna beatitudine gaudere faciat. Lilia enim sanctae sunt 336Canimae, virtutum studiis et bonis operibus candidatae.

« Ego dilecto meo, et dilectus meus mihi, qui pascitur inter lilia. » « Ego dilecto meo, » subauditur, gratiam pastionum praeparo; « et dilectus meus mihi, » gratiam perfectionis, vel praemium aeternae remunerationis praestabit. Sive, « ego dilecto meo » in meipsa mansionem paro, et dilectus meus mihi, quia ipse in me habitat, et me in se habitare facit, sicut ipse in Evangelio dicit, « Ego in vobis, et vos in me. »

« Pulchra es, amica mea, suavis et decora sicut Hierusalem. » Quaerebat Ecclesia sponsum suum tanquam absentem, sed ille ut se semper praesentem ostenderet, eique de se colloquenti seque desideranti semper adesse demonstraret, cito respondet: « Pulchra es, amica mea, suavis et decora sicut Hierusalem. » 336DPulchra es, quia maculam et rugam non habes peccati, sed omni nitore virtutum vernas. « Amica mea, » Ecclesia vel anima quaeque fidelis. « Suavis et decora sicut Hierusalem. » Hierusalem, quae visio pacis interpretatur, illam supernam civitatem designat, in qua est vera pax et felicitas; quia ibi sic gaudent sancti de munere, ut non sit quod doleant de corruptione. Ergo Ecclesia, quae adhuc in terris est, vel anima quaeque fidelis, ad similitudinem illius Hierusalem suavis est et decora, quia illam pacem in quantum potest imitatur. Sequitur: « terribilis ut castrorum acies ordinata. » Terribilis est Ecclesia malignis spiritibus, et est ordinata ut castrorum acies, cum videlicet unusquisque fidelis ordinem 337Asuum et vocationem suam recte custodit. Tres enim sunt ordines Ecclesiae: doctorum, continentium, et conjugatorum; quae species in tribus viris illis demonstratae sunt, Noe, Daniel et Job. Noe enim qui inter fluctus arcam rexit, sanctos doctores significat; Daniel, caelebs et castus, continentium ordinem significat; Job, qui uxorem habuit et filios, ordinem conjugatorum exprimit. Ergo Ecclesia ordinata est sicut acies castrorum, quia unusquisque ordo Ecclesiae velut in acie contra hostes malignorum spirituum consistit, dum sancti doctores praedicationi et doctrinae operam impendunt. Continentes, quae mundi sunt contemnentes, tantum quae Dei sunt cogitant, et libera a terrenis occupationibus mente, soli Deo vacant. Conjugati eleemosynis et caeteris actualis 337Bvitae operibus bonis insistunt, sicque utuntur mundo, ut quae Dei sunt non deserant. Haec ergo ordinatissima dispositio Ecclesiae terribilis est immundis spiritibus, velut acies castrorum ad bellum ordinata. Sive pulchra est Ecclesia et decora sicut Hierusalem in virtutibus; terribilis vero ut castrorum acies ordinata, quia imperium suum et potestatem proferre non cessat usque ad terminos totius orbis. Sive terribilis est ut castrorum acies ordinata, quia sic unitate charitatis connexa est et conjuncta, ut nulla vi maligni hostis, nulla peste discordiae possit penetrari: nihil enim sic terret malignos spiritus quomodo charitas.

« Averte oculos tuos a me, quia ipsi me avolare fecerunt. » Ac si diceret: Ego quidem dedi tibi oculos 337Ccolumbinos, quibus me intueri et arcana Scripturarum acutissime penetrare valeres; sed cave ne illos ad me videndum modo intendere velis, quia dum in praesenti vivis, non potes: « Non enim videbit me homo et vivet. » Cum autem vinculis carnis absoluta ad me veneris, tunc me aperta visione videbis, et implebitur quod in Evangelio promittitur: « Qui autem diligit me, diligetur a Patre meo, et ego diligam eum, et manifestabo ei meipsum. » Quod autem dicitur, « Quia ipsi me avolare fecerunt, » non est putandum quod Deus quaerentes se deserat, et ab illis recedat, cum ipse praecipiat: « Quaerite et invenietis, » sed ita potius accipiendum, quia quanto amplius quisque divinitatem majestatis Dei perscrutari voluerit, eo amplius intelligit quam imperscrutabilis 337Datque incomprehensibilis sit. Et hoc est quod Psalmista dicit: « Accedet homo ad cor altum, et exaltabitur Deus. » Ac si diceret: Licet homo ad videndum Deum cor sublevet, exaltabitur, quia quantus sit comprehendi non potest. « Capilli tui sicut grex caprarum, quae apparuerunt de Galaad. » Hi versiculi superius expositi sunt, sed non pigeat iterato exponere, quos non piguit Spiritum sanctum repetere. Ideo enim bis istos versiculos posuit, ut certissimum et stabile teneremus quod sub repetitione confirmari audiremus. Capilli Ecclesiae sanctae, multiplices sunt subtilium cogitationum varietates. Et bene comparantur gregi caprarum, nam capra peccatoris figura est. Unde et in lege praecitur 338Aut quicunque per ignorantiam peccaverit, et cognoverit reatum suum, offerat pro delicto suo capram in holocaustum. Capilli ergo Ecclesiae gregi caprarum comparantur, quia sancti, qui sunt membra Ecclesiae, omnes reatus cogitationis suae per dignam poenitentiam et per lacrymas justae compunctionis delere satagunt. Bene autem de monte Galaad apparuisse dicuntur. Galaad interpretatur acervus testimonii. Ideoque per Galaad mens sanctorum designatur, qui sunt acervus testimonii, quoniam omnia quae saeculi sunt negligere, et solum Deum desiderare evidentissimis attestatur indiciis. Potest etiam per Galaad ipse Dominus et Redemptor noster intelligi. Galaad enim et montis et civitatis nomen est. De monte ergo Galaad grex caprarum apparuit, 338Bquia Ecclesia super Christum est fundata, sicut ipse dicit: « Non potest civitas abscondi supra montem posita. » Ipse est acervus testimonii, quia multitudo prophetarum et patriarcharum gestis et scriptis ei testimonium perhibent.

« Dentes tui sicut greges ovium, quae ascenderunt de lavacro, omnes gemellis fetibus, et sterilis non est in eis. » Per dentes Ecclesiae firma sermonum ejus stabilitas intelligitur. Qui bene gregi ovium comparantur quae de lavacro ascenderunt, quia verba Ecclesiae nihil nisi innocentiam et mansuetudinem sonant, nihilque in se foedum, nihil turpe vel immundum retinent, sed lavacro sinceritatis et puritatis lavantur, audientes ab Apostolo: « Sermo malus ex ore vestro non procedat. » Bene autem 338Comnes gemellis fetibus esse dicuntur, quia sermo sanctorum nihil aliud sonat nec loquitur, nisi quod ad dilect onem Dei et proximi pertinet. Solummodo enim ea sancti loqui debent, quae ad perpetuam corporum et animarum pertineant sanitatem.

« Sicut cortex mali punici, sic genae tuae absque occultis tuis. » In genis diximus superius sanctorum castam verecundiam figurari; in cortice mali punici, mysterium passionis Christi, audientes a Paulo apostolo: Noli erubescere crucem Christi, neque me vinctum ejus. Et haec quidem magna sunt, quae in aperto sunt; maxima vero sunt illa quae sunt intrinsecus, hoc est quae in futuro sanctis reservantur.

« Sexaginta sunt reginae, et octaginta concubinae, et adolescentularum non est numerus. » Reginae sunt 338Ddoctores sanctae Ecclesiae, qui merito fidei et scientiae thoro regis aeterni propinquant, et spirituales Deo filios pariunt. Sexagenarius numerus ex denario et senario perficitur. Denarius enim per senarium multiplicatus, in sexagenarium consurgit. Et denario significantur divina praecepta, propter decalogum legis; senarius vero pro perfectione ponitur, quia sex diebus perfecit Deus opera sua. Reginae ergo sexaginta esse dicuntur, quia eos significant, ut diximus, qui perfectione scientiae et operis pollent, et propter solum amorem Conditoris sui et intuitum supernae remunerationis praedicant. Hic etiam numerus, id est sexagenarius, ex duodenario quinquies ducta conficitur. Nam quinquies duodeni, vel duodecies 339Aquini, sexaginta fiunt. Reginae ergo sexaginta esse dicuntur, quia illos significant qui in Ecclesia constituti, quinque sui corporis sensus juxta institutionem duodecim apostolorum caste disponere noverunt. Concubinae eos significant qui non sincere Christum praedicant, sed propter lucra temporalia vel propter laudes populares. Nam et istae sunt reginae, et ante thorum regis per scientiam accedunt, et filios spirituales generant, sed a corona aeterni regni alienae exsistunt. Et bene octogenario numero comprehenduntur, nam hic numerus ex denario et octonario conficitur. Octogenarius vero in malo accipitur aliquando, propter quaternarium, quo multiplicato consistit. Quaternarius enim temporalia quaeque et praesentia significat, propter quatuor mundi climata, vel quatuor 339Banni tempora. Recte ergo concubinae istae octoginta esse dicuntur, quia (ut dictum est) propter sola temporalia et visibilia praedicant, et spiritualia minus curant. De talibus Apostolus dicebat: « Sunt quidam Christum annuntiantes non sincere, » etc., donec dicit: « In hoc gaudeo, sed et gaudebo. » « Adolescentularum non est numerus. » Adolescentulae sunt animae jam in Christo regeneratae, quae deposita primi hominis vetustate, in Christo renovatae sunt, necdum tamen sunt nobiles, necdum thoro regis aptae, quia necdum ad perfectionem Christum praedicandi pervenerunt. Quarum non est numerus, quia infinitus est numerus animarum in Christo credentium, nec ab ullo homine comprehendi potest. Caeterum Deo numerati sunt omnes 339Celecti. Nam apud quem etiam stellarum numerus comprehensus est, ignorari non potest electorum numerus.

« Una est columba mea, perfecta mea, » hoc est una catholica per universum mundum diffusa Ecclesia, quae ex reginis et adolescentibus constans, etiam concubinas habet, hoc est, quosdam qui nomine tenus Christiani vel doctores dicuntur. Una est ergo Ecclesia, quia scissuram et schismata non recipit, sed sicut unus est Deus, una fides, unum baptisma, ita una est generalis Ecclesia: quae recte et columba vocatur, quia Spiritus sancti dote, qui in specie columbae apparuit, Christo sponsata et sanctificata est. « Una est matri suae, electa genitrici suae. » Mater nostra illa est coelestis Hierusalem, de qua 339DApostolus dicit: « Illa autem quae sursum est Hierusalem, libera est, quae est mater nostra. » Haec ergo Ecclesia una est matri suae, quia ad exemplum illius Ecclesiae, quae jam Christo fruitur, ipsa in hac peregrinatione instituitur et informatur, atque ad illam beatitudinem, in qua cum Christo regnat, pervenire nititur. « Viderunt illam filiae, et beatissimam praedicaverunt reginae et concubinae, et laudaverunt eam. » Quas superius adolescentulas, hic filias appellat. Reginas vero et concubinas in hoc loco ut superius intelligamus. Filiae ergo, reginae et concubinae praedicant Ecclesiam et laudant, quia universitas fidelium catholicam admiratur Ecclesiam. Ipsa enim Ecclesia, quae ex multis fidelium constat personis, 340Acatholicam (id est universalem) Ecclesiam conficit.

« Quae est ista, quae progreditur quasi aurora consurgens? » Vox est Synagogae. In fine siquidem saeculi cum plebs Judaica ad praedicationem Eliae et Enoch conversa fuerit ad Christum, ipsa quoque admirabitur, dicens: Quae est ista? id est quanta et qualis est Ecclesia quae progreditur? hoc est, proficit de virtute in virtutem. Non enim stare, sed progredi dicitur Ecclesia, quia non in uno loco contenta, sed totum mundum coelesti praedicatione occupavit. Quasi aurora progreditur, quia transactis tenebris infidelitatis, lumen veritatis habere ostendit. « Pulchra ut luna. » Lunam ferunt physici a sole illuminari. Et Ecclesia pulchra est ut luna, quia charitate 340Bsponsi sui Christi illuminatur, et ejus gratia resplendet. Sive pulchra est ut luna in praesenti vita, ubi aliquando concessa sibi pace et securitate crescit, aliquando adversitatibus obtrita decrescit. « Electa ut sol, » in alia vita, ubi perpetuo resplendebit gaudio de visione Conditoris sui. Sive electa est ut sol, quia illum verum imitari nititur, de quo scriptum est: « Vobis timentibus nomen meum orietur sol justitiae. » Hunc solem imitatur vivendo in humilitate et justitia et pietate. « Terribilis ut castrorum acies ordinata. » Terribilis aeriis tempestatibus, ordinata ut castrorum acies, id est charitatis unitate unita, ut nullis tentationibus penetrari valeat.

« Descendi ad hortum nucum. » Laudata Ecclesia a Synogoga, respondet: « Descendi ad hortum nucum. » 340CHortus nucum est vita praesens. Nam sicut in nuce tegmen quidem durissimum videtur, sed nucleus latet, ita in praesenti vita nostrae conscientiae clausae sunt, et non apparent, donec fracta testa corporis manifestentur. Descendit ergo ad hortum nucum Ecclesia, quando per doctores suos vitam singulorum considerat. Unde sequitur: « ut viderem poma convallium. » Poma convallium sunt virtutes, quae humilitate condiuntur. « Descendi (inquit) ad hortum, ut viderem poma convallium, » id est ut considerarem sanctos, excellentia quidem virtutum praeditos, sed humilitate depressos. « Et inspicerem si floruisset vinea. » Hoc est ut considerarem qui in studio sanctarum virtutum proficerent. « Et germinassent mala punica. » Hoc est ut eos quoque perquirerem, 340Dqui jam apti sunt ad imitandam passionem Christi. Nam mala punica, ut saepe dictum est, mysterium passionis Christi significant. Considerat ergo Ecclesia per praelatos suos, qui in virtutibus, vel qui jam perfecti sunt, ut imitantes passionem Christi, pro illo quoque idonei sint sanguinem fundere, quales erant illi quibus Apostolus dicebat: « Vobis datum est, non solum ut credatis in Christum, sed ut pro illo patiamini. »

« Nescivi. » Vox est Synagogae. Quae videns tantam Ecclesiae gratiam inspecta veritate Evangelii, dolet quod antea non cognoverit fidem Christi, et quasi excusans se quod tarde conversa sit, dicit: Nescivi, o sponsa Christi Ecclesia. Nescivi, id est non 341Acognovi tantam gratiam et tanta dona spiritualium virtutum tibi a sponso tuo collata. « Anima mea conturbavit me propter quadrigas Aminadab. » Conturbata (inquit) sum nimia animi sollicitudine, propter tam subitam Evangelii praedicationem. Sciebam enim legem et prophetas divinitus datos, et ideo cum subito vidissem praedicari Evangelium, conturbata sum propter quadrigas Aminadab. Aminadab abnepos fuit Judae, per quem generatio Christi contexitur. Interpretatur Aminadab populi mei spontaneus, quia cum esset Deus, sponte factus est homo; cum esset conditor et creator, sola benignitate sua factus est portio populi sui. Et est sensus: Conturbata (inquit) sum propter subitam Evangelii praedicationem, quae veluti velocissimae quadrigae, totum subito 341Bmundum pervolavit. Et bene hanc praedicationem non currus, sed quadrigas appellat, quia Evangelii praedicatio quatuor evangelistarum auctoritate consistit, et quatuor Evangelia quasi quadrigae sunt Novi Testamenti, cui praesidet auriga ipse Christus temperans et disponens ipse cursum Evangeliorum.

« Revertere, revertere, Sunamitis. » Vox est Ecclesiae, Synagogam ad fidem invitantis: « Revertere, revertere, Sunamitis. » Revertere, o Synagoga ab infidelitate ad fidem, revertere ab odio ad dilectionem. Sunamitis interpretatur captiva vel despecta. Talis erat Synagoga ante adventum Christi, captiva videlicet vinculo diaboli, et despecta a Deo, quia Christum ad salutem suam missum non cognovit. « Revertere, revertere, ut intueamur te, » id est ut 341Cpulchritudinem tuae castitatis aspiciamus.

CHAPTER VI.

336A

« My beloved is come down into his garden to the bed of aromatics, that he may feed in the gardens, and gather the lilies. » We have already been taught above what the garden of Christ is, namely the holy Church, of which it was said above: « An enclosed garden is my sister, my bride. » Christ is come down (she says) into his garden to the bed, which he himself prepared and cultivated, which he sowed with the shoots of the virtues, which he made bright by his grace, which he defended by the wall of his keeping from the assault of the malign spirits. And well is he said to have come down into his garden, for in a garden he willed to be buried, and first appeared in a garden to Mary Magdalene, and there in a certain manner consecrated the firstfruits of the Church. He is said to have come down, when from the higher and heavenly 336Bseats he came down into the garden, which he himself had planted in a lowly place: but to this end he himself came down, that the garden (that is, the Church) might ascend to him. Whence also in what follows he says of the garden, that is, of his Church: « Who is this that ascendeth up through the desert? » « To the bed (he says) of aromatics. » By the bed is understood the soul of every faithful one, which, cultivated by heavenly discipline and diligently composed, has been squared by the evenness of its sides. « That he may feed (he says) in the gardens, » that is, that he may be delighted by the pious labors of the saints. For he himself is fed with our good things, so much so that in the poor man he himself is refreshed. « And that he may gather (he says) the lilies, » that is, that the holy souls, brought by the maturity of virtue to perfect brightness, he may gather to himself from this world, and make them rejoice with him in eternal beatitude. For the lilies are the holy 336Csouls, made white by the pursuits of the virtues and by good works.

« I to my beloved, and my beloved to me, who feedeth among the lilies. » « I to my beloved, » that is, understood, I prepare the grace of feedings; « and my beloved to me, » he will bestow the grace of perfection, or the reward of eternal recompense. Or, « I to my beloved » prepare a dwelling in myself, and my beloved to me, because he himself dwells in me, and makes me to dwell in him, as he himself says in the Gospel, « I in you, and you in me. »

« Thou art beautiful, my love, sweet and comely as Jerusalem. » The Church was seeking her bridegroom as though absent, but he, that he might show himself always present, and demonstrate that he was always at hand to her who conversed of him and desired him, quickly answers: « Thou art beautiful, my love, sweet and comely as Jerusalem. » 336DThou art beautiful, because thou hast not the spot and wrinkle of sin, but thou art green with every brightness of the virtues. « My love, » the Church or every faithful soul. « Sweet and comely as Jerusalem. » Jerusalem, which is interpreted as the vision of peace, designates that heavenly city, in which is true peace and happiness; because there the saints so rejoice in the gift, that there is nothing for them to grieve over concerning corruption. Therefore the Church, which is still on earth, or every faithful soul, in the likeness of that Jerusalem, is sweet and comely, because she imitates that peace as far as she can. There follows: « terrible as an army set in array. » The Church is terrible to the malign spirits, and is set in array as an army, when, namely, each of the faithful rightly keeps his own order 337Aand his own vocation. For there are three orders of the Church: of teachers, of the continent, and of the married; which kinds were shown in those three men, Noah, Daniel, and Job. For Noah, who ruled the ark amid the waves, signifies the holy teachers; Daniel, unmarried and chaste, signifies the order of the continent; Job, who had a wife and sons, expresses the order of the married. The Church, therefore, is set in array as an army, because each order of the Church stands as it were in battle-line against the enemies, the malign spirits, while the holy teachers give their labor to preaching and doctrine. The continent, despising the things that are of the world, think only on the things that are of God, and, with a mind free from earthly occupations, are free for God alone. The married press on with alms and the other good works of the active 337Blife, and so use the world that they do not forsake the things that are of God. This most orderly disposition of the Church, then, is terrible to the unclean spirits, like an army set in array for battle. Or the Church is beautiful and comely as Jerusalem in the virtues; but terrible as an army set in array, because she does not cease to extend her rule and power unto the ends of the whole world. Or she is terrible as an army set in array, because she is so connected and joined in the unity of charity, that by no force of the malign enemy, by no pest of discord, can she be penetrated: for nothing so terrifies the malign spirits as charity does.

« Turn away thy eyes from me, for they have made me flee away. » As if he should say: I indeed gave thee dove-like eyes, 337Cwith which thou couldst behold me and most keenly penetrate the secrets of the Scriptures; but beware lest thou wish now to strain them upon seeing me, because while thou livest in the present thou canst not: « For man shall not see me and live. » But when, loosed from the bonds of the flesh, thou shalt come to me, then thou shalt see me by an open vision, and there shall be fulfilled what is promised in the Gospel: « But he that loveth me shall be loved of my Father, and I will love him, and will manifest myself to him. » But that it is said, « For they have made me flee away, » it is not to be thought that God forsakes those who seek him, and withdraws from them, since he himself commands: « Seek and you shall find, » but it is rather to be taken thus, that the more anyone wishes to search out the divinity of the majesty of God, the more he understands how unsearchable 337Dand incomprehensible it is. And this is what the Psalmist says: « Man shall come to a deep heart, and God shall be exalted. » As if he should say: Though a man lift up his heart to see God, he shall be exalted, because how great he is cannot be comprehended. « Thy hair is as a flock of goats, which have appeared from Gilead. » These little verses have been expounded above, but let it not be irksome to expound again what it was not irksome for the Holy Spirit to repeat. For to this end he set these verses twice, that we might hold most certain and firm what we heard confirmed under repetition. The hairs of the holy Church are the manifold varieties of subtle thoughts. And they are well compared to a flock of goats, for the goat is a figure of the sinner. Whence also in the law it is commanded 338Athat whosoever has sinned through ignorance, and has come to know his guilt, should offer for his offense a she-goat in holocaust. The hairs of the Church, then, are compared to a flock of goats, because the saints, who are members of the Church, strive to blot out all the guilt of their thought through worthy penance and through the tears of just compunction. And well are they said to have appeared from the mount of Gilead. Gilead is interpreted as a heap of testimony. And therefore by Gilead is designated the mind of the saints, who are a heap of testimony, since it attests by most evident tokens that they neglect all things that are of the world, and desire God alone. By Gilead can also be understood our very Lord and Redeemer. For Gilead is the name both of a mountain and of a city. From the mount of Gilead, then, the flock of goats appeared, 338Bbecause the Church is founded upon Christ, as he himself says: « A city seated on a mountain cannot be hid. » He himself is the heap of testimony, because the multitude of the prophets and of the patriarchs bear him testimony by their deeds and writings.

« Thy teeth are as flocks of sheep, which came up from the washing, all with twin young, and there is none barren among them. » By the teeth of the Church is understood the firm stability of her words. Which are well compared to a flock of sheep that came up from the washing, because the words of the Church sound nothing but innocence and meekness, and retain in themselves nothing foul, nothing base or unclean, but are washed in the laver of sincerity and purity, hearing from the Apostle: « Let no evil word proceed out of your mouth. » And well 338Care they all said to be with twin young, because the discourse of the saints sounds and speaks nothing else than what pertains to the love of God and of neighbor. For the saints ought to speak only those things which pertain to the perpetual health of bodies and of souls.

« As the bark of a pomegranate, so are thy cheeks, besides that which is hidden within thee. » By the cheeks we said above that the chaste modesty of the saints is figured; by the bark of the pomegranate, the mystery of the passion of Christ, hearing from Paul the apostle: Be not ashamed of the cross of Christ, nor of me his prisoner. And these things indeed are great, which are in the open; but greatest are those things which are within, that is, which are reserved for the saints in the future.

« There are sixty queens, and eighty concubines, and of young maidens there is no number. » The queens are 338Dthe teachers of the holy Church, who by the merit of faith and knowledge draw near to the couch of the eternal King, and bring forth spiritual sons to God. The number sixty is completed out of ten and six. For ten multiplied by six rises to sixty. And by ten are signified the divine precepts, on account of the decalogue of the law; but six is put for perfection, because in six days God completed his works. The queens, then, are said to be sixty, because they signify those, as we said, who are strong in the perfection of knowledge and of work, and who preach for the sole love of their Creator and the regard of the heavenly recompense. This number too, that is, sixty, is made from twelve drawn out five times. For five twelves, or twelve 339Afives, make sixty. The queens, then, are said to be sixty, because they signify those who, established in the Church, have learned to dispose chastely the five senses of their body according to the institution of the twelve apostles. The concubines signify those who do not preach Christ sincerely, but for temporal gains or for popular praises. For these too are queens, and approach before the couch of the king through knowledge, and generate spiritual sons, but remain estranged from the crown of the eternal kingdom. And well are they comprised in the number eighty, for this number is made from ten and eight. But eighty is sometimes taken in a bad sense, on account of the four, by whose multiplication it consists. For four signifies all things temporal and present, on account of the four climes of the world, or the four 339Bseasons of the year. Rightly, then, are these concubines said to be eighty, because (as has been said) they preach for the sake of the temporal and visible things alone, and care less for the spiritual. Of such the Apostle said: « There are certain who announce Christ not sincerely, » and so on, until he says: « In this I rejoice, yea, and will rejoice. » « Of young maidens there is no number. » The young maidens are the souls already regenerated in Christ, which, having laid aside the oldness of the first man, are renewed in Christ, but are not yet noble, not yet fit for the couch of the king, because they have not yet come to the perfection of preaching Christ. Of whom there is no number, because infinite is the number of the souls believing in Christ, nor can it be comprehended by any man. But by God all the elect are numbered. For 339Cwith him, by whom even the number of the stars is comprehended, the number of the elect cannot be unknown.

« One is my dove, my perfect one, » that is, one Catholic Church spread throughout the whole world, which, consisting of queens and young maidens, has also concubines — that is, certain persons who are called Christians or teachers in name only. There is therefore one Church, because she admits no rending or schism, but just as there is one God, one faith, one baptism, so there is one universal Church: which is rightly also called a dove, because by the endowment of the Holy Spirit, who appeared in the form of a dove, she has been betrothed to Christ and sanctified. « She is the only one of her mother, the chosen one of her that bore her. » Our mother is that heavenly Jerusalem, of which 339Dthe Apostle says: « But that Jerusalem which is above is free, which is our mother. » This Church, therefore, is the only one of her mother, because after the example of that Church which already enjoys Christ, she herself is instructed and formed in this pilgrimage, and strives to come to that blessedness in which she reigns with Christ. « The daughters saw her, and declared her most blessed; the queens and concubines, and they praised her. » Those whom above he called young maidens, here he calls daughters. But the queens and concubines let us understand in this place as above. The daughters, therefore, the queens and concubines, proclaim the Church and praise her, because the whole company of the faithful marvels at the Catholic Church. For the Church herself, which consists of the many persons of the faithful, 340Amakes up the Catholic (that is, universal) Church.

« Who is she that cometh forth as the morning rising? » It is the voice of the Synagogue. For at the end of the age, when the Jewish people, at the preaching of Elijah and Enoch, shall have been converted to Christ, it too will marvel, saying: Who is she? that is, how great and of what kind is the Church that comes forth? that is, that advances from virtue to virtue. For the Church is said not to stand, but to advance, because, not content in one place, she has occupied the whole world with heavenly preaching. She comes forth as the morning, because, the darkness of unbelief having passed away, she shows that she possesses the light of truth. « Fair as the moon. » The natural philosophers hold that the moon is illumined by the sun. And the Church is fair as the moon, because she is illumined by the love 340Bof her spouse Christ, and shines again by his grace. Or she is fair as the moon in the present life, where at times, peace and security being granted her, she grows, and at times, crushed by adversities, she wanes. « Chosen as the sun, » in the other life, where she will shine again with perpetual joy at the vision of her Maker. Or she is chosen as the sun, because she strives to imitate that true one of whom it is written: « Unto you that fear my name the sun of justice shall arise. » This sun she imitates by living in humility and justice and piety. « Terrible as an army set in array. » Terrible in airy tempests, set in array like an army, that is, made one by the unity of charity, so that it can be penetrated by no temptations.

« I went down into the garden of nuts. » The Church, praised by the Synagogue, answers: « I went down into the garden of nuts. » 340CThe garden of nuts is the present life. For just as in the nut the covering indeed seems very hard, but the kernel lies hidden, so in the present life the things of our conscience are closed and do not appear, until, the shell of the body being broken, they are made manifest. The Church, therefore, goes down into the garden of nuts, when through her teachers she considers the life of individuals. Whence follows: « that I might see the fruits of the valleys. » The fruits of the valleys are the virtues, which are seasoned with humility. « I went down (she says) into the garden, that I might see the fruits of the valleys, » that is, that I might consider the saints, endowed indeed with excellence of virtues, but pressed down with humility. « And I would look if the vineyard had flourished. » That is, that I might consider who were making progress in the pursuit of the holy virtues. « And if the pomegranates had budded. » That is, that I might also seek out those 340Dwho are now fit to imitate the passion of Christ. For the pomegranates, as has often been said, signify the mystery of the passion of Christ. The Church therefore considers, through her prelates, who are in the virtues, or who are already perfect, so that, imitating the passion of Christ, they too may be fit to shed their blood for him — such as were those to whom the Apostle said: « To you it is given, not only that you believe in Christ, but that you suffer for him. »

« I knew not. » It is the voice of the Synagogue. Seeing so great a grace of the Church when the truth of the Gospel is beheld, she grieves that she had not before known the faith of Christ, and, as it were excusing herself that she was converted late, she says: I knew not, O spouse of Christ, the Church. I knew not, that is, I did not 341Arecognize so great a grace and so great gifts of spiritual virtues bestowed upon thee by thy spouse. « My soul troubled me for the chariots of Aminadab. » I have been troubled (she says) with too great anxiety of mind, because of so sudden a preaching of the Gospel. For I knew the law and the prophets to be given by God, and therefore, when I suddenly saw the Gospel preached, I was troubled for the chariots of Aminadab. Aminadab was the great-great-grandson of Judah, through whom the generation of Christ is woven. Aminadab is interpreted "spontaneous of my people," because, although he was God, of his own accord he became man; although he was the founder and creator, of his own goodness alone he became a portion of his people. And the sense is: I have been troubled (she says) because of the sudden preaching of the Gospel, which, like most swift chariots, suddenly flew through the whole 341Bworld. And well does he call this preaching not a chariot but chariots, because the preaching of the Gospel rests on the authority of the four evangelists, and the four Gospels are as it were the four-horse chariots of the New Testament, over which Christ himself presides as charioteer, tempering and disposing the very course of the Gospels.

« Return, return, O Shulamite. » It is the voice of the Church, inviting the Synagogue to the faith: « Return, return, O Shulamite. » Return, O Synagogue, from unbelief to faith, return from hatred to love. Shulamite is interpreted "captive" or "despised." Such was the Synagogue before the coming of Christ, namely captive by the chain of the devil, and despised by God, because it did not recognize Christ, sent for its own salvation. « Return, return, that we may behold thee, » that is, that 341Cwe may look upon the beauty of thy chastity.

CAPUT VII.

« Quid videbis in Sunamite nisi choros castrorum? » Vox est sponsi. Qui audiens invitari Synagogam a sponsa sua Ecclesia ad fidem, dicit: « Quid videbis in Sunamite nisi choros castrorum? » Ac si diceret: Tu quidem o sponsa doles de perditione Synagogae, et quod tarde ad fidem veniat, sed adest tempus quo convertatur, et tunc nihil videbis in ea nisi choros castrorum, id est nisi charitatis concordiam et stabile robur fidei, quo pro defensione meae fidei pugnabit. Nam chori canentium sunt, castra vero militum pugnantium. In Sunamite ergo chori castrorum videbuntur, quia conversa Synagoga et concorditer 341DChristum praedicabit cum Ecclesia, et pro fide Christi usque ad mortem pugnabit. « Quam pulchri sunt gressus tui! » Ab hoc loco incipit sponsus exponere laudes sponsae suae. Et notandum quod a gressibus incipit ejus pulchritudinem texere, et in ore laudes ejus finit, sicut superius ab oculis coeperat. Hoc autem fecit vel ad pulchritudinem carminis variandam, vel etiam quia sic mysteriis aptissimum est. « Quam pulchri (inquit) sunt gressus tui! » Vidit Ecclesiam non otiose in uno loco stantem, sed veloci praedicatione totum mundum peragrantem, ideoque a gressibus coepit eam laudare. Gressus enim Ecclesiae sunt velox praedicatio apostolorum, per quos in omnem terram Evangelium insonuit. « In calceamentis, » id 342Aest in patrum praecedentium exemplis. Calceamenta enim ex mortuis fiunt animalibus. Et calceamenta Ecclesiae sunt Patrum praecedentium exempla, quibus gressus habet munitos, ut securius eum imitando et sine offensione gradiatur. « Filia principis. » Alia translatio, « filia Aminadab » dicit. Aminadab (ut superius dictum est) populi mei spontaneus dicitur, qui recte figuram Christi tenet; nam Ecclesia et filia est et sponsa. Filia, quia ejus sanguine redempta, ejus baptismate regenerata: sponsa, quia dote Spiritus sancti illi est conjuncta. « Juncturae feminum tuorum sicut monilia quae fabricata sunt manu artificis. » In feminibus vel in femoribus solet sacra Scriptura generationis propaginem designare. Unde de Jacob legimus: « Omnes animae quae egressae sunt de femore 342BJacob. » Femina ergo Ecclesiae sunt spiritualis generatio credentis populi per verbum praedicationis et lavacrum regenerationis. Juncturae feminum est conjunctio duorum populorum in una fide, judaici et gentilis. Et bene hae juncturae monilibus comparantur, quia fides utriusque populi maxime in operibus manifestatur. Nam « fides sine operibus mortua est. » Fabricata autem sunt manu artificis, hoc est Dei omnipotentis, cujus munere virtus boni operis credentibus tribuitur. Hic est ille artifex, de quo Paulus dicebat, quando memoriam Abrahae partriarchae faciebat: Exspectabat enim manentem civitatem, cujus conditor et artifex Deus est.

« Umbilicus tuus crater tornatilis, nunquam indigens poculis. » Umbilicus frigidissima pars est corporis, 342Cet ideo per umbilicum fragilitatem nostrae mortalitatis debemus accipere. Crater vero est calix major, ansas hinc inde habens. Umbilicus ergo Ecclesiae, crater est tornatilis, quia quanto magis doctores Ecclesiae suae fragilitatis meminerint, tanto magis verbo praedicationis insistunt, ut eleemosynam verbi Dei praebentes, ipsi misericordiam a Deo consequantur. Et bene crater tornatilis dicitur. Torno enim facilius operamur, per quod significatur facilitas erogandi verbum vel eleemosynam dandi. Non enim morose, nec haesitando eleemosyna danda est, juxta quod Salomon dicit: « Ne dixeris amico tuo: Vade et revertere, cras dabo tibi, cum statim possis dare. » Quod etiam torno fit, non eget exterius adhibita regula vel linea, sed inerrate ex seipso perficitur. 342DUmbilicus ergo Ecclesiae crater est tornatilis, quia quicunque vel praedicat, vel eleemosynam dat, pro sola dilectione et supernae mercedis exspectatione hoc facere debet. Nam quicunque vel praedicat, vel proximis miseretur pro remuneratione temporali vel laude, crater est, sed non tornatilis. Et hic crater nunquam indiget poculis, quia praedicatoribus nunquam deest praedicatio verbi Dei, quia ipse quem praedicant, pocula scientiae et virtutem constantiae illis subministrat. Unde et Psalmista: Dominus dabit verbum evangelizantibus virtute multa.

« Venter tuus sicut acervus tritici vallatus liliis. » Eadem fragilitas quae per umbilicum designata est, ostenditur hoc loco per ventrem, quia venter nulla 343Aossium firmitate premitur. Bene ergo venter Eccle siae acervo tritici comparatur, quia sancti quique quanto magis memores sunt snae fragilitatis, eo studiosius ad proferendos bonorum operum fructus insistunt. Et recte non copiam tritici, sed acervum tritici dicit, ut per hoc significet incrementa virtutum, quae veluti acervus ab inferioribus ad superiora crescendo conscendunt. Nam sicut acervus inferioribus lata amplitudine spatiatur, superius vero quadam brevitate contrahitur et acuminatur, ita in Ecclesia multi sunt qui indulgentius vivunt, pauci vero qui summae perfectioni insistunt. Plures enim inveniuntur qui de bonis suis eleemosynam faciunt, quam qui sua pro Deo relinquunt. Hic acervus liliis vallatus dicitur, quia sancti bona quae faciunt, pro 343Bdesiderio supernae claritatis et candoris agunt. Nam per lilia coelestis illa claritas designatur. Potest etiam per ventrem Ecclesiae divinitus fons baptismatis accipi, quo fideles in novam creaturam regenerantur. Hic ergo venter acervo tritici vallato liliis comparatur, quia regenerati fonte baptismatis, docentur bonis insudare operibus, et ad intuendam supernae claritatis gloriam semper inhiare.

« Duo ubera tua sicut duo hinnul gemelli capreae. » Ubera Ecclesiae, sicut jam superius dictum est, sanctos doctores significant, qui parvulos quosque (hoc est, nuper in Christo regeneratos) lacte apertioris et facilioris doctrinae instruunt. Bene autem duo ubera dicuntur, quia de duobus populis, judaico videlicet et gentili, fideles congregantur, et lacte spiritualis doctrinae 343Caluntur. Haec duo ubera sicut duo hinnuli capreae gemelli esse dicuntur. Duo hinnuli sunt duo Testamenta, quibus eorumdem doctorum omnis praedicatio subsistit, quorum Testamentorum unus est auctor et promulgator Christus, juxta quod Salomon dicit: Verba sapientium quasi stimuli, et quasi clavi in altum defixi, quae data sunt a pastore uno, Christo videlicet. Caprea enim recte Christum significat, quia et intuitu et velocitate caeteris animantibus praeeminet, et mundum est animal ungulam dividens et ruminans. Tales enim sunt membra Christi, doctores videlicet, intuitu scientiae insignes, et ea velocitate terrena deserentes, atque ad coelestia festinantes. Dividens ungulam, id est habens boni et mali discretionem; et ruminans, hoc est sedula 343Dmemoria praecepta Dei meditans.

« Collum tuum sicut turris eburnea. » Doctores sancti, qui designantur per ubera, ipsi designantur et per collum. Nam per arterias colli vox egreditur, et colli ministerio caeteris membris vitalia alimenta ministrantur. Sic sancti doctores et verba vitae populis annuntiant, et alimento doctrinae coelestis eos nutriunt. Collum ergo Ecclesiae turri eburneae comparatur, quia sancti doctores ejusdem Ecclesiae et pulchritudinem et robur praestant. Pulchritudinem quidem, quae per candorem eboris designatur, caste et sincere vivendo. Robur vero praestant, quod significatur per turrim, quando eamdem Ecclesiam contra tentationes diaboli vel impetus haereticorum 344Amuniunt. « Oculi tui sicut piscinae in Hesebon, quae sunt in porta filiae multitudinis. » Id est, ipsi doctores, qui per collum designati sunt, designantur et per oculos. Ipsi enim oculi universo corpori Ecclesiae provident, et iter quod ingredi debeant, ostendunt. Hi oculi Ecclesiae comparantur piscinis Hesebon in porta civitatis, quia sancti doctores populos in Christum credentes vitali lavacro abluunt, et salutaris doctrinae potu reficiunt. Filia autem multitudinis vocatur Hesebon juxta litteram, ob multitudinem confluentium inibi populorum. Sic et sancta Ecclesia recte dicitur filia multitudinis, quia numerositatem quotidie colligit gentium. Hesebon autem interpretatur cingulum moeroris. Oculi ergo Ecclesiae piscinis Hesebon comparantur, quia sancti doctores, 344Bneglectis caducis saeculi gaudiis, cingulo moeroris se constringunt, quia caste et sobrie viventes in hoc saeculo, et pro suis erratibus deflent, quatenus in praesenti lugentes, ad vera aeternaque possint pervenire gaudia. In porta autem Hesebon hae piscinae esse dicuntur, quia nullus Ecclesiam ingredi valet, nisi prius aqua baptismatis abluatur, et nisi fonte salutaris doctrinae potetur. Hoc etiam significabat labrum aeneum, quod Salomon in porticu templi posuerat, ut sacerdotes ingressuri templum, et hostias immolaturi, ibi corpora abluerent.

« Nasus tuus sicut turris Libani, quae respicit contra Damascum. » Quia naso fetores et bonos odores discernimus, recte per nasum Ecclesiae iidem sancti doctores intelliguntur. Ipsi enim sagaciter 344Cdiscernere noverunt inter fragilitatem catholicae fidei doctrinam et letiferum haeretici corporis fetorem. Nam et in divinis charismatibus, quae per Spiritum sanctum distribuuntur, dicitur: Alii datur per Spiritum discretio spirituum. Nasus ergo Ecclesiae turri Libani comparatur, quia sancti doctores et summum locum in Ecclesia tenent, ac veluti in Libano monte consistunt, et defendunt Ecclesiam a malignorum spirituum incursibus. Haec turris contra Damascum esse dicitur. Damascus metropolis civitas est Syriae, quae quondam contra filios Israel, utpote fortissimos et crudelissimos reges habens, dimicabat. Interpretatur autem Damascus, sanguinis potus, vel sanguinis oculus. Ferunt ibi Abel interfectum, ideoque significat potestates hujus saeculi, quae sanguinis potum 344Dsitiunt, quia voluptatibus et illecebris carnis et sanguinis delectantur. Significat etiam aerias potestates, quae animarum nostrarum cruorem sitiunt. Contra Damascum ergo est haec turris, quia haec Ecclesia semper diabolo et membris illius, adjutorio Christi munita, resistit.

« Caput tuum ut Carmelus. » Caput ergo Ecclesiae principalitas mentis fidelium intelligitur: nam sicut capite membra reguntur caetera, ita mente cogitationes disponuntur. Bene autem caput Ecclesiae Carmelo comparatur. Carmelus enim, qui et Carmel dicitur, interpretatur cognitio vel scientia circumcisionis. Ergo caput Ecclesiae Carmelo assimilatur, quia novit circumcisionem aliquando corporaliter 345Acelebratam, nunc se spiritualiter observare debere, juxta quod Propheta dicit: « Circumcidimini Domino in cordibus vestris. » Hanc sententiam circumcisionis non habuerunt Judaei, ideoque reprehenduntur a beato Stephano protomartyre dicente: « Dura cervice et incircumcisis cordibus et auribus, semper Spiritui sancto restitistis. » In libro quoque Regum legitur quod Elias in montem Carmeli ascenderit, et ibi orando aridam terram coelestibus pluviis irrigaverit, post longam trium annorum et semis siccitatem. Quae historia pulchram redolet allegoriam. Nam Elias, qui interpretatur Deus meus Dominus, Christum significat. Qui cum sit Deus et Dominus omnium et totius creaturae, specialiter suus, id est proprius, est Deus et Dominus Ecclesiae. Hic in Carmelum ascendit, 345Bquando per gratiam suam mentes fidelium suorum conscendit, et per eos arenti mundo gratiarum suarum pluvias tribuit. Recte ergo (ut dictum est) caput Ecclesiae Carmelo monti comparatur, quia mens sanctorum conversatione sublimis est, et Christo in se ascensum praebet per profectum virtutum.

« Et comae capitis tui ut purpura regis vincta canalibus. » Si per caput Ecclesiae mens accipitur, per comas capitis cogitationes mentis debemus accipere, quae assidue de illa prodeunt. Purpureus vero color, qui sanguinis habet speciem, mysterium Dominicae passionis significat. Juxta litteram quod dicit, « purpura regis vincta canalibus, » significat lanam jam purpureo colore confectam et necdum 345Cin fila deductam, sed adhuc in canalibus constitutam, quas Graeci βάτια vocant, in quibus sanguis conchyliorum solet defluere, et lanam in purpureum vertere colorem. Mystice canales istae humilitatem sanctorum significant, in quibus aeterni regis purpura tingitur, cum sancti quique humilitatem sui Redemptoris imitando, passionum illius conformes fieri satagunt, quatenus in purpurae dignitatem vertantur; id est, laborantes pro Christo, mereantur regnare cum Christo. Vincta autem haec purpura esse dicitur, quia sanctorum praecordia fixa sunt et stabilita in timore et amore Redemptoris sui, nec unquam a charitate ejus separari possunt.

« Quam pulchra es et quam decora, charissima, in deliciis. » Quam pulchra es, scilicet in fide, et 345Ddecora in operatione, charissima in deliciis. Supra dictum est de Ecclesia: « Pulchra ut luna, electa ut sol, terribilis ut castrorum acies ordinata; » hic vero dicitur, « Pulchra et decora, charissima in deliciis; » quae quasi contraria esse videntur. Nam ordinatam esse velut castrorum aciem, et esse in deliciis, repugnat, quia deliciae fortitudinem militum solent enervare. Sed sancta Ecclesia utrumque agit. Ordinata est velut castrorum acies, quia infidelibus haereticis resistit, et malignos spiritus sua sanctitate et perfectione terret. In deliciis etiam est, quia suavitatem supernae dulcedinis assidue palato mentis degustat, et inter pressuras hujus saeculi illas adipisci desiderat et continue illis satiari. Unde merito 346Ailla quae talis est, sponso suo Christo charissima esse dicitur.

« Statura tua assimilata est palmae. » Statura Ecclesiae est perfecta ejus rectitudo. Neque enim ad vitia incurvatur, sed recta et immobilis persistit. Unde Psalmista exspectantium voce dicebat: « Incurvatus sum et humiliatus usquequaque. » Et propheta sub specie Hierusalem peccanti animae de malignis spiritibus dicit: « Quia humiliaverunt te, et dixerunt animae tuae, Incurvare ut transeamus. » At contra sancta Ecclesia recta stat, nec unquam incurvari consentit, audiens ab Apostolo: « Vigilate, state in fide, viriliter agite et confortamini, et omnia vestra in charitate fiant. » Et Dominus filiis Israel loquitur: « Ego sum Dominus Deus vester, qui eripui vos de 346Bergastulo Aegyptiorum, ut incederetis recte. » Statura ergo Ecclesiae palmae assimilatur, quae in omnibus quae agit semper in memoria supernam retinet remunerationem. Palma enim victricem ornat manum, et ideo significat praemium illud quod victoribus sanctis in coelestibus est dandum. « Et ubera tua botris. » Ubera Ecclesiae (ut superius dictum est) sunt doctores sancti, qui lacte simplicis doctrinae regeneratos in Christo nutriunt. Sed haec ubera botris assimilantur, quando iidem doctores perfectis perfectiora denuntiant, sicut Apostolus loquens simplicioribus, dicebat: « Non potui vobis loqui tanquam spiritualibus, sed tanquam carnalibus; tanquam parvulis in Christo lac potum vobis dedi, non escam. » Ecce ubera lactis plena. Quae ubera 346Cin botros sunt versa, cum diceret: « Nos praedicamus Christum Dei virtutem et Dei sapientiam, et loquimur sapientiam inter perfectos. »

« Dixi, ascendam in palmam, apprehendam fructus ejus. » Ut ostenderetur omnis haec excellentia et magnitudo donorum Ecclesiae de Dei gratia venire, introducitur idem sponsus dicens: « Ascendam in palmam. » Quasi diceret: Inde habes ubera ad nutriendos parvulos, unde habes botros, quibus perfectioribus perfectionem propines doctrinam, quia ego mea gratia in te ascendi, et hanc tibi perfectionem concessi. « Ascendam (inquit) in palmam. » Palma Ecclesiam significat, vel etiam animam cujuscunque fidelis, qui memor est Domini Dei sui qui dicit: Confidite, ego vici mundum. Ipsa quoque omnium 346Dvitiorum et iniquitatis victrix esse studet. Bene autem in palmam hic ascendisse dicitur, cum superius in hortum descendisse legatur. Dixit enim Ecclesia: « Dilectus meus descendit in hortum suum. » Idem est hortus quod et palma, videlicet una eademque Ecclesia. Sed in eam Dominus descendit, et ad eam ascendit. Descendit in eam, copiam suae gratiae illi de coelestibus mittendo; ascendit ad illam, dona sua in ea incrementando, eamque quibusdam profectibus veluti quibusdam gradibus ad celsiora sublevando. Sed et hoc de natura palmae est sciendum, quod in inferioribus aspera est, in superiobus pulcherrima decore foliorum et pulchritudine fructuum. Sic et sancta Ecclesia in inferioribus 347Aasperitatem laborum et passionum sustinet pro Christo: in superioribus vero, hoc est in coelestibus, pulchritudinem et suavitatem exspectat praemiorum. Potest et per palmam arbor Dominicae crucis exprimi, in quam Redemptor noster pro humani generis redemptione ascendit, et in qua hostem humani generis superavit. Ait ergo: « Dixi, ascendam in palmam; » id est, per meos prophetas locutus sum, et promisi me ad passionem venturum, et meam Ecclesiam redempturum. « Apprehendam (inquit) fructus ejus; » id est fructus bonorum operum et laborum, quibus Ecclesia desudat remunerando; vel etiam secundum meum sensum, quo per palmam crux intelligitur. « Apprehendam fructus, » id est, impleto triumpho passionis, veniam ad gloriam resurrectionis, 347Bet apostolis meis spiritualia dona concedam. « Et erunt ubera tua sicut botri vineae. » Hoc superius jam expositum est: nam ubera Ecclesiae doctores sunt, cum parvulos simplici doctrina nutriunt; sed botri vineae efficiuntur, cum perfectioribus sublimia quaeque annuntiant. Nam beatus Paulus uno codemque tempore ubera et botros habuit, quando de Christo loquens dixit: « Quorum patres, et ex quibus Christus. » Ecce ubera, id est simplex de Christo praedicatio. Sed haec ubera in suavissimum et fortissimum vinum conversa sunt, cum subjunxit: Qui est Deus benedictus in saecula.

Et odor oris tui sicut malorum. Guttur tuum sicut vinum optimum. » Os et odor Ecclesiae, est praesens ejus praedicatio, quae fama vel scripto ad 347Cabsentes ejus pervenit. Guttur vero est vox ipsa praedicatoris, quae in praesenti auditur. Odor ergo oris Ecclesiae odoribus malorum comparatur, quia ipsa fama praedicationis plena est suavitatis et gratiae. Guttur vero vino optimo assimilatur, quia ipsa vox praedicantium magnam in se virtutem auditoribus ostendebat. Quod vero malorum odor in novitate est, vini vero in vetustate, significat praedicationem Ecclesiae et in sui novitate, et etiam in sui perfectione omnem habere suavitatem et gratiam. « Dignum dilecto meo ad potandum, labiisque et dentibus illius ruminandum. » Vox Ecclesiae est. Quae audiens se laudari a sponso, gratulabunda respondet: « Dignum dilecto meo ad potandum. » Ac si diceret: Sponsus meus guttur meum vino optimo 347Dcomparavit, et illud vinum dignum est ad potandum ipso dilecto meo, hoc est praedicatio evangelica, quam in ore meo posuit, per ipsum, et non per alium annuntiari debet in mundo. Neque enim per alium decebat mysteria regni coelestis praedicari in mundo, quam per mediatorem Dei et hominum. « Et labiis (inquit) et dentibus illius ruminandum. » Labia et dentes sponsi, ut supra dictum est, doctores sancti sunt. Illud ergo vinum optimum ruminandum est labiis et dentibus sponsi, quia praedicatio evangelica a doctoribus tractanda et exponenda est. Nec absurdum est, quod cum vinum potetur, et non ruminetur, hic tamen dicitur labiis et dentibus ruminandum, quod magis ad cibum 348Aquam ad potum pertinet. Nam sancti doctores vinum spirituale ruminant, quando praecepta evangelica diligenter exquirunt, et crebra meditatione investigant atque invicem conferunt.

« Ego dilecto meo, et ad me conversio ejus. » « Ego dilecto meo, » subaudis obsequium justitiae et devotionis impendo: illi, et non alteri fidem et servitutem meam promitto. « Et ad me conversio ejus, » ut me in praesenti inter labores et aerumnas hujus vitae tueatur, et in futuro ad gaudia vitae coelestis inducat. Potest etiam haec esse vox Synagogae ex persona sanctorum Patrum, adventum Christi desiderantium. Ac si diceret: Scio (inquit) illum spirituali praesentia mihi semper adesse, et ideo ego dilecto meo omni devotione famulari cupio, sed 348Btandem « ad me conversio ejus, » quia opto ut tandem, sicut promisit, in mea substantia mihi appareat. Nam dixerat supra sponsus: « Ascendam in palmam; » id est, promiserat se in carne venturum, et pro humano genere passurum, et inde gratulabunda dicebat, « Ego dilecto meo, et ad me fit conversio ejus; » id est, credo illum hominem venturum, et in mea substantia hominem mundo manifestandum.

« Veni, dilecte mi, egrediamur in agrum, commoremur in villis. » Ejusdem Synagogae vox est, « Veni, dilecte mi, » id est, appare per assumptum hominem. Tale est et illud Psalmistae, « Excita potentiam tuam et veni; » et Isaiae, « Utinam dirumperes coelos, et venires. » « Egrediamur in agrum. » 348CAger, mundus iste est. « Egrediamur (inquit) in agrum, » id est, praedicemus mundo. « Commoremur in viliis, » hoc est, ipsis quoque paganis fidem tuam annuntiemus. Nemo nescit paganos a villa dictos, quia πάγος Graece, villa dicitur Latine. Inde pagani dicti, quia longe sunt a civitate Dei. Potest et vox Ecclesiae esse, « Veni, dilecte mi. » Ac si diceret: Jam mysterio ascensionis assumptum hominem ad coelos sublevasti, sed veni, divinae majestatis praesentia me saepius visitando. « Egrediamur in agrum. » Eodem sensu quo et superius, Praedicemus tuae incarnationis fidem mundo. « Commoremur in villis; » id est, etiam ignorantibus te paganis tuam fidem annuntiemus, nec in transitu, sed commoremur ibi, donec illos ex paganis catholicos, ex alienis tibi proprios 348Dfaciamus.

« Mane surgamus ad vineas. » Mane exortus est novae gratiae, quando videlicet, transeuntibus tenebris infidelitatis, Evangelium mundo coepit praedicari. « Videamus si floruit vinea. » Hoc est, inspiciamus Ecclesiam, si jam prima fidei rudimenta suscepit. « Si flores fructus parturiunt. » Inspiciamus etiam si flores parturiunt fructum, id est, si illi qui jam in Christum crediderunt, idonei sunt ad bene operandum. Nam flores emittit Ecclesia, cum primum fideles ad fidem veniunt. Flores vero fructus parturiunt, cum jam ipsa fide et dilectione bene operatur, ne ipsa fides otiosa sit, vel mortua sine operibus; nam, ut Jacobus dicit, « fides sine operibus mortua est. » 349A« Si floruerunt mala punica. » Mala punica (ut supra dictum est) rubicundum vel sanguinem habent corticem, et ideo significant passionem Domini, vel membrorum ipsius. Dicit ergo, « Si floruerunt mala punica, » hoc est, si sancti qui jam fide et opere perfecti sunt, idonei etiam sint imitari passionem Christi et pati pro illo. Mala punica erant illi quibus Apostolus dicebat: « Vobis datum est non solum nosse, verum etiam ut pro illo patiamini. » « Ibi dabo tibi ubera mea. » Ubera Ecclesiae doctores sunt, qui fideles primum ad fidem erudiunt. Cum (inquit) ad vineas exierimus, ibi dabo tibi, o sponse, ubera mea, hoc est tibi filios generabo. Unde adhuc subditur:

« Mandragorae dederunt odorem in portis nostris. » 349BMandragora herba est aromatica, cujus radix similitudinem habet humani corporis. Poma ejus optimi sunt odoris, in similitudinem pomi Matiani, quod nostri terrae malum vocant. Haec herba rebus medicinalibus aptissima est. Nam ferunt eos qui incommodo vigiliarum laborant, haustu hujus pomi relevari, et posse dormire. Item ferunt eos qui ob curam secandi sunt, si exteriorem hujus pomi corticem biberint, non sentire sectionem vel adustionem. Quae omnia si quis mystice disquirere voluerit, spirituales sensus et Ecclesiae convenientes ibi inveniet. Dicit ergo, « Mandragorae dederunt odorem in portis nostris. » Portae Ecclesiae sunt apostoli et eorum successores, quia nemo civitatem Dei (id est Ecclesiam) intrat, nisi per sanctos doctores aqua baptismatis 349Cregeneratus et vitali doctrina instructus fuerit. De his portis Psalmista dicebat: « Diligit Dominus portas Sion super omnia tabernacula Jacob. » Mandragorae autem fragrantiam virtutum designant. In portis ergo Ecclesiae mandragorae odorem dederunt, quando apostoli vel successores eorum famam suavissimae opinionis et odorem virtutum longe lateque sparserunt. Ideoque invitat sponsum suum ut veniat, quia jam nomen ejus longe lateque per apostolos annuntiatur, et odor praedicationis Christi longe lateque dispergitur. « Omnia poma nova et vetera, dilecte mi, servari tibi. » Id est, praecepta vel promissa Veteris Testamenti et Novi servari tibi, o dilecte mi. Sciebat enim omnia quae in Veteri Testamento annuntiata sunt, non nisi per Christum et in 349DChristo posse compleri. Ideoque dicebat, Tibi servavi poma nova et vetera, hoc est, te exspecto, ut per te impleantur in Novo Testamento quae praedicta sunt in Veteri.
