Unde et in passione sua jam pendens in cruce, cum gustasset acetum, dixit: « Consummatum est. » Et post resurrectionem apostolis: Necesse est impleri omnia quae scripta sunt in lege et prophetis et psalmis de me. Possunt etiam poma nova 350Aet vetera justorum personae intelligi, qui vel in Veteri vel in Novo Testamento fuerunt. Dicit ergo Ecclesia, Servari tibi nova et vetera, id est, justos qui in Veteri Testamento praecesserunt, vel in Novo subsecuti sunt, tibi servavi, ut per te laborum suorum praemia perciperent. Nullus enim justorum regnum coelorum intrare valuit, nisi per Christum; quia ipse primus ascendit, et sequentibus suis membris coelum aperuit.

CHAPTER VII.

« What shalt thou see in the Shulamite but the companies of camps? » It is the voice of the spouse. Who, hearing the Synagogue invited to the faith by his spouse the Church, says: « What shalt thou see in the Shulamite but the companies of camps? » As if he said: Thou indeed, O spouse, grievest over the perdition of the Synagogue, and because it comes late to the faith; but the time is at hand when it shall be converted, and then thou shalt see in it nothing but the companies of camps, that is, nothing but the concord of charity and the steadfast strength of faith, by which it will fight for the defense of my faith. For choirs are of those who sing, but camps of soldiers who fight. In the Shulamite, therefore, the companies of camps shall be seen, because the Synagogue, once converted, will both harmoniously 341Dpreach Christ with the Church, and will fight for the faith of Christ even unto death. « How beautiful are thy steps! » From this place the spouse begins to set forth the praises of his spouse. And it is to be noted that he begins from the steps to weave together her beauty, and ends her praises at the mouth, just as above he had begun from the eyes. And this he did either to vary the beauty of the song, or also because it is thus most fitting for the mysteries. « How beautiful (he says) are thy steps! » He saw the Church not standing idly in one place, but wandering through the whole world with swift preaching, and therefore he began to praise her from the steps. For the steps of the Church are the swift preaching of the apostles, through whom the Gospel resounded in all the earth. « In sandals, » that 342Ais, in the examples of the fathers who went before. For sandals are made from dead animals. And the sandals of the Church are the examples of the fathers who went before, by which she has her steps fortified, so that she may walk the more safely by imitating him, and without stumbling. « O daughter of the prince. » Another translation says, « daughter of Aminadab. » Aminadab (as was said above) is called "spontaneous of my people," who rightly holds the figure of Christ; for the Church is both daughter and spouse. Daughter, because redeemed by his blood, regenerated by his baptism; spouse, because she is joined to him by the endowment of the Holy Spirit. « The joints of thy thighs are like jewels that are wrought by the hand of a craftsman. » By the thighs or by the loins Holy Scripture is accustomed to designate the offspring of generation. Whence of Jacob we read: « All the souls that came out of the thigh of 342BJacob. » The thighs of the Church, therefore, are the spiritual generation of the believing people through the word of preaching and the laver of regeneration. The joints of the thighs are the joining of the two peoples in one faith, the Jewish and the Gentile. And well are these joints compared to jewels, because the faith of each people is manifested chiefly in works. For « faith without works is dead. » And they are wrought by the hand of a craftsman, that is, of God almighty, by whose gift the power of good work is granted to believers. This is that craftsman of whom Paul spoke, when he made mention of the patriarch Abraham: For he looked for a city that hath abiding foundations, whose builder and maker is God.

« Thy navel is like a turned bowl, never lacking cups. » The navel is the coldest part of the body, 342Cand therefore by the navel we ought to understand the frailty of our mortality. But a bowl is a larger cup, having handles on either side. The navel of the Church, therefore, is a turned bowl, because the more the teachers of the Church remember their own frailty, the more they persist in the word of preaching, so that, offering the alms of the word of God, they may themselves obtain mercy from God. And well is it called a turned bowl. For with the lathe we work more easily, by which is signified the ease of dispensing the word or of giving alms. For alms is to be given neither peevishly nor with hesitation, according to what Solomon says: « Say not to thy friend: Go, and come again, tomorrow I will give to thee, when thou canst give at once. » And what is made with the lathe needs no rule or line applied from outside, but is completed unerringly of itself. 342DThe navel of the Church, therefore, is a turned bowl, because whoever either preaches or gives alms ought to do this for love alone and for the expectation of the heavenly reward. For whoever either preaches or shows mercy to his neighbors for temporal recompense or praise is a bowl, but not a turned one. And this bowl never lacks cups, because to preachers the preaching of the word of God is never wanting, since he himself whom they preach supplies to them the cups of knowledge and the strength of constancy. Whence also the Psalmist: The Lord shall give the word to them that preach the gospel with great power.

« Thy belly is like a heap of wheat, set about with lilies. » The same frailty which was designated by the navel is shown in this place by the belly, because the belly is pressed by no firmness 343Aof bones. Well, therefore, is the belly of the Church compared to a heap of wheat, because all the saints, the more they are mindful of their own frailty, the more zealously they persist in bringing forth the fruits of good works. And rightly he says not an abundance of wheat, but a heap of wheat, that by this he may signify the increases of the virtues, which, like a heap, climb by growing from the lower parts to the higher. For just as a heap spreads out below with broad breadth, but above is drawn in with a certain narrowness and comes to a point, so in the Church there are many who live more indulgently, but few who press on toward the highest perfection. For more are found who give alms of their goods than who leave their goods for God's sake. This heap is said to be set about with lilies, because the saints do the good that they do for 343Bthe desire of the heavenly brightness and whiteness. For by the lilies that heavenly brightness is designated. By the belly of the Church can also be understood the divinely given font of baptism, by which the faithful are regenerated into a new creature. This belly, therefore, is compared to a heap of wheat set about with lilies, because those regenerated by the font of baptism are taught to sweat over good works, and always to yearn after gazing upon the glory of the heavenly brightness.

« Thy two breasts are like two young twin fawns of a roe. » The breasts of the Church, as has already been said above, signify the holy teachers, who instruct the little ones (that is, those newly regenerated in Christ) with the milk of a more open and easier doctrine. And well are they called two breasts, because from two peoples, namely the Jewish and the Gentile, the faithful are gathered together, and are nourished with the milk of spiritual doctrine. 343CThese two breasts are said to be like two twin fawns of a roe. The two fawns are the two Testaments, on which all the preaching of these same teachers rests, of which Testaments the one author and promulgator is Christ, according to what Solomon says: The words of the wise are as goads, and as nails deeply fastened in, which are given by one shepherd, namely Christ. For the roe rightly signifies Christ, because both in sight and in swiftness he surpasses the other animals, and it is a clean animal, dividing the hoof and chewing the cud. For such are the members of Christ, namely the teachers, distinguished by the sight of knowledge, and with that swiftness forsaking earthly things and hastening toward the heavenly. Dividing the hoof, that is, having the discernment of good and evil; and chewing the cud, that is, with diligent 343Dmemory meditating upon the precepts of God.

« Thy neck is like a tower of ivory. » The holy teachers, who are designated by the breasts, are themselves designated also by the neck. For through the arteries of the neck the voice goes forth, and by the ministry of the neck the vital nourishments are supplied to the other members. So the holy teachers both announce the words of life to the peoples, and nourish them with the food of heavenly doctrine. The neck of the Church, therefore, is compared to a tower of ivory, because the holy teachers furnish to this same Church both beauty and strength. Beauty indeed, which is designated by the whiteness of ivory, by living chastely and sincerely. But they furnish strength, which is signified by the tower, when they fortify this same Church against the temptations of the devil or the assaults of the heretics. 344A« Thy eyes are like the fishpools in Heshbon, which are in the gate of the daughter of the multitude. » That is, the teachers themselves, who were designated by the neck, are designated also by the eyes. For the eyes themselves provide for the whole body of the Church, and show the way which it ought to enter upon. These eyes of the Church are compared to the fishpools of Heshbon in the gate of the city, because the holy teachers wash the peoples who believe in Christ with the vital laver, and refresh them with the draught of saving doctrine. And Heshbon, according to the letter, is called the daughter of the multitude, because of the multitude of peoples flowing together therein. So also the holy Church is rightly called the daughter of the multitude, because daily she gathers a great number of the nations. And Heshbon is interpreted "the girdle of sorrow." The eyes of the Church, therefore, are compared to the fishpools of Heshbon, because the holy teachers, 344Bneglecting the fleeting joys of the world, gird themselves with the girdle of sorrow, because, living chastely and soberly in this world, they also weep for their errors, so that, mourning in the present, they may be able to come to the true and eternal joys. And these fishpools are said to be in the gate of Heshbon, because no one can enter the Church unless he first be washed with the water of baptism, and unless he be given to drink of the font of saving doctrine. This also that bronze laver signified, which Solomon had placed in the porch of the temple, so that the priests, about to enter the temple and to offer the victims, might there wash their bodies.

« Thy nose is like the tower of Lebanon, that looketh toward Damascus. » Because by the nose we discern stenches and good odors, rightly by the nose of the Church these same holy teachers are understood. For they themselves have known how sagaciously 344Cto discern between the doctrine of the frailty of the Catholic faith and the death-bearing stench of the heretical body. For also among the divine graces, which are distributed through the Holy Spirit, it is said: To another is given by the Spirit the discerning of spirits. The nose of the Church, therefore, is compared to the tower of Lebanon, because the holy teachers both hold the highest place in the Church, and, as it were, stand upon Mount Lebanon, and defend the Church from the assaults of the malignant spirits. This tower is said to be toward Damascus. Damascus is the metropolitan city of Syria, which once fought against the sons of Israel, having, as it were, most mighty and most cruel kings. And Damascus is interpreted "the drink of blood," or "the eye of blood." They say that Abel was slain there, and therefore it signifies the powers of this world, which thirst for the drink of blood, 344Dbecause they delight in the pleasures and enticements of flesh and blood. It signifies also the airy powers, which thirst for the gore of our souls. This tower, therefore, is toward Damascus, because this Church, fortified by the help of Christ, ever resists the devil and his members.

« Thy head is as Carmel. » The head of the Church, then, is understood as the ruling part of the mind of the faithful; for just as by the head the other members are ruled, so by the mind the thoughts are ordered. And well is the head of the Church compared to Carmel. For Carmel, which is also called Carmel, is interpreted "the knowledge or science of circumcision." Therefore the head of the Church is likened to Carmel, because it has known that circumcision, once bodily 345Acelebrated, must now be spiritually observed by it, according to what the Prophet says: « Be circumcised to the Lord in your hearts. » This understanding of circumcision the Jews did not have, and therefore they are rebuked by the blessed Stephen, the protomartyr, saying: « You stiff-necked and uncircumcised in heart and ears, you have always resisted the Holy Spirit. » In the book of Kings also it is read that Elijah ascended into Mount Carmel, and there by praying watered the dry earth with heavenly rains, after the long drought of three years and a half. This history breathes a beautiful allegory. For Elijah, who is interpreted "my God the Lord," signifies Christ. Who, although he is God and Lord of all and of the whole creation, is in a special way his own, that is, the proper, God and Lord of the Church. This one ascends into Carmel, 345Bwhen by his grace he mounts up into the minds of his faithful, and through them bestows upon the parched world the rains of his graces. Rightly, therefore (as was said), the head of the Church is compared to Mount Carmel, because the mind of the saints is sublime in its manner of life, and offers to Christ an ascent into itself through the advance of the virtues.

« And the hairs of thy head are like the king's purple bound in the channels. » If by the head of the Church the mind is understood, by the hairs of the head we ought to understand the thoughts of the mind, which continually issue from it. But the purple color, which has the appearance of blood, signifies the mystery of the Lord's passion. According to the letter, when he says « the king's purple bound in the channels, » it signifies wool already dyed with the purple color and not yet 345Cdrawn out into threads, but still set in the channels, which the Greeks call batia, in which the blood of the shellfish is accustomed to flow down and to turn the wool into a purple color. Mystically these channels signify the humility of the saints, in which the purple of the eternal king is dyed, when all the saints, by imitating the humility of their Redeemer, strive to become conformed to his sufferings, so that they may be turned into the dignity of purple; that is, laboring for Christ, they may deserve to reign with Christ. And this purple is said to be bound, because the hearts of the saints are fixed and made steadfast in the fear and love of their Redeemer, nor can they ever be separated from his charity.

« How beautiful art thou, and how comely, dearest, in delights. » How beautiful art thou, namely in faith, and 345Dcomely in operation, dearest in delights. Above it was said of the Church: « Fair as the moon, chosen as the sun, terrible as an army set in array; » but here it is said, « Beautiful and comely, dearest in delights; » which seem to be as it were contrary. For to be set in array like an army, and to be in delights, is repugnant, because delights are accustomed to enervate the strength of soldiers. But the holy Church does both. She is set in array like an army, because she resists the unbelieving heretics, and by her holiness and perfection terrifies the malignant spirits. She is also in delights, because she continually tastes the sweetness of the heavenly sweetness with the palate of the mind, and amid the pressures of this world desires to attain those delights and continually to be satisfied with them. Whence deservedly 346Ashe who is such is said to be dearest to her spouse Christ.

« Thy stature is likened to a palm tree. » The stature of the Church is her perfect uprightness. For she is not bent toward vices, but stands upright and unmoved. Whence the Psalmist said, in the voice of those who wait: « I am bowed down and humbled exceedingly. » And the prophet, under the figure of Jerusalem, says to the sinning soul concerning the malignant spirits: « Because they have humbled thee, and have said to thy soul: Bow down, that we may pass over. » But on the contrary the holy Church stands upright, and never consents to be bent, hearing from the Apostle: « Watch ye, stand fast in the faith, do manfully and be strengthened, and let all your things be done in charity. » And the Lord speaks to the sons of Israel: « I am the Lord your God, who brought you out of 346Bthe prison-house of the Egyptians, that you might walk upright. » The stature of the Church, therefore, is likened to a palm tree, which in all that it does ever keeps in memory the heavenly recompense. For the palm adorns the victorious hand, and therefore signifies that reward which is to be given to the victorious saints in the heavens. « And thy breasts to clusters of grapes. » The breasts of the Church (as was said above) are the holy teachers, who nourish those regenerated in Christ with the milk of simple doctrine. But these breasts are likened to clusters, when these same teachers announce more perfect things to the perfect, just as the Apostle, speaking to the simpler, said: « I could not speak to you as unto spiritual, but as unto carnal; as unto little ones in Christ I gave you milk to drink, not meat. » Behold breasts full of milk. Which breasts 346Cwere turned into clusters, when he said: « We preach Christ the power of God and the wisdom of God, and we speak wisdom among the perfect. »

« I said, I will go up into the palm tree, and will take hold of the fruit thereof. » That it might be shown that all this excellence and greatness of the gifts of the Church comes from the grace of God, the same spouse is introduced saying: « I will go up into the palm tree. » As if he said: Thence hast thou breasts to nourish the little ones, whence thou hast clusters, by which thou mayest offer to the more perfect the more perfect doctrine, because I by my grace have gone up into thee, and have granted thee this perfection. « I will go up (he says) into the palm tree. » The palm signifies the Church, or also the soul of any of the faithful, who is mindful of the Lord his God who says: Have confidence, I have overcome the world. She too strives to be the victor over all 346Dvices and iniquity. And well is he said here to have gone up into the palm, when above he is read to have gone down into the garden. For the Church said: « My beloved is gone down into his garden. » The garden is the same as the palm, namely one and the same Church. But into her the Lord goes down, and to her he goes up. He goes down into her, sending her an abundance of his grace from the heavens; he goes up to her, increasing his gifts in her, and raising her up by certain advances, as by certain steps, to higher things. But this too concerning the nature of the palm is to be known, that in the lower parts it is rough, in the higher parts most beautiful with the grace of leaves and the beauty of fruits. So also the holy Church in the lower parts 347Aendures the roughness of labors and sufferings for Christ; but in the higher parts, that is, in the heavens, she awaits the beauty and sweetness of rewards. By the palm also can be expressed the tree of the Lord's cross, into which our Redeemer ascended for the redemption of the human race, and on which he overcame the enemy of the human race. He says, therefore: « I said, I will go up into the palm; » that is, through my prophets I have spoken, and have promised that I will come to the passion, and will redeem my Church. « I will take hold (he says) of the fruit thereof; » that is, of the fruit of the good works and labors, in which the Church sweats, by recompensing them; or also according to my meaning, by which the cross is understood by the palm. « I will take hold of the fruit, » that is, the triumph of the passion accomplished, I will come to the glory of the resurrection, 347Band will grant spiritual gifts to my apostles. « And thy breasts shall be like the clusters of the vine. » This has already been expounded above: for the breasts of the Church are the teachers, when they nourish the little ones with simple doctrine; but they become clusters of the vine, when they announce sublime things to the more perfect. For the blessed Paul at one and the same time had breasts and clusters, when, speaking of Christ, he said: « Whose fathers, and of whom is Christ. » Behold breasts, that is, the simple preaching concerning Christ. But these breasts were turned into most sweet and most strong wine, when he added: Who is God blessed forever.

And the odor of thy mouth like that of apples. Thy throat like the best wine. » The mouth and the odor of the Church are her present preaching, which by report or by writing reaches 347Cto those absent from her. But the throat is the very voice of the preacher, which is heard in the present. The odor, therefore, of the mouth of the Church is compared to the odors of apples, because the very report of her preaching is full of sweetness and grace. But the throat is likened to the best wine, because the very voice of those preaching showed in itself great power to the hearers. And that the odor of apples is in newness, but that of wine in age, signifies that the preaching of the Church has all sweetness and grace both in its newness and also in its perfection. « Worthy for my beloved to drink, and to be chewed by his lips and teeth. » It is the voice of the Church. Who, hearing herself praised by the spouse, joyfully answers: « Worthy for my beloved to drink. » As if she said: My spouse has compared my throat to the best wine, 347Dand that wine is worthy to be drunk by my beloved himself, that is, the evangelical preaching which he has placed in my mouth is to be announced in the world through him, and not through another. For it was not fitting that the mysteries of the kingdom of heaven be preached in the world through any other than through the mediator of God and men. « And to be chewed (he says) by his lips and teeth. » The lips and teeth of the spouse, as was said above, are the holy teachers. That best wine, therefore, is to be chewed by the lips and teeth of the spouse, because the evangelical preaching is to be handled and expounded by the teachers. Nor is it absurd that, though wine be drunk and not chewed, here nevertheless it is said to be chewed by the lips and teeth, which belongs rather to food 348Athan to drink. For the holy teachers chew the spiritual wine, when they diligently search out the evangelical precepts, and investigate them by frequent meditation and confer them one with another.

« I am my beloved's, and his turning is toward me. » « I am my beloved's, » understand: I render the obedience of justice and devotion: to him, and to no other, I promise my faith and my service. « And his turning is toward me, » that he may protect me in the present amid the labors and troubles of this life, and in the future lead me to the joys of the heavenly life. This can also be the voice of the Synagogue, in the person of the holy Fathers, desiring the coming of Christ. As if she said: I know (she says) that he is always present to me by his spiritual presence, and therefore I desire to serve my beloved with all devotion, but 348Bat length « his turning is toward me, » because I wish that at length, as he promised, he may appear to me in my own substance. For the spouse had said above: « I will go up into the palm; » that is, he had promised that he would come in the flesh, and would suffer for the human race, and thence she joyfully said, « I am my beloved's, and his turning is made toward me; » that is, I believe that he will come as man, and, in my substance, be manifested as man to the world.

« Come, my beloved, let us go forth into the field, let us abide in the villages. » It is the voice of the same Synagogue, « Come, my beloved, » that is, appear through the man thou hast assumed. Such also is that of the Psalmist, « Stir up thy might and come; » and of Isaiah, « O that thou wouldst rend the heavens, and come down. » « Let us go forth into the field. » 348CThe field is this world. « Let us go forth (she says) into the field, » that is, let us preach to the world. « Let us abide in the villages, » that is, let us announce thy faith also to the very pagans. No one is ignorant that the pagans are named from the village, because pagos in Greek is "village" in Latin. Thence they are called pagans, because they are far from the city of God. It can also be the voice of the Church, « Come, my beloved. » As if she said: Thou hast now, by the mystery of the ascension, raised the man thou didst assume up to the heavens, but come, by visiting me more often with the presence of thy divine majesty. « Let us go forth into the field. » In the same sense as above, Let us preach the faith of thy incarnation to the world. « Let us abide in the villages; » that is, let us announce thy faith even to the pagans who know thee not, and not in passing, but let us abide there, until we make them Catholics out of pagans, thine own 348Dout of strangers.

« Let us get up early to the vineyards. » The morning is the rising of the new grace, when, namely, the darkness of unbelief passing away, the Gospel began to be preached to the world. « Let us see if the vineyard flourish. » That is, let us inspect the Church, if she has now taken up the first rudiments of faith. « If the flowers bring forth fruits. » Let us inspect also if the flowers bring forth fruit, that is, if those who have now believed in Christ are fit for doing good. For the Church puts forth flowers, when the faithful first come to the faith. But the flowers bring forth fruits, when by that same faith and love she works well, lest the faith itself be idle, or dead without works; for, as James says, « faith without works is dead. » 349A« If the pomegranates flourish. » The pomegranates (as was said above) have a reddish or blood-colored rind, and therefore signify the passion of the Lord, or of his members. He says, therefore, « If the pomegranates flourish, » that is, if the saints who are now perfect in faith and in work are also fit to imitate the passion of Christ and to suffer for him. Pomegranates were those to whom the Apostle said: « To you it is given, not only to know, but also that you suffer for him. » « There will I give thee my breasts. » The breasts of the Church are the teachers, who first train the faithful to the faith. When (she says) we have gone out to the vineyards, there I will give thee, O spouse, my breasts, that is, I will beget thee sons. Whence there is further added:

« The mandrakes have given a smell in our gates. » 349BThe mandrake is an aromatic herb, whose root has the likeness of a human body. Its apples are of the best smell, in the likeness of the Matian apple, which our people call an apple of the earth. This herb is most fit for medicinal purposes. For they say that those who labor under the trouble of sleeplessness are relieved by a draught of this apple, and can sleep. Likewise they say that those who for the sake of a cure are to be cut, if they drink the outer rind of this apple, do not feel the cutting or the burning. All of which, if anyone should wish to inquire into mystically, he will find there spiritual meanings, and ones suited to the Church. He says, therefore, « The mandrakes have given a smell in our gates. » The gates of the Church are the apostles and their successors, because no one enters the city of God (that is, the Church) unless through the holy teachers he be regenerated by the water of baptism 349Cand instructed in the vital doctrine. Of these gates the Psalmist said: « The Lord loveth the gates of Sion above all the tabernacles of Jacob. » And the mandrakes designate the fragrance of the virtues. In the gates of the Church, therefore, the mandrakes have given a smell, when the apostles or their successors scattered far and wide the report of a most sweet reputation and the odor of the virtues. And therefore she invites her spouse to come, because now his name is announced far and wide through the apostles, and the odor of the preaching of Christ is dispersed far and wide. « All the apples, new and old, my beloved, I have kept for thee. » That is, the precepts or promises of the Old Testament and the New I have kept for thee, O my beloved. For she knew that all the things which were announced in the Old Testament could not be fulfilled except through Christ and in 349DChrist. And therefore she said, For thee I have kept the apples new and old, that is, I await thee, that through thee there may be fulfilled in the New Testament what was foretold in the Old. Whence also in his passion, already hanging on the cross, when he had tasted the vinegar, he said: « It is consummated. » And after the resurrection, to the apostles: It is necessary that all things be fulfilled which are written in the law and the prophets and the psalms concerning me. By the apples new 350Aand old can also be understood the persons of the just, who were either in the Old or in the New Testament. The Church says, therefore, I have kept for thee the new and the old, that is, the just who went before in the Old Testament, or followed after in the New, I have kept for thee, that through thee they might receive the rewards of their labors. For none of the just could enter the kingdom of heaven, except through Christ; because he first ascended, and to his following members opened heaven.

CAPUT VIII.

« Quis mihi det te fratrem meum sugentem ubera matris meae? » Ex his verbis intelligitur totum hoc carmen divinum esse et spirituale, et nihil in se quod juxta litteram intelligi possit, habere. Quae 350Benim unquam amatrix hoc optavit ut is quem diligit, parvulus efficiatur, et ejus frater, et in infantem versus sugat ubera matris suae? Est ergo vox illius Ecclesiae, quae adventum Christi praecessit, quam Synagogam (id est congregationem) appellamus. « Quis (inquit) mihi det te fratrem meum sugentem ubera matris meae? » Et est sensus, Tu qui modo es in sinu Patris, quis det ut homo efficiaris, et particeps ac consors meae naturae exsistens, frater meus appelleris? Nam ipse se fratrem apostolorum vocare dignatus est, dicens per Psalmistam: « Narrabo nomen tuum fratribus meis. » Et post resurrectionem mulieribus, « Ite, inquit, nuntiate fratribus meis. » « Sugentem ubera matris meae, » inquit, hoc est, implentem omnia quae naturae humanae conveniunt, 350Cexcepto peccato. Nam mater Synagogae in hoc loco ipsa humana natura intelligitur, de qua ipsa Synagoga exorta est. « Sugentem (inquit) ubera matris meae, » id est, sustinentem omnia quae humanae fragilitati competunt. « Ut inveniam te foris. » Hoc est, ut hominem factum in aperto videam. Quasi enim intus erat Deus, cum jam in sinu majestatis paternae lateret, sed foris inventus est, quando homo factus sobolem vilem se hominibus praebuit, et incomprehensibilis voluit comprehendi. « Et deosculer te. » Et deosculer te, inquit, id est, manifesta te aspiciam visione, quem nunc tantum in fidei teneo osculo, quod initio hujus carminis optavit, dicens: « Osculetur me osculo oris sui, » id est, per semetipsum jam veniat ut mihi appareat. 350DQuasi enim per prophetas osculabatur Ecclesiam dilectus, cum adventum suum per eos repromitteret. Optat ergo ut per seipsum veniens illam osculetur, hoc est in carne sua praesentiam exhibeat. « Et jam me nemo despiciat. » Quasi enim despecta erat ante Synagoga quam Christus veniret, quia jam legalibus observationibus dedita, sub typo et figura, et non in veritate adventum Christi 351Apraedicabat. At postquam Jesus apparuit, et adventus sui gratia mundum lustravit, jam nemo despexit eam, quinimo terribilis ut castrorum acies hostibus suis fuit, quia per universum mundum Christum praedicavit, et mundanum imperium sibi subdidit. Nec mirum videri debet si nunc Ecclesiam Synagogam dicimus. Una est enim Ecclesia in praecedentibus et in sequentibus Patribus.

« Apprehendam te, et ducam te in domum matris meae. » O dilecte, per fidem et charitatem tibi adhaerendo, ducam te in domum matris meae. Mater Ecclesiae, hoc loco coelestis Hierusalem intelligitur, de qua Paulus dicit: Illa autem quae sursum est Hierusalem, libera est, quae est mater nostra. « Ducam te (inquit) in domum matris meae, » id est, prosequar 351Bte revertentem in coelum, post actum incarnationis tuae et passionis mysterium. Ducam autem te, inquit, pro eo quod est, deducam et prosequar, quia ascendentem Dominum in coelos apostoli oculis deduxerunt, et quousque eum nubes suscepit, pio aspectu prosecuti sunt, donec angeli assistentes dixerunt: « Viri Galilaei, quid statis aspicientes in coelum? Hic Jesus qui assumptus est a vobis in coelum, sic veniet quemadmodum vidistis eum euntem in coelum. » « Ibi me docebis. » Hoc ad superiora refertur, ubi dicitur: « Inveniam te foris. » Cum (inquit) te foris invenero, hoc est, cum te in assumpta carne aspexero, ibi docebis me, quia ad omnia quae interrogare voluero, respondebis. Nam saepe etiam volentes interrogare apostolos Dominus 351Cpraeveniebat, eosque instruebat. Potest et ad hunc locum jungi, ubi dicit: « Ducam te in domum matris meae, ibi me docebis. » Cum (inquit) coelo receptus fueris, et me quoque in coelum tecum assumpseris, ibi me docebis, et in omnem veritatem induces, quod impossibile est homini in praesinti vita posse consequi; hinc est quod Dominus repromittit apostolis, dicens: « Venit hora, cum jam non in proverbiis loquar vobis, sed palam de Patre annuntiabo vobis. » Ergo ibi me docebis, id est, te et Patrem mihi aperta visione revelabis, hoc est, divinitatem tuam mihi facie ad faciem ostendes. « Et dabo tibi poculum ex vino condito. » Per vinum fervor charitatis et dilectionis intelligitur. Dabo (inquit) tibi vinum, hoc est, ostendam quam 351Dferventissima charitate te diligam. Et hoc vinum non simplex erit, sed conditum, bonis videlicet operibus et virtutibus. Nam amor Dei, sicut beatus Gregorius dicit, nunquam est otiosus, operatur magna si est. Si enim operari renuit, amor non est. Vinum enim conditum est dilectio cum operatione. Unde et Dominus in Evangelio: « Si quis diligit me, sermonem meum servabit. » Probatio dilectionis exhibitio est operis. « Et mustum malorum granatorum meorum. » Malum granatum idem est quod et malum Punicum. Dicitur autem malum granatum, eo quod multitudine granorum sit plenum. Malum Punicum, eo quod in Africa plurimum abundat. Punica enim ipsa est Africa. Habet autem 352Acorticem sanguinei coloris, et ideo (ut saepe dictum est) figuram tenet Dominicae passionis. Mustus enim malorum granatorum est dilectio sanctorum martyrum, qui intantum Deum dilexerunt, ut pro illo mori non dubitarent. Et bene mustum dicitur, non aliud quodlibet poculum, quia mustum ferventissimum est, ita ut in nimio fervore omnem sordem ex se excoquat et purget. Dabit ergo Ecclesia dilecto mustum granatorum suorum, id est, ostendet in passionibus sanctorum martyrum quanta charitate Christus diligatur.

« Laeva ejus sub capite meo, et dextera illius amplexabitur me. » Per laevam (ut beatus Gregorius dicit) omnia prospera praesentis vitae accipiuntur; per dexteram vero futura beata vita in 352Bcoelis accipitur. Laevam ergo sponsi Ecclesia sub capite habet et dextera ejus illam amplexatur, quia quaecunque prospera praesentis saeculi blandiuntur, pro nihilo deputat, cuncta quae temporaliter arrident despicit; et dum ad solam supernae beatitudinis visionem tendit, illam solummodo amplexari desiderat.

« Adjuro vos, filiae Hierusalem, ne suscitetis, neque evigilare faciatis dilectam donec ipsa velit. » Habet Ecclesia quosdam in se qui baptismo Christi sunt renati, et nomine Christianitatis titulati, moribus tamen et conversatione saeculo serviunt, et Ecclesiam sauvissima sopitam quiete inquietare, atque ad perferendos tumultus excitare nituntur. Hi ergo propter gratiam mysteriorum Christi, quae 352Cperceperunt, filiae Hierusalem appellantur in hoc loco, cum tamen moribus contraria agant. Et adjurat eos sponsus ne suscitent vel evigilare faciant dilectam, hoc est, ne illam in somno dulcissimae quietis positam, hoc est, in contemplatione sui Conditoris sua inquietudine excitent, et ad sollicitudines et curas saeculi evigilare compellant. « Donec ipsa velit; » hoc est, donec peracto officio debitae servitutis, qua in psalmis, hymnis, et cantibus spiritualibus Deo famulatur, admoneatur iterum cura corporeae fragilitatis ad usum saeculi redire.

« Quae est ista quae ascendit de deserto, deliciis affluens, innixa super dilectum meum? » Vox Synagogae, admirantis Ecclesiam de gentibus congregatam. 352DQuae est, inquit, id est qualis est, quanti meriti, quae ascendit de deserto? Derelicta enim fuerat a Deo per idololatriam et veritatis ignorantiam. Ascendebat ergo virtutum gradibus de deserto, hoc est de errore infidelitatis, in quo ad tempus derelicta fuerat. « Deliciis affluens, » id est virtutibus abundans. « Innixa (inquit) super dilectum meum, » id est, fiducialiter agens in Christo, et omnes virtutes suas et cuncta bona ad ejus gratiam referens, sciebat se nihil suis viribus boni implere posse, ideoque cum Apostolo dicens: « Gratia Dei sum id quod sum. » Et notandum quod dicit, Super dilectum meum. Nam Synagoga solam putabat se habere cognitionem Dei, caeteris gentibus in ignorantia 353Adestitutis. « Notus enim, » ut ait Psalmista, « in Judaea Deus, in Israel magnum nomen ejus. » Sed ita miranti respondet Synagogae ipsius sponsus et dilectus quod sequitur.

« Sub arbore malo suscitavi te. » Arborem malum, crucem Dominicam debemus accipere, de qua arbore suscitata est Synagoga, quia ipsa a praevaricatione originalis peccati et potestate diaboli per crucem Christi redempta est, ibi suscitata est quae peccatis mortua erat. Et bene arborem mali dicit. Nam, ut supra dictum est, huic arbori Christum comparavit, dicens: « Sicut malum inter ligna silvarum, sic dilectus meus inter filios. » « Ibi corrupta est mater tua, ibi violata est genitrix tua. » Matrem et genitricem Synagogae, majorem et principaliorem 353BJudaicae plebis patrem dicit, quales erant Scribae et Pharisaei, qui Christum Pilato tradiderunt, qui reliquam plebem tanquam mater et genitrix regere debebant et erudire, et quales erant illi qui suadentibus illis principibus, improbe acclamabant: « Crucifige, crucifige eum. » Haec ergo mater Synagogae sub arbore malo corrupta est et violata, quando se suosque posteros crudeli maledictione constrinxit, dicens: « Sanguis ejus super nos et super filios nostros; » nam et isti sub cruce Christi erant, licet non fide et devotione, tamen crudeles vindictae sibi et suis posteris imprecatione.

« Pone me ut signaculum super cor tuum, ut signaculum super brachium tuum. » Quia (inquit) sub arbore malo suscitavi te, hoc est, quia passione 353Cmea in cruce te redemi, pone me ut signaculum super cor tuum, hoc est, habeto me semper in memoria, et ne obliviscaris mei, quanta pro tua salute pertulerim. Pone me (inquit) ut signaculum super cor tuum per fidem, pone me ut signaculum super cor tuum per dignam operationem. Nam et nos quando aliquid in memoria habere volumus, signaculum digito vel brachio imponere solemus, ut illo admoniti, illius rei cujus curam habemus recordemur. Et nos ut signaculum super cor et brachium Christum ponere debemus, ut ejus memoriam semper habeamus, et ut recte in illum credentes, fidem ipsam bonis operibus exornemus. Quod si fecerimus, ipse quoque nos velut signaculum assumet, et nostri recordabitur, juxta quod Zorobabel duci Judaeae repromittit: 353D« Et ponam te sicut signaculum in conspectu meo. » Nam si nos beneficiorum ejus obliti fuerimus, nec ei dignas gratiarum actiones retulerimus, ipse quoque nostri obliviscetur, sicut de impio rege Jechonia filio Joachim dicitur: « Si fuerit annulus dexter in manu mea, inde evellam eum. » « Quia fortis est ut mors dilectio, dura ut inferus aemulatio. » Dilectio Christi fortis est ut mors. Sicut enim mors animam a corpore separat, et jam nihil concupiscere, nihil in praesenti vita homini licet ambire, ita dilectio Christi quem vere pervaserit, totum huic saeculo mortificat, et quasi insensibilem reddit, solumque pro Christo vivens, mundo mortuus est. Idem sensus est cum dicit: « Dura ut 354Ainferus aemulatio. » Nam sicut infernus eos quos semel recipit nunquam restituit, sed semper retinet, ita verus Christi amor quem semel acceperit, nunquam dimittit; unde Apostolus dicit: « Quis nos separabit a charitate Christi? tribulatio? an angustia? an persecutio? an fames? an nuditas? an periculum? an gladius? » Sed et dilectio Christi hoc loco potest accipi, cujus dilectio fortis fuit ut mors, quia in tantum dilexit nos, ut pro nobis immortalis mortem susciperet. Dura fuit aemulatio ejus ut inferus, quia inferus nescit misereri aut a poenis miserorum mitigari, sic et Christi dilectio non potuit a misericordia cessare, aut ullis improbitatibus persecutorum removeri ab amore humani generis. Quidam hoc quod dicitur, « Dura est ut inferus 354Baemulatio, » ad Synagogam proprie referunt, quae gentes aemulabatur, et invidebat saluti eorum. Monet ergo Christus hoc dicens, ut ab invidia cesset, et saluti Ecclesiae congaudeat. « Lampades ejus, lampades ignis atque flammarum. » Id est, dilectionis lampades sunt lampades ignis atque flammarum. Lampades dilectionis praecordia sunt sanctorum, in quibus veluti in vasis dilectio habitat. Hae ergo lampades, lampades quidem sunt ignis, quia in corde ardent per amorem; lampades vero flammarum, quia exterius lucent per operationem.

« Aquae multae non poterunt exstinguere charitatem, nec flumina obruent illam. » Aquae et flumina sunt violentae persecutorum minae, vel etiam blandimenta, quibus sanctos a charitate Dei separare 354Cnituntur. De quibus Dominus in Evangelio dicit, de viro qui aedificavit domum suam supra petram: « Venerunt flumina, flaverunt venti, et impegerunt in domum illam, et non cecidit: fundata enim erat supra pertram. » « Aquae ergo multae non poterunt exstinguere charitatem, » quia nulla tribulatio, nulla angustia sanctos a charitate Dei separare valebit. « Si dederit homo omnem substantiam domus suae pro dilectione, quasi nihil despiciet eam. » Hic versiculus non eget expositione. Nam nullius mundi substantiae, nullae opes comparatione dilectionis aliquid sunt. Legimus quidem sanctos sua dimisisse pro amore Christi, sed tamen nihil sibi videbantur dimittere in comparatione charitatis Christi, quem amabant. Unde Apostolus cum non solum terrenam substantiam, sed 354Det paternas traditiones dimitteret pro Christo, dicebat: « Haec omnia contemno et arbitror ut stercora, ut Christum lucrifaciam, » id est, ut amorem illius acquiram.

« Soror nostra parva est, et ubera non habet. Quid faciemus sorori nostrae in die quando alloquenda est? » Vox dilecti Synagogam alloquentis pro Ecclesia gentium. « Soror nostra, » inquit, id est gentium Ecclesia, « parva est, et ubera non habet; » et magna dignatione communicato cum Synagoga nomine dicit, « Soror nostra: » quia enim ex Judaeis carnem assumpsit, ideo se quasi Synagogae contemperat et exaequat. « Parva est (inquit) soror nostra. » Sicut alicujus hominis aetas describitur, 355Asic et aetas describitur Ecclesiae. Parva enim erat Ecclesia adhuc de gentibus congregata, sub ipsis Domini et apostolorum temporibus, quia et parvitate credentium parva exsistebat; et ubera necdum habebat, id est nequaquam praedicando spirituales Deo filios generare valebat. Dicit ergo quasi consilium Synagogae requirens, « Quid faciemus sorori nostrae in die quando alloquenda est? » Allocutio Ecclesiae est vel cum intrinsecus occulta inspiratione, vel exterius aperta praedicatione admonet Deus animam cujusque fidelis. Et est sensus: Quando per apostolos meos alloquar Ecclesiam de gentibus congregatam, quid faciemus? quomodo illi praecepta coelestia committemus? Utrum videlicet minima quaeque praecepta illi committemus, an quo major efficiatur, 355Bperfectiora illi secretorum coelestium mysteria trademus? Nam Deus omnipotens pro mensura et capacitate mentis humanae temperat verbum praedicationis, considerans vires et capacitatem auditoris. Sed Synagoga tacente, ipse sponsus pro ea respondit:

« Si murus est, aedificemus super eum propugnacula argentea; si ostium est, compingamus illud tabulis cedrinis. » Si (inquit) soror nostra est murus, aedificemus super eum propugnacula argentea. Ac si dicat: Cum coeperint apostoli mei praedicare Ecclesiae de gentibus congregatae, si tales fuerint auditores qui merito perfectionis murus appellari possint, dum videlicet vel naturali ingenio praediti, vel philosophica eruditione praestantes, quasi alios 355Cdefendere et munire possint vice muri, aedificemus super hunc talem murum propugnacula argentea, id est, addamus illis eloquia divinarum Scripturarum. Nam, ut Psalmista dicit: « Eloquia Domini eloquia casta, argentum igne examinatum. » Si vero tales fuerint auditores qui nequaquam hostibus resistere possint, nec tela contra venientia repellere, sed fuerint vel ostium ut simplicia annuntiantes possint aliquos in Ecclesia et ad fidem adducere, quales sunt sacerdotes minoris gradus, qui baptizando in Ecclesia, et divinis mysteriis initiando homines in Ecclesiam introducunt, compingamus illud ostium tabulis cedrinis; id est, proponamus eis exempla praecedentium patrum, qui, veluti cedri, imputribilia videlicet ligna, id est immarcescibilia opera 355Dhabent, et veluti tabulae scientiae et charitatis latitudine diffusi sunt. Latitudo enim tabularum latitudinem cordis vel scientiae significat, dicente Psalmista: « Viam mandatorum tuorum cucurri, cum dilatares cor meum. » His ergo cedrinis tabulis, (id est sanctorum exemplis) compingamus illud ostium, ut videlicet illorum imitanda exempla aliis Ecclesiam introeundi veluti ostium januam aperiant.

« Ego murus, et ubera mea sicut turris. » Vox Ecclesiae. Quae cum audisset pro sua salute consulentem dilectum suum, ipsa laeta respondit: « Ego sum murus, et ubera mea sicut turris. » Ego (inquit) murus, ego supra firmam petram fundata sum, et glutine divinae charitatis solidata. Sive murus 356Asum, quia de vivis et electis lapidibus (hoc est sanctis) aedificata sum; « et ubera mea sicut turris, » quia tales intra me contineo, qui vice uberum alios nutrire possunt spirituali doctrina, et vice turris possunt alios defendere et munire, quique sicut turres in muro eminent, sic merito doctrinae et conversationis inter reliquos membra mea praecellunt. Hoc autem non meis meritis, neque libertate mei arbitrii habeo, sed dono et gratia sponsi mei. « Ex quo facta sum coram eo quasi pacem reperiens. » Id est, ex quo ille sanguinem suum pro me fudit, et repropitiando mihi Deum Patrem, dissolvit inimicitias quae erant inter Deum et hominem, mihique coelestia pacificavit. Ex quo ergo hanc pacem reperi, murus esse et ubera vice turrium 356Bmerui.

« Vinea fuit pacifico, » id est dilecto meo, qui est verus pacificus, quia per eum pacificata sunt omnia quae sunt in coelis et in terra. Unde, et a Propheta Pater futuri saeculi, princeps pacis appellatur, in cujus veri pacifici typo Salomon pacificus, auctor hujus carminis praecessit. Huic ergo fuit vinea, id est sancta Ecclesia. « In ea quae habet populos. » Hoc dicit ut se de Ecclesia gentium, non de Synagoga, ostenderet canere. Nam Synagoga unius gentis fuit tantum. Ecclesia vero habet populos, quia omnes gentes intra capacissimum suum gremium capit et recipit. « Tradidit eam custodibus. » Custodes hujus vineae (id est sanctae Ecclesiae) sunt prophetae, apostoli apostolorumque successores. 356CSive etiam custodes hujus saeculi sunt angelicae virtutes, quae ab incursibus tentationum et daemonum Ecclesiam custodiunt. « Vir affert pro fructu ejus mille argenteos. » Viros, fortes dicit et perfectos, nam vir a virtute dicitur. Fructus Ecclesiae est coelestis vitae remuneratio, pro qua sancta Ecclesia in praesenti laborare non cessat. Mille autem argentei, quos pro fructu vir affert, significant omnia quae in hoc mundo possideri possunt. Millenarius enim numerus pro summa perfectione et plenitudine ponitur. Vir ergo pro fructu hujus vineae mille argenteos affert, quia sancti omnia sua relinquunt, et pauperibus erogant, ut fructum hujus vineae (hoc est, supernam mercedem) consequantur. Tale est illud quod Dominus in Evangelio dicit: 356D« Simile est regnum coelorum thesauro abscondito in agro, quem qui invenit homo, abscondit, et prae gaudio illius vadit, et vendit omnia quae habet, et emit agrum illum. »

« Vinea mea coram me est. » Vox sponsi. Tu (inquit) dicis quia vineam meam tradidi custodibus, tamen vinea semper coram me est, quia licet eam custodibus meis commiserim, ego tamen semper curam illius habeo, nec pro illa semper sollicitus esse desisto, sed sum cum illa usque ad consummationem saeculi. « Mille tui pacifici, et ducenti his qui custodiunt fructus ejus. » Mille tui, subauditur argentei, quos pro vineae dedisti fructu, pacifici tibi sunt. Quasi diceret: O anima fidelis, noli dubia 357Aesse pro aeterna mercede, pro qua omnia tua dedisti, quia mille argentei pacifici sunt tibi, id est, manet te certa illa remuneratio, et verum inde fructum consecutura es. Et ducenti, subaudis argentei, pacifici sunt his qui custodiunt fructus meos. Sicut per millenarium plenitudo perfectionis, sic et per centenarium summa perfectio designatur, quia centenarius de laeva in dexteram transfertur. Ducenti ergo argentei, qui est duplicatus centenarius, significant eos qui doctrina et sancta operatione perfecti sunt, qui juste et pie vivendo semetipsos custodiunt, et etiam praedicando alios convertunt. His ergo talibus ducenti argentei sunt pacifici, quia duplicata in coelis eis manet remuneratio. De qualibus per Danielem dicitur: « Et qui ad justitiam 357Berudiunt multos, fulgebunt quasi stellae in perpetuas aeternitates. » Hos enim Apostolus dupliciter jubet honorari, dicens: « Presbyteri qui in nobis sunt, duplici honore digni habeantur, maxime qui praesunt vobis, et laborant in verbo. »

« Quae habitas in hortis, amici auscultant, fac me audire vocem tuam. » Vox dilecti sponsam alloquentis: O Ecclesia, quae habitas in hortis, id est, quae excolendis virtutum germinibus insistis. Hortus etenim ipsa Ecclesia est, quam (ut superius dictum est) crebro se promittit invisere. Ecclesia ergo in hortis habitat, quia plantaria virtutum assidue nutrit, nec transitorie modicum recessura, ibi tugurium ponit, sed fixa statione habitat. « Fac me, inquit, audire vocem tuam, » id est, praedica quibus 357Cvales Evangelium, praecepta meae legis simul et promissum coelestium praemiorum annuntia. Hoc enim mihi jucundum et delectabile est, ut audiam vocem tuam, hoc est ut audiam te verba salutis aeternae annuntiantem. Sed « amici auscultant, » id est, angelici spiritus vocem tuam audire delectant, quos tibi amicos feci effusione sanguinis mei, quosque adjutores et protectores contra malignos spiritus institui. Sive amici, id est, justorum spiritus auscultant, quos de tuo coetu ad me in coelestia assumpsi. Ipsi enim de tua praedicatione et fratrum salute congaudent. Respondet confestim ipsa sponsa dilecto suo:

« Fuge, dilecte mi, et assimilare capreae hinnu loque cervorum super montes aromatum. » Mira 357Dresponsio. Invitatur ad praedicandum, et ut sponsus 358Aaudiat vocem ejus, illa vero hortatur sponsum suum ut fugiat. Sed magna est mysterii consequentia. Nam quia superius praedicta fuerat Christi nativitas, cum dixit: « Quis det te fratrem meum sugentem ubera. » Praedicta enim fuerat ejus passio, ubi dictum est: « Sub arbore malo suscitavi te, » nunc consequenter hortatur sponsum ut fugiat, hoc est velocissime per ascensionem ad Patrem redeat. Et est sensus: Jubes me praedicare? vis vocem meam audire? fuge, dilecte mi, id est, impleto incarnationis tuae et passionis mysterio, revertere ad coelum, ut non jam te sicut hominem, sed sicut Deum super omnia cogitare incipiam. Tunc enim audies vocem meam, quia tunc te liberius omni mundo praedicabo. Et hoc est quod Dominus in Evangelio dicit: 358B« Ego veritatem dico vobis, expedit vobis ut ego vadam. » Expediebat enim apostolis et omni Ecclesiae ut Christus rediret, et praesentiam corporalem absentaret, quia dum in corpore praesens erat, non poterant eum spiritu aliter amare, non poterant eum quem hominem cernebant, ut Deum invisibilem et ubique praesentem cogitare. At postquam ipse coelos ascendit, coepit eum Ecclesia spiritualiter diligere, et sic jam Deum cogitare et praedicare. Et hoc est quod Apostolus dicit: « Et si noveramus Christum secundum carnem, sed nunc jam non novimus. » Ergo, dilecte mi, si vis ita me te praedicare, fuge (id est recede) ad coelestia, et qui fuisti comprehensibilis per humanitatem, efficere incomprehensibilis per divinitatem, verumtamen ne me 358Cin praesenti sine tua visitatione deseras, sed dignare me crebra tua visitatione requirere, mihique te saepius videndum praebere. Et hoc est quod dicit: « Similis esto capreae hinnuloque cervorum super montes aromatum. » Sicut (inquit) capreae et hinnuli cervorum indomita animalia humanum consortium refugiunt, et tamen crebrius in montibus videntur, sic et tu post tuam ascensionem, licet invisibilis, dignare mihi tamen saepius dono tuae gratiae apparere, et hoc super montes aromatum. Montes aromatum sancti Dei sunt, excellentia sanctae conversationis coelo proximi, et odore ac fama virtutum longe lateque fragrantes. Hinc Apostolus de se suique similibus dicit: « Nostra conversatio in coelis est. » Et alibi: « Christi bonus odor sumus in 358Domni loco. »

CHAPTER VIII.

« Who shall give thee to me for my brother, sucking the breasts of my mother? » From these words it is understood that this whole song is divine and spiritual, and has in it nothing that could be understood according to the letter. For 350Bwhat lover ever wished this, that he whom she loves should be made a little child, and her brother, and, turned into an infant, should suck the breasts of his mother? It is, therefore, the voice of that Church which preceded the coming of Christ, whom we call the Synagogue (that is, the congregation). « Who (she says) shall give thee to me for my brother, sucking the breasts of my mother? » And the sense is: Thou who now art in the bosom of the Father, who will grant that thou be made man, and, existing as a partaker and sharer of my nature, be called my brother? For he himself deigned to call himself the brother of the apostles, saying through the Psalmist: « I will declare thy name to my brethren. » And after the resurrection to the women, « Go, he said, tell my brethren. » « Sucking the breasts of my mother, » he says, that is, fulfilling all things that belong to human nature, 350Csin excepted. For the mother of the Synagogue is understood in this place to be human nature itself, from which the Synagogue itself sprang. « Sucking (he says) the breasts of my mother, » that is, enduring all things that pertain to human frailty. « That I may find thee without. » That is, that I may see thee, made man, in the open. For God was, as it were, within, when he yet lay hidden in the bosom of the paternal majesty, but he was found without, when, made man, he offered himself as lowly offspring to men, and, being incomprehensible, willed to be comprehended. « And I may kiss thee. » And I may kiss thee, he says, that is, I may behold with manifest vision him whom now I hold only in the kiss of faith — which she desired at the beginning of this song, saying: « Let him kiss me with the kiss of his mouth, » that is, let him now come through himself, that he may appear to me. 350DFor the beloved, as it were, kissed the Church through the prophets, when he promised his coming through them. She desires, therefore, that, coming through himself, he may kiss her, that is, may display his presence in his own flesh. « And now let no one despise me. » For the Synagogue was, as it were, despised before Christ came, because, given up now to the observances of the law, she preached the coming of Christ under a type and figure, and not in truth. 351ABut after Jesus appeared, and by the grace of his coming brightened the world, now no one despised her; nay rather she was terrible to her enemies as an army set in array, because throughout the whole world she preached Christ, and subjected the worldly empire to herself. Nor ought it to seem a wonder if we now call the Church the Synagogue. For there is one Church in the preceding and in the following Fathers.

« I will take hold of thee, and lead thee into the house of my mother. » O beloved, by cleaving to thee through faith and charity, I will lead thee into the house of my mother. The mother of the Church, in this place, is understood as the heavenly Jerusalem, of which Paul says: But that Jerusalem which is above is free, which is our mother. « I will lead thee (she says) into the house of my mother, » that is, I will follow after 351Bthee returning into heaven, after the act of thy incarnation and the mystery of thy passion. And "I will lead thee," she says, for that which is "I will conduct and follow after," because the apostles conducted with their eyes the Lord ascending into heaven, and with devout gaze followed after him until the cloud received him, until the angels standing by said: « Ye men of Galilee, why stand you looking up to heaven? This Jesus who is taken up from you into heaven shall so come as you have seen him going into heaven. » « There thou shalt teach me. » This is referred to the words above, where it is said: « I will find thee without. » When (she says) I have found thee without, that is, when I have beheld thee in the assumed flesh, there thou shalt teach me, because to all that I shall wish to ask, thou wilt answer. For often the Lord anticipated even the apostles wishing to ask, 351Cand instructed them. It can also be joined to this place, where she says: « I will lead thee into the house of my mother, there thou shalt teach me. » When (she says) thou hast been received into heaven, and hast taken me also with thee into heaven, there thou shalt teach me, and lead me into all truth, which is impossible for man in the present life to be able to attain; hence it is that the Lord promises to the apostles, saying: « The hour cometh, when I will no more speak to you in proverbs, but will show you plainly of the Father. » Therefore there thou shalt teach me, that is, thou wilt reveal to me, in open vision, thee and the Father, that is, thou wilt show me thy divinity face to face. « And I will give thee a cup of spiced wine. » By the wine is understood the fervor of charity and love. I will give (she says) thee wine, that is, I will show with how 351Dmost fervent charity I love thee. And this wine will not be simple, but spiced, namely with good works and virtues. For the love of God, as blessed Gregory says, is never idle; it works great things if it be present. For if it refuses to work, it is not love. For spiced wine is love with operation. Whence also the Lord in the Gospel: « If any one love me, he will keep my word. » The proof of love is the showing of the work. « And the new wine of my pomegranates. » The pomegranate is the same as the Punic apple. And it is called a pomegranate (malum granatum), because it is full of a multitude of seeds (grana). It is a Punic apple, because it abounds most greatly in Africa. For Punica is Africa itself. And it has 352Aa rind of a blood color, and therefore (as has often been said) holds the figure of the Lord's passion. For the new wine of pomegranates is the love of the holy martyrs, who so loved God that they did not hesitate to die for him. And well is it called new wine, and no other cup whatever, because new wine is most fervent, so that in its exceeding fervor it boils off and purges from itself all filth. The Church, therefore, will give the beloved the new wine of her pomegranates, that is, she will show in the sufferings of the holy martyrs with how great charity Christ is loved.

« His left hand is under my head, and his right hand shall embrace me. » By the left (as blessed Gregory says) are understood all the prosperous things of the present life; but by the right the future blessed life in 352Bthe heavens is understood. The Church, therefore, has the left hand of the spouse under her head, and his right hand embraces her, because whatever prosperous things of the present world flatter her, she counts as nothing, and despises all that smile upon her temporally; and while she strives toward the vision of the heavenly blessedness alone, she desires to embrace that alone.

« I adjure you, daughters of Jerusalem, that you stir not up, nor make the beloved to awake, till she please. » The Church has within her certain persons who are reborn by the baptism of Christ, and marked with the name of Christianity, yet in their morals and manner of life serve the world, and strive to disquiet the Church, lulled in most sweet rest, and to rouse her to endure tumults. These, therefore, on account of the grace of the mysteries of Christ which 352Cthey have received, are called daughters of Jerusalem in this place, although in their morals they do contrary things. And the spouse adjures them that they stir not up, nor make the beloved to awake, that is, that they rouse not her — set in the sleep of most sweet rest, that is, in the contemplation of her Maker — by their disquiet, and compel her to awake to the solicitudes and cares of the world. « Till she please; » that is, until, the office of due service accomplished, by which in psalms, hymns, and spiritual songs she serves God, she be admonished again by the care of bodily frailty to return to the use of the world.

« Who is she that cometh up from the desert, flowing with delights, leaning upon my beloved? » It is the voice of the Synagogue, marveling at the Church gathered from the nations. 352DWho is she, she says, that is, of what kind is she, of what merit, who cometh up from the desert? For she had been forsaken by God through idolatry and ignorance of the truth. She was coming up, therefore, by the steps of the virtues from the desert, that is, from the error of unbelief, in which for a time she had been forsaken. « Flowing with delights, » that is, abounding in virtues. « Leaning (she says) upon my beloved, » that is, acting confidently in Christ, and referring all her virtues and all her good things to his grace, she knew that she could accomplish no good by her own strength, and therefore said with the Apostle: « By the grace of God I am what I am. » And it is to be noted that she says, Upon my beloved. For the Synagogue thought that she alone had the knowledge of God, the other nations being left in 353Aignorance. « For God is known, » as the Psalmist says, « in Judea; in Israel his name is great. » But to the Synagogue thus marveling, her own spouse and beloved answers that which follows.

« Under the apple tree I raised thee up. » By the apple tree we ought to understand the Lord's cross, from which tree the Synagogue was raised up, because she was redeemed from the transgression of original sin and the power of the devil through the cross of Christ; there was she raised up who was dead in sins. And well does he say the apple tree. For, as was said above, to this tree he compared Christ, saying: « As the apple tree among the trees of the woods, so is my beloved among the sons. » « There was thy mother corrupted, there was she deflowered that bore thee. » By the mother and her that bore the Synagogue he means the greater and more principal 353Bfathers of the Jewish people, such as were the Scribes and Pharisees, who delivered Christ to Pilate, who ought to have ruled and instructed the rest of the people as a mother and one who bears, and such as were those who, at the urging of those princes, wickedly cried out: « Crucify, crucify him. » This mother of the Synagogue, therefore, was corrupted and deflowered under the apple tree, when she bound herself and her posterity with a cruel curse, saying: « His blood be upon us and upon our children; » for these too were under the cross of Christ, though not by faith and devotion, yet by the cruel imprecation of vengeance upon themselves and their posterity.

« Set me as a seal upon thy heart, as a seal upon thy arm. » Because (he says) under the apple tree I raised thee up, that is, because by my passion 353Con the cross I redeemed thee, set me as a seal upon thy heart, that is, hold me always in memory, and forget not me, how much I endured for thy salvation. Set me (he says) as a seal upon thy heart through faith, set me as a seal upon thy heart through worthy operation. For we too, when we wish to hold something in memory, are accustomed to set a seal upon the finger or the arm, so that, admonished by it, we may remember that thing for which we have care. And we ought to set Christ as a seal upon our heart and arm, so that we may always have his memory, and, rightly believing in him, may adorn that very faith with good works. And if we do this, he too will take us up as a seal, and will remember us, according to what he promises to Zerubbabel the leader of Judea: 353D« And I will set thee as a seal in my sight. » For if we have forgotten his benefits, and have not rendered him worthy thanksgivings, he too will forget us, as is said of the impious king Jeconiah the son of Joachim: « If he were a ring on my right hand, thence would I pluck him off. » « For love is strong as death, jealousy hard as hell. » The love of Christ is strong as death. For just as death separates the soul from the body, and now nothing is it permitted to man to desire, nothing to strive after in the present life, so the love of Christ, which has truly pervaded anyone, mortifies him wholly to this world, and renders him as it were insensible, and, living for Christ alone, he is dead to the world. The same is the sense when he says: « Jealousy hard as 354Ahell. » For just as hell never restores those whom it once receives, but ever retains them, so the true love of Christ, which it has once received, it never lets go; whence the Apostle says: « Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation? or distress? or persecution? or famine? or nakedness? or danger? or the sword? » But also the love of Christ can be understood in this place, whose love was strong as death, because he so loved us that, being immortal, he took death for us. His jealousy was hard as hell, because hell knows not to have mercy or to be softened by the punishments of the wretched, so also the love of Christ could not cease from mercy, or by any wickednesses of persecutors be removed from the love of the human race. Some refer this that is said, « Jealousy is hard as hell, » 354Bproperly to the Synagogue, which was jealous of the nations, and envied their salvation. Christ therefore admonishes by saying this, that she cease from envy, and rejoice together at the salvation of the Church. « The lamps thereof are lamps of fire and of flames. » That is, the lamps of love are lamps of fire and of flames. The lamps of love are the hearts of the saints, in which, as in vessels, love dwells. These lamps, therefore, are indeed lamps of fire, because they burn in the heart through love; but they are lamps of flames, because outwardly they shine through operation.

« Many waters cannot quench charity, neither shall the floods overwhelm it. » The waters and the floods are the violent threats of persecutors, or also the enticements, by which they strive to separate the saints from the love of God. 354COf which the Lord says in the Gospel, concerning the man who built his house upon a rock: « The floods came, the winds blew, and beat upon that house, and it fell not: for it was founded upon a rock. » « Many waters, therefore, cannot quench charity, » because no tribulation, no distress, will be able to separate the saints from the love of God. « If a man should give all the substance of his house for love, he shall despise it as nothing. » This little verse needs no exposition. For no substance of the world, no riches, are anything in comparison with love. We read indeed that the saints left their goods for the love of Christ, but yet they seemed to themselves to leave nothing in comparison with the charity of Christ, whom they loved. Whence the Apostle, when he left not only earthly substance, but 354Dalso the paternal traditions for Christ, said: « All these things I count and esteem as dung, that I may gain Christ, » that is, that I may acquire his love.

« Our sister is little, and hath no breasts. What shall we do to our sister in the day when she is to be spoken to? » It is the voice of the beloved, speaking to the Synagogue on behalf of the Church of the nations. « Our sister, » he says, that is, the Church of the nations, « is little, and hath no breasts; » and with great condescension, the name being shared with the Synagogue, he says, « Our sister: » because he assumed flesh from the Jews, therefore he tempers himself, as it were, to the Synagogue and makes himself its equal. « Our sister (he says) is little. » Just as the age of some man is described, 355Aso also the age of the Church is described. For the Church, as yet gathered from the nations, was little in the very times of the Lord and the apostles, because both by the smallness of believers she was little; and she had not yet breasts, that is, she was by no means able, by preaching, to beget spiritual sons to God. He says, therefore, as if seeking the counsel of the Synagogue, « What shall we do to our sister in the day when she is to be spoken to? » The speaking to the Church is when God admonishes the soul of each of the faithful, either inwardly by hidden inspiration, or outwardly by open preaching. And the sense is: When through my apostles I shall speak to the Church gathered from the nations, what shall we do? how shall we commit to her the heavenly precepts? Namely whether we shall commit to her the least precepts, or, that she may be made the greater, 355Bshall we hand over to her the more perfect mysteries of the heavenly secrets? For God almighty tempers the word of preaching according to the measure and capacity of the human mind, considering the strength and capacity of the hearer. But while the Synagogue was silent, the spouse himself answered for her:

« If she be a wall, let us build upon it bulwarks of silver; if she be a door, let us join it together with boards of cedar. » If (he says) our sister be a wall, let us build upon it bulwarks of silver. As if he should say: When my apostles shall have begun to preach to the Church gathered from the nations, if the hearers shall be such as by the merit of perfection can be called a wall — that is, when, being either endowed with natural genius, or excelling in philosophical learning, they can as it were 355Cdefend and fortify others in the stead of a wall — let us build upon such a wall bulwarks of silver, that is, let us add to them the eloquences of the divine Scriptures. For, as the Psalmist says: « The words of the Lord are chaste words, silver tried by the fire. » But if the hearers shall be such as can by no means resist the enemy, nor repel the darts coming against them, but shall be a door, as, announcing simple things, they can bring some into the Church and to the faith — such as are priests of a lesser grade, who, by baptizing in the Church, and initiating men into the divine mysteries, introduce them into the Church — let us join that door together with boards of cedar; that is, let us set before them the examples of the fathers who went before, who, like cedars — namely incorruptible wood, that is, imperishable works 355D— have works, and, like boards, are spread out with breadth of knowledge and charity. For the breadth of the boards signifies the breadth of the heart or of knowledge, the Psalmist saying: « I have run the way of thy commandments, when thou didst enlarge my heart. » With these boards of cedar, then (that is, with the examples of the saints), let us join that door together, so that, namely, their examples, to be imitated, may open to others, as a door, an entrance for entering the Church.

« I am a wall, and my breasts are as a tower. » It is the voice of the Church. Who, when she had heard her beloved taking counsel for her salvation, herself joyfully answers: « I am a wall, and my breasts are as a tower. » I (she says) am a wall, I am founded upon the firm rock, and made solid with the glue of divine charity. Or I am a wall, 356Abecause I am built of living and chosen stones (that is, of the saints); « and my breasts are as a tower, » because I contain within me such as, in the stead of breasts, can nourish others with spiritual doctrine, and, in the stead of a tower, can defend and fortify others, and who, as towers stand out in the wall, so by the merit of doctrine and manner of life excel my other members. And this I have not by my own merits, nor by the liberty of my own will, but by the gift and grace of my spouse. « Since I am become in his presence as one finding peace. » That is, since he shed his blood for me, and, propitiating God the Father toward me, dissolved the enmities which were between God and man, and made heavenly things peaceful for me. Since, then, I have found this peace, I have deserved to be a wall, and to have breasts in the stead of towers. 356B

« The peaceable had a vineyard, » that is, my beloved, who is the true peaceable one, because through him all things were made peaceful that are in the heavens and on the earth. Whence also by the Prophet the Father of the world to come, the prince of peace, is he called, in whose type — the true peaceable one — Solomon the peaceable, the author of this song, went before. To this one, then, there was a vineyard, that is, the holy Church. « In that which hath peoples. » This he says to show that he sings of the Church of the nations, not of the Synagogue. For the Synagogue was of one nation only. But the Church has peoples, because she takes and receives all the nations within her most capacious bosom. « He delivered it to keepers. » The keepers of this vineyard (that is, of the holy Church) are the prophets, the apostles, and the successors of the apostles. 356COr also the keepers of this world are the angelic powers, which keep the Church from the assaults of temptations and demons. « A man bringeth for the fruit thereof a thousand pieces of silver. » By men he means the strong and the perfect, for vir (man) is named from virtus (strength). The fruit of the Church is the recompense of the heavenly life, for which the holy Church ceases not to labor in the present. And the thousand pieces of silver, which the man brings for the fruit, signify all the things that can be possessed in this world. For the number of a thousand is set for the highest perfection and fullness. The man, therefore, brings for the fruit of this vineyard a thousand pieces of silver, because the saints leave all their goods, and dispense them to the poor, that they may obtain the fruit of this vineyard (that is, the heavenly reward). Such is that which the Lord says in the Gospel: 356D« The kingdom of heaven is like to a treasure hidden in a field, which a man having found, hideth, and for joy thereof goeth and selleth all that he hath, and buyeth that field. »

« My vineyard is before me. » It is the voice of the spouse. Thou (he says) sayest that I delivered my vineyard to keepers, yet the vineyard is always before me, because, although I have committed it to my keepers, I nevertheless always have care of it, nor do I ever cease to be solicitous for it, but I am with it even to the consummation of the age. « A thousand are thine, O peaceable one, and two hundred to them that keep the fruit thereof. » A thousand are thine — understand pieces of silver, which thou hast given for the fruit of the vineyard — they are at peace with thee. As if he said: O faithful soul, be not doubtful 357Aconcerning the eternal reward, for which thou hast given all thy goods, because the thousand pieces of silver are at peace with thee, that is, that sure recompense awaits thee, and thou art about to obtain from thence the true fruit. And two hundred — understand pieces of silver — are at peace with them that keep my fruits. As by the number of a thousand is designated the fullness of perfection, so also by the number of a hundred the highest perfection is designated, because the hundred is transferred from the left hand to the right. Two hundred pieces of silver, then, which is the doubled hundred, signify those who are perfect in doctrine and in holy operation, who, by living justly and piously, keep themselves, and also, by preaching, convert others. To such, then, the two hundred pieces of silver are at peace, because a doubled recompense awaits them in the heavens. Of such it is said through Daniel: « And they that instruct 357Bmany unto justice shall shine as stars for all eternity. » For the Apostle commands these to be doubly honored, saying: « The priests that are among us, let them be esteemed worthy of double honor, especially they that are set over you and labor in the word. »

« Thou that dwellest in the gardens, the friends hearken; make me hear thy voice. » It is the voice of the beloved speaking to the spouse: O Church, that dwellest in the gardens, that is, that persistest in tending the shoots of the virtues. For the garden is the Church herself, whom (as was said above) he often promises to visit. The Church, then, dwells in the gardens, because she continually nourishes the plantings of the virtues, nor, about to withdraw for a little while in passing, does she there set up a hut, but with fixed station she dwells. « Make me (he says) to hear thy voice, » that is, preach the Gospel to whom 357Cthou art able, announce the precepts of my law together with the promise of the heavenly rewards. For this is pleasant and delightful to me, that I may hear thy voice, that is, that I may hear thee announcing the words of eternal salvation. But « the friends hearken, » that is, the angelic spirits delight to hear thy voice, whom I made thy friends by the shedding of my blood, and whom I appointed as helpers and protectors against the malignant spirits. Or the friends, that is, the spirits of the just, hearken, whom I took up from thy company to me into the heavens. For they rejoice together at thy preaching and the salvation of the brethren. The spouse herself at once answers her beloved:

« Flee away, my beloved, and be like to the roe and to the fawn of the harts upon the mountains of aromatical spices. » A wonderful 357Danswer. She is invited to preach, and that the spouse may hear her voice, but she exhorts her spouse to flee. 358ABut great is the sequence of the mystery. For because the nativity of Christ had been foretold above, when she said: « Who shall give thee to me for my brother, sucking the breasts. » For his passion had been foretold, where it was said: « Under the apple tree I raised thee up, » now consequently she exhorts the spouse to flee, that is, most swiftly to return to the Father by the ascension. And the sense is: Dost thou bid me to preach? dost thou wish to hear my voice? Flee, my beloved, that is, the mystery of thy incarnation and passion accomplished, return to heaven, so that now I may begin to think of thee not as man, but as God above all things. For then thou shalt hear my voice, because then I shall preach thee more freely to all the world. And this is what the Lord says in the Gospel: 358B« I tell you the truth: it is expedient for you that I go. » For it was expedient for the apostles and for the whole Church that Christ should return, and should absent his bodily presence, because while he was present in the body, they could not otherwise love him in spirit, they could not think of him whom they beheld as man, as invisible God and everywhere present. But after he himself ascended into the heavens, the Church began to love him spiritually, and so now to think of him as God and to preach him. And this is what the Apostle says: « And if we have known Christ according to the flesh, but now we know him no longer. » Therefore, my beloved, if thou wishest me thus to preach thee, flee (that is, withdraw) to the heavenly things, and thou who wast comprehensible through the humanity, become incomprehensible through the divinity; yet nevertheless forsake me not 358Cin the present without thy visitation, but deign to seek me again with thy frequent visitation, and to offer thyself more often to me to be seen. And this is what she says: « Be like to the roe and to the fawn of the harts upon the mountains of aromatical spices. » As (she says) the roe and the fawns of the harts, being untamed animals, flee human company, and yet are more frequently seen upon the mountains, so also thou, after thy ascension, though invisible, yet deign more often to appear to me by the gift of thy grace, and this upon the mountains of aromatical spices. The mountains of aromatical spices are the saints of God, near to heaven by the excellence of their holy manner of life, and fragrant far and wide with the odor and report of the virtues. Hence the Apostle says of himself and those like him: « Our conversation is in heaven. » And elsewhere: « We are the good odor of Christ in 358Devery place. »

The Latin is Migne's printing of Commentarium in Cantica canticorum (PL 117, coll. 295A–358D), by Haimo of Halberstadt, from the Corpus Corporum transcription. Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself. Every gilt column mark is an address: migne.app/pl/117/295a resolves to the first.
